# Janusly Roadmap

> Tactical ticket pool. Strategic context lives in [`docs/PLAN.md`](PLAN.md). Operational invariants live in [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md). README is descriptive only — no planning.

This file is the **source of truth** for what is in flight, what shipped, what is blocked, and what is intentionally deferred. The implementer skill (`janus-ship`) picks tickets from §3b among `Pending` and `Partial`. The reviewer skill (`janus-review`) verifies that staged diffs flip Status correctly.

---

## 1. Convention

- **ID:** stable `ENG-NNN`. Once assigned, never renumbered.
- **Title:** short imperative phrase, ≤ 8 words.
- **Status:** one of `Pending`, `Partial`, `Shipped`, `Gated`, `Deferred` (see §2).
- **Priority:** `P0` (urgent / unblocking others), `P1` (next), `P2` (later), `P3` (someday).
- **Phase:** which §11 phase from `docs/PLAN.md` this rolls into (`1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, or `—` for cross-phase / standalone).
- **Scope cell:** one paragraph of acceptance criteria + (when `Partial`/`Shipped`) a `**Remaining:**` or `**Summary:**` line at the end. Patterns ENG-001/002/003 below show the shape.

When this file and `docs/PLAN.md` disagree on a Status, this file wins.

When `AGENTS.md` and `docs/PLAN.md` disagree on an operational invariant, `AGENTS.md` wins.

---

## 2. Status legend

| Status | Meaning | Picked by `janus-ship`? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pending | Ready to pick. AC defined. No code yet. | Yes |
| Partial | Scaffolding exists, specific work remains. `Remaining:` line at the end of Scope. | Yes (preferred over Pending — less risk) |
| Shipped | Closed. 2-3 line summary at the end of Scope. | No |
| Gated | Blocked by an external decision (open question, license choice, vendor selection). | No — resolve the gate first |
| Deferred | Punted to a later phase by design. Don't pick. | No |

---

## 3. Tickets

### 3a. Sequencing recommendation

1. **Phase 1 (Foundations for AI scale):** ENG-008..010 + ENG-015 (shipped) → ENG-011..014 (provider abstraction, usage events, MCP server stub, generateObject migration). Quick wins (ENG-016..023) parallel any time — they don't gate phases.
2. **Phase 2 (Workflow expressiveness):** ENG-024 split into ENG-028..ENG-041. Sub-sequencing inside Phase 2: foundation first (**ENG-041** api route registry [shipped first to keep the index file maintainable as Phase 2 routes land], **ENG-028** workflow inputs/outputs schema, **ENG-029** run cancellation [shipped with ENG-041 as its first user]) → node types parallel (**ENG-030** schedule, **ENG-031** subworkflow [needs ENG-028], **ENG-032** parallel_fork, **ENG-033** human_form, **ENG-034** wait_until) → tools + surface parallel (**ENG-035** Layer 1 tools, **ENG-036** email.send, **ENG-037** pdf.generate, **ENG-038** db.query.* [gated until customer pull], **ENG-039** diff UX, **ENG-040** MCP validation with writes gated by ENG-052).
3. **Phase 3 (Self-learning loop):** ENG-025 — gated on Phase 2. Will be split into sub-tickets when Phase 2 closes.
4. **Phase 4 (Operator-grade ergonomics):** ENG-026 — gated on Phase 3. Will be split into sub-tickets when Phase 3 closes.
5. **Market and enterprise readiness overlay:** prioritize the recovery/MTTR wedge before broad integration breadth. ENG-091..ENG-095 sharpen the Recovery Center, cost governance, private-beta measurement, MCP client path, and competitive packet; ENG-096..ENG-101 add the auth/SSO foundation needed for B2B enterprise sales without replacing the current Supabase/dev/service-token base.
6. **May 2026 improvement backlog:** ENG-111..ENG-121 are the curated output from `docs/proposals/20260520-product-improvement-plan.md`. They preserve the live naming/ID sequence, split useful research into shippable tickets, and reject contradictions such as Express middleware, OpenAI-only memory assumptions, duplicate Replay Lab work, unsupervised production mutation, and fail-closed Redis rate limiting.
7. **World-class execution waves (proposed, not yet in §3b):** `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` validates ENG-111..ENG-121, promotes the world-class candidate backlog (ENG-122..ENG-136) into ROADMAP-grade AC, adds twelve world-class additions (ENG-137..ENG-148), and — per Section 11 (iteration 2) — adds thirteen more tickets (ENG-149..ENG-161) covering mobile/PWA/push, upstream health awareness, AI evidence panel, notification routing, recovery debounce, conversational AI/recovery chat, snippets library, bulk credential rotation, trigger surface expansion, cron observability, onboarding routing fix, meta-agent proactive observations, and operator-driven PII tagging. Iteration 2 also bumps ENG-143 (Slack app) into Wave A2, bundles ENG-127 + ENG-128, simplifies ENG-138 to "confidence transparency v1" (calibration math moves to v2), scopes ENG-140 to table-only (no visual graph), cuts ENG-144 (embed widget) entirely, and defers ENG-142 (workflow-shape simulator) indefinitely. Execution is split into Wave A1 + A2 + B + C + D, with 49 active tickets total. Promote rows into §3b as the team decides to execute each wave.
8. **May 2026 codebase hardening pass (ENG-162..ENG-179):** Output of a five-dimension audit (security, web performance, SQL/Drizzle, refactor, inline documentation) across the live monorepo. Eighteen `Pending` tickets bundle 53 audit findings into ship-sized work. Pick order suggested: **Security first (P0)** — ENG-162 (DLQ role gate) and ENG-163 (admin self-modification block) are one-PR-each safety fixes; ENG-164 (dep CVE refresh) gates enterprise procurement. **Reliability + scale next (P1)** — ENG-169 (composite + GIN indexes) and ENG-170 (run-events fan-in cap + PG percentile) are independent and parallelizable; ENG-171 (retention sweeps) closes the unbounded-growth gap on `audit_logs` + `scim_processed_events`. **Web performance (P1)** — ENG-165 (polling coalesce + request dedup) and ENG-166 (React Flow edge stabilization) and ENG-167 (list virtualization) together drop observed jank on mid-spec laptops. **Maintainability (P2)** — ENG-172 / ENG-173 / ENG-174 / ENG-175 / ENG-176 / ENG-178 / ENG-179 reduce the per-change cognitive cost; pick by team appetite. **Polish + naming (P2/P3)** — ENG-168 (cold-load) + ENG-177 (repo barrel + naming) close cleanup debt. Treat the eighteen as independent unless a Scope cell calls out an explicit dependency — none chain into a critical path.
9. **UX Wave U1 — operational screen redesign (ENG-180..ENG-187):** Output of a UX/UI audit across the 11 workspace tabs that exposed the same root problem in 8 of them: the React Flow DAG canvas was being rendered everywhere except `home`, occupying 60-70% of the viewport when the operator entered Operations / Credentials / Members / Marketplace / Templates / Runs / MultiAgent / Workflows-list — the actual admin content was crushed into a ~387 px right rail. Eight tickets total — five main improvements (ENG-180..184) + three follow-ups (ENG-185..187) caught during the post-shipping accounting of the audit: **ENG-180** (DAG-gating, `Shipped` via commit `9c84adb`) added the `CANVAS_TABS = ['copilot', 'inspector']` closed-enum allowlist so only AI Studio and Inspector keep the canvas; the other 8 tabs now render at full main-slot width inside a `<div data-layout="contextual">` wrapper capped at `--we-content-max-width: 1280px`. **ENG-181** (Operations sub-tab rail) breaks the 9-card vertical stack of OperationsPanel into a 220 px rail + 4 sub-sections (Overview / Reliability / Access / Integrations), reducing 9 parallel fetches per platform bump to 2-3. **ENG-182** (severity stripes + EmptyState design system) adds a 3 px left-border CSS rule keyed by `[data-severity]` and a shared `<EmptyState>` component — cross-cutting visual polish, zero new deps. **ENG-183** (sidebar 3-group reorg) redistributes the 11 nav items from 4 groups to 3 (Pinned / Build / Run-Operate). **ENG-184** (split RightPanel god-component) extracts the 1671-line RightPanel.tsx into per-tab modules with zero UX change. **Follow-ups (caught after the initial 5):** **ENG-185** (adopt compact density tokens) introduces a single `--we-density-*` token family + removes the noisy inline descriptions from Operations cards. **ENG-186** (lift ReactFlowProvider) closes the "acknowledged limitation" from ENG-180 by mounting the canvas DOM persistently and toggling `display: none` on non-canvas tabs — preserves zoom/pan across canvas↔non-canvas transitions. **ENG-187** (cross-surface vital-signs status strip) extracts the 4-tile metric pattern to a `<VitalSignsStrip>` component shared between Operations + Home (Datadog-style consistency, depends on ENG-181). Original UX wave proposal used numbers ENG-162..166; renumbered to ENG-180..187 after collision with the hardening pass above.

Legacy umbrella tickets whose `Phase` is later than Phase 1 stay `Deferred` until their dependencies close. Explicit later-phase slices may be `Pending` when their AC is self-contained, dependency notes are in the Scope cell, and `janus-ship` can validate the prerequisite before picking them.

| ENG-001 | Redact secrets in node output | Shipped | P0 | — | Wrap every executor result with `redactValues` before persistence so `run_nodes.state_json` and `run_events.payload` never contain raw secret values. AC: redaction runs before any DB write; existing rows are not back-filled (out of scope). **Summary:** Closed the security finding "secret values stored in run_nodes.state_json and run_events.payload." |
| ENG-002 | Validate BullMQ job payload | Shipped | P0 | — | BullMQ worker validates `job.data` against `NodeSchema` + `WorkflowSchema` on entry. Poisoned jobs throw `UnrecoverableError` so they go to DLQ instead of retrying forever. AC: validation runs as the first step of the worker handler; bad payloads land in `dead_letters` with a useful reason. **Summary:** No more infinite-retry loops on malformed jobs. |
| ENG-003 | Require editor role on POST /validate | Shipped | P1 | — | The only mutating-shape endpoint without `requireRole`. Promoted to `editor`. AC: anonymous + viewer return 403; editor + admin pass. **Summary:** Closed RBAC gap on validation endpoint. |
| ENG-004 | Cap getUsageSummary scan | Shipped | P1 | — | `getUsageSummary` was an unbounded scan that would OOM the API. Scoped to last 30 days with a 10k-row cap. AC: query plan shows time bound; cap enforced at repo layer. **Summary:** Constant-memory usage summary. |

Env `JANUSLY_REQUIRE_SAVED_WORKFLOW=true` forbids ad-hoc in production. AC: audit table shows the distinction; production env rejects ad-hoc when flag is on. **Summary:** Audit row + prod kill-switch in place. |
| ENG-006 | Redact secret-shaped keys in audit | Shipped | P0 | — | `audit()` redacts `secret*`/`password*`/`token*`/`authorization*` etc. keys in metadata before persisting to `audit_logs`. AC: round-trip test confirms redaction; key list documented in code. **Summary:** Audit metadata is safe to read. |
| ENG-007 | Bump @supabase/supabase-js to 2.105.0 | Shipped | P3 | — | Routine dep bump; touched both `apps/api` and `apps/web`. AC: `pnpm test` green; no breaking-change touches in our code. **Summary:** Latest supabase-js across the two workspaces. |
| ENG-008 | Formal Drizzle migrations for production | Shipped | P1 | 1 | Today `packages/db/src/schema-management.ts` does idempotent `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` at runtime. Production wants versioned, repeatable Drizzle migrations. AC: `pnpm migrate` runs `drizzle-kit migrate` against `DATABASE_URL`; schema bootstrap moves to `migrations/` files; e2e boots Compose, runs migrate, runs the suite. **Summary:** `schema.ts` enriched with TZ-aware timestamps and the full index set; generated `migrations/0000_tranquil_gravity.sql` mirrors the deleted runtime bootstrap at the table/index level. Root `pnpm migrate` and a `waitForPostgres()` step in `scripts/run-e2e.mjs` wire the new flow; API and worker now call `assertMigrationsApplied()` and fail fast against an unmigrated DB. |
| ENG-009 | Lazy load very large run timelines | Shipped | P2 | — | Runs view loads the full timeline up-front. For runs with thousands of events the canvas chokes. AC: timeline paginates / virtualizes; `bumpPlatformVersion()` semantics preserved on terminal state. **Summary:** `GET /run` and `GET /status` now cap `events` at 200 (max 500) and return `eventsCursor` + `eventsHasMore` for older pages; the web store merges polled events by id and adds a `loadOlderEvents` action backed by a "Load older events" button on both Run-events and Multi-agent-timeline panels. Terminal-state `bumpPlatformVersion()` plumbing left untouched. |
| ENG-010 | Vitest browser mode for canvas tests | Shipped | P3 | — | Jsdom can't execute React Flow canvas paths reliably (drag, resize, edge layout). Want Vitest browser mode for canvas-level component tests. ENG-015 wires the same suite into CI via the `test_browser` job, closing the "in CI" portion of the AC. |
| ENG-011 | AI provider abstraction (Vercel AI SDK) | Shipped | P0 | 1 | Migrate every OpenAI call to the `ai` package + `@ai-sdk/*` providers. Per-request, per-step, per-tenant provider/model selection. Default stays OpenAI; Anthropic and Ollama become tested alternatives. See `docs/PLAN.md` §4 phases A–C. AC: `JANUSLY_LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic` makes AI Studio still work; `parseAiWorkflow` looser is replaced by `generateObject({ schema: WorkflowSchema })` (covered by ENG-014); `mode + aiError` contract preserved across providers. **Summary:** Built `packages/ai/src/llm-client.ts` with a registry-backed abstraction — `PROVIDERS: Record<string, ProviderSpec>` makes adding a vendor a four-field record entry. Five OpenAI call sites (api `/ai/{generate,explain}-workflow` + `/ai/explain-run`, engine `agent-planner` + `ai` node) now route through `getLlmClient().generateText({ system, prompt, responseFormat?, modelHint? })`; `modelHint` accepts both bare ids and `"<provider>/<model>"` specs so per-call provider override comes for free. Three duplicate `getOpenAIClient()` factories collapsed into one. `JANUSLY_LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic` + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` flips the entire deploy to Anthropic with zero code changes. Direct `openai` dep dropped from `apps/api` + `packages/engine`; only `@janusly/ai` keeps it transitively. The fallback contract (`{ mode, aiError, ... }` per AGENTS.md) is preserved at every call site — new error sources (unknown provider, missing override key) intentionally throw to flow through the same try/catch. `parseAiWorkflow` looser untouched (ENG-014 owns its replacement). |
| ENG-012 | usage_events instrumentation | Shipped | P0 | 1 | Every LLM call writes one row to `usage_events` (org, run, node, provider, model, tokens, latency, cost). AC: row written from a single chokepoint (the new provider abstraction's wrapper); Runs view surfaces tokens + cost per node; cost is computed from a per-model price map (env or table). **Summary:** New `packages/ai/src/usage-recorder.ts` owns a process-global `setUsageRecorder` registry; `packages/ai/src/pricing.ts` ships built-in defaults for 7 OpenAI/Anthropic models plus a `JANUSLY_LLM_PRICE_<MODEL>` env override (USD per 1M tokens). `LlmClient.generateText` measures `latencyMs`, computes `costUsd`, fires the recorder fire-and-forget on success AND failure paths — fail-open by design (try/catch around the recorder; telemetry never breaks an LLM call). New `packages/data/src/usageRepo.ts` writes one row per call (`metric: "llm.completion"`, `quantity: totalTokens`, full breakdown in `metadata` jsonb) so the existing `getUsageSummary` + `/billing/usage` + web "Usage summary" card light up automatically. Engine `NodeContext.orgId` plumbed once per node execution via `getRunOrgId(runId)`; api boots `setUsageRecorder` once and threads `context: { orgId, userId, runId? }` through the three AI routes. New `AiUsageFooter` in `RightPanel.tsx` renders the per-node "model · tokens · $cost · ms" line whenever the selected `RunNode.stateJson` carries usage. No schema migration — the existing `usage_events` shape absorbs the data via `metadata`. |
| ENG-013 | @janusly/mcp-server (read-only) | Shipped | P1 | 1 | Stand up `packages/mcp-server` with read-only tools: `workflows.list`, `workflows.get`, `recipes.list`, `tools.list`, `runs.get`. Boot via `pnpm --filter @janusly/mcp-server dev`. AC: Claude Desktop can discover and call these tools against a local instance; auth uses dev headers or service-token mode; org scoping enforced. **Summary:** New `packages/mcp-server` workspace ships a stdio MCP server (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk@^1.29.0`) that proxies all five tools to the existing HTTP API — no DB access, no auth duplication. Auth is env-driven (`JANUSLY_API_SERVICE_TOKEN` for production, dev headers via `JANUSLY_API_ORG_ID` / `JANUSLY_API_USER_ID` for local). 17 unit tests + a stdio protocol smoke (`initialize` + `tools/list`) confirm the handshake and dispatch. AGENTS.md gets a new MCP server invariant; root README adds the Claude Desktop config snippet; `packages/mcp-server/README.md` carries the full architecture + auth-flow document. |
| ENG-014 | generateObject replaces parseAiWorkflow looser | Shipped | P1 | 1 | Replace `parseAiWorkflow`'s shape-coercing looser with `generateObject({ schema: WorkflowSchema })` from the Vercel AI SDK so the model returns a typed object directly. AC: looser code path removed; existing AI-mode tests still pass; fallback contract `{ mode: "fallback", aiError }` unchanged on validation failure. Depends on ENG-011. **Summary:** `LlmClient` gained a sibling `generateObject<T>({ schema, ... })` method that calls `aiGenerateText({ output: Output.object({ schema }) })` under the hood — same provider/model resolution + recorder fire (ENG-012) as `generateText`, just schema-aware. `/ai/generate-workflow` now calls `llm.generateObject<Workflow>({ schema: WorkflowSchema, ... })` and reads `result.object` directly; the ~30-line `looseAiWorkflow` shape-coercer is deleted, and `parseAiWorkflow` is renamed to `sanitizeAiWorkflow` (post-Zod work that filters grammar-invalid edge/condition expressions through `validateExpression`). Schema rejections and sanitize throws both flow through the existing try/catch into `{ mode: "fallback", aiError, ...fallbackWorkflow }`. Seven new tests in `packages/ai` cover the happy path, schema-rejection failure mode, the `experimental_output ?? output` field read, `provider/model` spec propagation, recorder fail-open under generateObject, and the absent-output defensive throw. |
| ENG-015 | GitHub Actions CI | Shipped | P1 | 1 | Add `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: build + test + e2e + dep-audit (osv-scanner or `pnpm audit`). Cache pnpm store. Run on PR + main. AC: green CI on a clean clone; e2e job uses Compose; dep-audit fails the build on HIGH+. **Summary:** Single workflow with four parallel jobs — `build_test`, `test_browser`, `test_e2e`, `dep_audit` — pinning `pnpm@10.23.0` + `node@24` and reusing `actions/setup-node@v4`'s `cache: pnpm`. The e2e job invokes `pnpm test:e2e` which already owns the full Compose lifecycle (up → migrate → API/worker → Playwright → down). Dep-audit runs `pnpm audit --audit-level high`, letting today's two pre-existing moderate advisories through (cleanup tracked in ENG-016) and failing only on high+. Closes ENG-010's "in CI" gap as a side effect. |
| ENG-016 | Drop deprecated @esbuild-kit/* subdeps | Shipped | P3 | — | Trace which top-level dep pulls in `@esbuild-kit/*` (likely `drizzle-kit`) and update once a clean version ships. AC: `pnpm why @esbuild-kit/core-utils` shows no path; `pnpm install` clean; deprecation warning gone. **Summary:** drizzle-kit's clean cut (`1.0.0-rc.1`) replaces `tsx` + `@esbuild-kit/esm-loader` with `jiti`, but the RC requires a matching runtime ORM (`./_relations` subpath added in `drizzle-orm@1.0.0-rc.1`). Bumped both deps in lockstep across `packages/db`, `packages/data`, `packages/engine`, `apps/api`. The drizzle-orm 1.0 wire change (positional client → `drizzle({ client })` options object) hit one site (`packages/db/src/index.ts`); rest of the surface (~100+ Drizzle query call sites across engine/api/data) compiles unchanged. drizzle-kit 1.0 reformatted the migrations folder layout from flat `0000_<name>.sql` + `meta/_journal.json` to per-migration `<timestamp>_<name>/{migration.sql,snapshot.json}` directories — applied via `drizzle-kit up` (hash-preserving); `migrations.test.ts` updated to assert the new shape. Lockfile audit: `grep '@esbuild-kit' pnpm-lock.yaml` returns 0 matches; `pnpm why @esbuild-kit/{core-utils,esm-loader}` both report not found. |
| ENG-017 | Root pnpm dev script | Shipped | P3 | — | `pnpm dev` at root boots `docker compose up -d redis postgres` + api + worker + web with `concurrently`. AC: single command brings up the full stack on a fresh checkout; `Ctrl+C` shuts everything down cleanly including Compose. **Summary:** New `scripts/run-dev.mjs` orchestrator mirrors the `run-e2e.mjs` lifecycle — Compose up → `waitForPostgres` → `pnpm migrate` → spawn api/worker/web binaries directly (`tsx watch` for api/worker, `vite` for web) with `cwd` set per workspace and `PATH` augmented to find the local `node_modules/.bin`. Skipping the `pnpm <script>` wrapper keeps Ctrl+C teardown quiet (no `ERR_PNPM_RECURSIVE_RUN_FIRST_FAIL` chatter). Signal handlers + child-crash detection always end with `docker compose down` and exit 0 on clean shutdown. Pure-Node orchestration; `concurrently` not needed. |
| ENG-018 | Evals harness for AI workflow generation | Shipped | P2 | — | Write `evals/generate-workflow.jsonl` with 10 prompts and the node-type counts we expect. Add `pnpm evals` script that runs each prompt against `/ai/generate-workflow` and asserts shape. AC: script returns non-zero on regressions; runs against either OpenAI or a recorded fixture. **Summary:** New `evals/generate-workflow.jsonl` (10 cases — 3 lock the deterministic fallback templates by id, 7 exercise the live-LLM path with loose `requiredTypes` assertions and `requiresMode: "ai"` so they skip when no `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set). New `scripts/run-evals.mjs` POSTs each case sequentially to `/ai/generate-workflow` with dev headers, asserts shape, prints ✓/✗/⊘ per row, and exits 1 only on real regressions (skipped cases don't gate). Wired as root `pnpm evals` script. |
| ENG-019 | Move rate limiter to Redis | Shipped | P1 | — | The in-memory rate limiter is bypassed in multi-process deployments. Move to Redis (already a dependency). AC: limit enforced across N API processes; existing Redis connection reused; tests cover both single-process and multi-process scenarios. **Summary:** `apps/api/src/rate-limit.ts` now uses `INCR` + `PEXPIRE` against a dedicated `apps/api/src/redis.ts` ioredis client (sane request-path defaults, separate from BullMQ's connection). `enforceRateLimit` is built via a `createRateLimiter(client)` DI seam so tests can inject a fake Redis. Five tests cover single-process, per-org isolation, multi-process state sharing, retry-after wording, and Redis-down fail-open. AGENTS.md documents the fail-open invariant. |
| ENG-020 | Property-based test for parseAiWorkflow looser | Deferred | P2 | — | Convert the looser to a property-based test: 1000 random LLM-shaped inputs (fast-check or similar) should never crash, never throw uncaught. AC: test added to `apps/api` suite; runs in `pnpm test`. **Note:** becomes redundant if ENG-014 lands first — close as `Deferred` in that case. **Resolution:** Closed as `Deferred` per its own AC — ENG-014 deleted `looseAiWorkflow` entirely (the LLM now goes through `generateObject({ schema: WorkflowSchema })`, schema enforcement is real). Nothing left to property-test. |
| ENG-021 | Pin AWS metadata IP after assertPublicHostname | Shipped | P0 | — | Close DNS-rebinding TOCTOU on the SSRF guard: after `assertPublicHostname` resolves a hostname, pin the resolved IP for the actual connect using `undici.Agent` with a `connect` hook. AC: a regression test exercises a hostname that resolves to a public IP first, private IP second — connection is refused. **Summary:** `packages/engine/src/http-policy.ts` now resolves DNS once, validates every returned address, and constructs an `undici.Agent` whose `connect.lookup` returns the pinned address — `fetchHttpTarget` dispatches through it so the TCP connect can't be redirected by a second DNS lookup. Defence in depth: the pinned `lookup` re-asserts the IP isn't private at connect time. New regression test exercises a public-then-private rebinding and proves only the validation lookup hits DNS. `ALLOW_PRIVATE_HTTP_TARGETS=true` bypass shape preserved exactly. |
| ENG-022 | OTel resource enrichment | Shipped | P3 | — | Add `service.namespace="janusly"` and an env-derived `service.instance.id` to the OTel resource so multi-instance dashboards can disambiguate. AC: traces and metrics carry both attributes; no change to `service.name`. **Summary:** New `packages/engine/src/observability/resource.ts` builds a single `janusResource` via `resourceFromAttributes` carrying `service.name`, `service.namespace`, and `service.instance.id` (precedence: `OTEL_SERVICE_INSTANCE_ID` → `HOSTNAME` → `os.hostname()`). Wired into both `NodeTracerProvider` and `MeterProvider`. Side benefit — `service.name="janusly"` is now literally true at the Resource level, where dashboards filter (was previously only set as the instrumentation library name; the actual `service.name` resource attribute defaulted to `unknown_service:node`). |
| ENG-023 | Tool registry → Zod schemas | Shipped | P1 | — | Convert each entry in the tool registry to a Zod schema for input + output. Foundation for `docs/PLAN.md` §8 contract migration. AC: `validateToolInput` becomes a real schema parse; existing tools (`http.request`, `text.uppercase`, `json.pick`) round-trip; new tool registration enforces the schema field. **Summary:** `packages/engine/src/tool-registry.ts` rewritten with a typed `ToolDefinition<TIn, TOut>` shape; `validateToolInput` is now `safeParse` (legacy "Missing required input" wording preserved), `executeTool` parses input AND validates output, `listTools` derives `required`/`optional` from the schema. New `defineTool` helper preserves per-tool TS inference under the `satisfies` constraint so registrations without both schemas fail at build time. Companion monorepo-wide maintainer-doc sweep covers the foundation workspaces (`scripts`, `shared`, `db`, `ai`, `data`, `domain`) plus the engine + api + web entry points; remaining files tracked as ENG-027. |

AC: every exported symbol in those workspaces carries a file-header + per-export JSDoc; AGENTS.md "Maintainer docs convention" stays in sync; no behavioural change. **Summary:** Added file-header block comments + per-export JSDoc to 52 files (24 engine, 8 api, 20 web — adapters/core/observability + http-policy/persistence/expression/template/secrets/etc.; api auth/dlq/http/permissions/rate-limit/redis/run-pagination/templates; web main/api/auth/Layout/MultiAgentTimeline/eventUtils/constants + 13 components incl. RightPanel). All headers cite ENG-NNN tickets where the surface exists because of a specific invariant (ENG-001 redaction, ENG-002 atomic claim, ENG-009 cursor pagination, ENG-012 usage telemetry, ENG-014 generateObject migration, ENG-019 rate-limit fail-open, ENG-021 SSRF pin, ENG-022 OTel resource). Audit script (`head -1 | grep '/\*\*?'`) reports zero MISSING. Zero behavioural change — `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` PASS unchanged (227 tests). |

| ENG-028 | Workflow inputs/outputs schema | Shipped | P1 | 2 | Workflows can declare typed `inputs` (consumed by `POST /start` payload) and `outputs` (projected on terminal state). `WorkflowSchema` gains optional `inputs?` + `outputs?` fields stored as JSON Schema; the engine validates `inputs` at run start and computes `outputs` from the run's terminal context. The AI Studio's Inspector surfaces the declared schema. AC: a workflow with `inputs: { invoiceId: z.string() }` rejects `POST /start` payloads missing `invoiceId` with a 400; the run's `succeeded` state surfaces a typed `output` block visible to API + web. Foundation for Phase 2 — unblocks ENG-031 (subworkflow needs typed call shape) and the §9 recipes. **Summary:** `WorkflowSchema.inputs` is a JSON-Schema-subset (string/number/boolean/object/array + properties/required/items/enum/description); `WorkflowSchema.outputs` is `Record<string, string>` of templates resolved against the run's context via the existing `renderTemplate`. New `packages/engine/src/inputs-validator.ts` is hand-rolled (~80 lines, no ajv dep) and aggregates every error in one pass with JSONPath-style paths. New `packages/engine/src/outputs-projector.ts` reuses `renderTemplate` to project terminal-context values. `runs.outputJson` (jsonb, nullable) added via migration `20260502032039_fearless_killraven`; `updateRunStatusFromNodes` writes it in the same UPDATE that flips status to `succeeded`. `POST /start` accepts `{ workflow, input }` (legacy flat-workflow body still works); `WorkflowInputValidationError` surfaces as 400 with the field-level error list. Web Inspector renders a "Workflow I/O" card in the no-selection branch; RunsPanel's active-run card surfaces a "Workflow output" details block when `run.outputJson` is populated. Backward compat: workflows without `inputs`/`outputs` continue to start, run, finish without `outputJson` writes. |
| ENG-029 | Run cancellation route + UI | Shipped | P1 | 2 | `POST /run/:runId/cancel` (editor role required) calls the existing `cancelRun(runId, reason?)` helper from `packages/engine/src/persistence.ts` (already wired); UI surfaces a "Cancel run" button on the Runs panel for any run in `pending|queued|running|waiting` status. Calls `bumpPlatformVersion()` after a successful cancel. AC: editor can cancel an in-flight run; the run's status flips to `cancelled` in the DB; the worker drains the BullMQ in-flight job per the existing SIGTERM contract and doesn't orphan a `running` node. Smaller scope than the Phase 2 node tickets — already half-built (the persistence helper exists); ship first to validate the Phase 2 UX rhythm. **Summary:** Ships as `POST /run/cancel` (body-param shape, mirrors `/resume` — no path-param routing exists in the project yet). Editor-gated; `cancelRun` flips the run + non-running nodes to `cancelled` and emits a `run.cancelled` event. The 409-on-terminal guard refuses cancels of runs already finalised. Web side: `cancelActiveRun` handler in `App.tsx` POSTs the route + `bumpPlatformVersion()` + refetch + toast; new "Active run" card in `RunsPanel` carries the Cancel button (disabled when terminal). Shipped in lockstep with ENG-041 — the cancel route is the first to register through the new route registry. |
| ENG-030 | `schedule` node type | Shipped | P1 | 2 | New `schedule` node triggers a workflow on a cron expression (or natural-language interval). Runtime adds a Redis-backed scheduler entry (BullMQ repeatable jobs are the natural fit) keyed by `(orgId, workflowVersionId, nodeId)`. AC: workflow with one `schedule` node + downstream `http` node fires on its cadence; pausing/resuming the workflow updates the scheduler entry; deleting the workflow removes the entry. The cron expression is parsed by `cron-parser`. Multi-tenant: every scheduler entry carries `orgId`. Closes the §9.1.3 scheduled summarizer recipe. **Summary:** 19th entry in `nodeTypeValues` (`packages/shared/src/workflow.ts`). New `schedule_entries` table (id / orgId / workflowId / workflowVersionId / nodeId / cronExpression / enabled / lastRunAt / lastRunId / createdBy / createdAt / updatedAt) with unique index on `(org_id, workflow_version_id, node_id)` and secondary indexes on `(org_id, enabled)` + `(org_id, workflow_id)`. The repo (`packages/data/src/scheduleEntriesRepo.ts`) is multi-tenant scoped on every function except the documented worker-only `listAllEnabled` (cold-start replay) and `getScheduleEntryById` (trigger handler — see security note below). `upsertScheduleEntry` uses Postgres `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` so concurrent saves are atomic. Cron firing is owned by BullMQ 5's `upsertJobScheduler` API keyed by the deterministic id `schedule:<orgId>:<workflowVersionId>:<nodeId>`; the worker handles `job.name === "schedule-trigger"`, the handler reads orgId from the PERSISTED row (not the BullMQ payload) so a spoofed payload can't trigger another tenant's workflow. Save lifecycle: `apps/api/src/workflows-save.ts` calls `syncWorkflowSchedules` after the transaction commits (outside the txn — failures logged but don't roll save back; next save or worker cold-start replay reconciles via the deterministic id). Delete lifecycle: new `DELETE /workflows/:id` route (Open/Closed routing — single registry entry, no inline `if (req.method === ...)`) hard-deletes `workflows` + `workflow_versions` and unregisters every BullMQ scheduler; orphaned `runs` / `audit_logs` rows are tolerated (no FK). Pause/resume is in-band: re-save the workflow with `schedule.config.enabled = false` and the BullMQ scheduler is removed; flip to true and it re-registers. Cold-start: the worker boots, awaits `replayAllScheduleEntries()` before constructing the `Worker`, so multi-replica clusters and Redis-loss recovery rebuild the scheduler set idempotently. New executor (`packages/engine/src/schedule.ts`) is a passthrough — `cronExpression` echoed back with `triggeredAt` ISO string. Workflow validation gains a `schedule_invalid_cron` rule via the shared `validateCronExpression` helper. Web inspector adds the catalogue entry + a Quick Setup block (text input for cron expression, checkbox for enabled) in `apps/web/src/components/RightPanel.tsx`. AI generation cap stays at 11/20 — `schedule` is operator-only (noop-promote pattern, same as `multi_agent`, `wait_until`, `webhook`, `agent_reflection`, `router_llm`, `subworkflow`, `parallel_fork`, `join`). New tests: `schedule.test.ts` (executor + cron validator), `schedule-scheduler.test.ts` (BullMQ lifecycle, multi-tenant payload spoofing, version churn, replay idempotency), `workflow-validation.test.ts` extended with cron-rule cases, `apps/web/src/constants.test.ts` extended with catalogue cases. AGENTS.md gains the scheduler invariant. |
| ENG-031 | `subworkflow` node type | Shipped | P1 | 2 | New `subworkflow` node calls another saved workflow as a step. The caller's `inputs` get forwarded; the callee's terminal `outputs` flow back into the caller's `context.<nodeId>.output`. Nested run spans share `traceId` for OTel. Recursion guard: depth limit configurable via `JANUSLY_MAX_SUBWORKFLOW_DEPTH` (default 5). AC: a parent workflow calling a child via `subworkflow` produces two `runs` rows linked by a `parent_run_id` column; child failure propagates to parent as a node failure; the recursion guard rejects depth > limit with a clear error. **Depends on ENG-028** (typed inputs/outputs). **Summary:** First user of ENG-028's typed inputs/outputs — closes the foundation→consumer loop. New `packages/engine/src/subworkflow.ts` owns the executor (depth guard, org-scoped `loadLatestWorkflowVersion`, traceId propagation, child startRun) and the terminal-status notifier (succeeds → `markNodeSucceeded` with child outputs + `enqueueReadyNodes`; fails/cancels → `markNodeFailed` + `updateRunStatusFromNodes` for the trampolined roll-up). Async pause-and-resume mirrors the existing `webhook`/`approval` machinery. `runs.{parent_run_id,parent_node_id,trace_id}` added via migration `20260502050433_grey_leopardon`; `startRun` writes the linkage in the same transaction as the existing inserts (atomicity preserved). Persistence/subworkflow circular-import resolved via a notifier-injection seam (`setSubworkflowNotifier`). Web: `subworkflow` registered in `nodeTypes`/`nodeUi`/`nodePresets`/`getNodeConfigSummary`; new `QuickConfigEditor` case for `workflowId` + optional input override. AI prompt grammar gains the new node type so `/ai/generate-workflow` can emit it. Multi-tenant scope preserved (child lookup org-scoped); cancel propagation deferred to a follow-up; child runs skip `run.started` audit (parent's `node.subworkflow.completed` event provides the trail). |
| ENG-032 | `parallel_fork` + `join` node types | Shipped | P1 | 2 | New `parallel_fork` node fans execution out to N labelled branches that converge at an explicit `join` node. Each branch advances independently; the join collects all branch outputs into a `Record<branchId, output>`. Engine atomicity: all branch claims go through the existing `tryClaimNodeForQueue` (ENG-002 invariant); the join node waits for all branches' terminal status before completing. AC: workflow with a 3-way fork → 3 `http` calls → join produces `{ a: {...}, b: {...}, c: {...} }` in the join's output; one branch failing fails the whole join (caller decides via `condition` downstream). The fork/join pair is a single atomic checkpoint. **Summary:** The discriminated union grew from 16 to 18 node types (`parallel_fork`, `join`) in `packages/shared/src/workflow.ts`. Both executors live in `packages/engine/src/parallel-fork.ts` as declarative shells over the runtime semantics that already existed: `enqueueReadyNodes` already required ALL incoming-edge predecessors in `succeeded\|skipped` before queuing a downstream node (gives "join waits for every branch" for free), and `tryClaimNodeForQueue` is already the atomic UPDATE...WHERE...pending guard (gives "fork/join is a single atomic checkpoint" for free). The shared branch/source validators run in both `validateWorkflow` and the executors, so malformed fork/join configs fail during `/validate` instead of first runtime execution. The fork executor validates `branches: Array<{ label, description? }>` (2-10 entries, unique labels ≤ 64 chars) and echoes them as `output.branches`. The join executor reads `ctx.context[predecessorId].output` for each declared `sources: Record<branchLabel, predecessorId>` entry and assembles `output = { branches: Record<label, output> }`. Three new readiness rules in `workflow-readiness.ts`: `fork_without_join_pair` (warn) catches forks with no transitively-reachable join; `fork_join_missing_branch_sources` (fail) catches forks whose downstream joins do not map every declared branch label; `join_sources_unreachable` (fail) catches a join's `sources` referencing a node that isn't an upstream — the production-mode gate at `POST /start` rejects fail-level issues with HTTP 422. Web inspector adds `Fan out` / `Merge branches` entries in `apps/web/src/constants.ts` plus matching `QuickConfigEditor` sections in `RightPanel.tsx` (JSON editor for `branches` array and `sources` map; helper text explains the data shapes). **AI generation deviation:** `parallel_fork` and `join` are intentionally NOT added to `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema`. The slim AI schema is already at the Anthropic compiled-grammar cap (11 branches; 12+ rejects with "The compiled grammar is too large"). The documented escape hatch — operator-promotes-noop-from-inspector, already used for `wait_until`, `webhook`, `agent_reflection`, `router_llm`, `subworkflow` — remains the path: the LLM emits a `noop` placeholder with a descriptive id (e.g. `fork_lead_enrichment`), the operator clicks the `<select>` in the inspector and changes the type. ENG-076 is the umbrella for re-visiting the cap. New tests: `parallel-fork.test.ts` (23 cases covering executor happy paths + every validation rejection branch); `core/runtime.test.ts` extended with 3 fan-in cases pinning the merge-queues-once / merge-blocked-on-pending / merge-blocked-on-failed invariants; `workflow-validation.test.ts` covers malformed and valid fork/join config at the structural validation layer; `workflow-readiness.test.ts` covers the no-join, incomplete-join-source, complete-join-source, and unreachable-source cases; `apps/web/src/constants.test.ts` (new, 7 cases) pins the catalogue + summary helpers. |
| ENG-033 | `human_form` node type | Shipped | P1 | 2 | New `human_form` node pauses execution like `approval` but accepts a Zod schema for the expected input. The web renders the schema as a typed form; submission resumes the workflow with the parsed input as `output`. Resume token is HMAC-signed (`packages/engine/src/secrets.ts` extension). AC: editor can submit a form; the schema rejects invalid input with field-level errors; the resume token is single-use and signed. Closes the §9.6 PTO request and §9.7 quarterly access review use cases. **Summary:** Added `human_form` to the shared workflow contract, runtime registry, validation, and `/resume` path. Waiting metadata now carries a signed HMAC resume token; the API validates that token, validates submitted fields against the node's schema, completes only a still-waiting node, and persists the parsed input as node output. Web UX adds a Collect form catalogue node, inspector quick setup for title/instructions/schema, a paused-step form dialog with field-level errors, and Playwright smoke coverage for run -> fill form -> resume. AI generation keeps the 11-branch Anthropic-safe grammar but reorganizes the set to include `human_form` and move `multi_agent` to the operator-only/noop-promotion lane. |
| ENG-034 | `wait_until` node type | Shipped | P1 | 2 | New `wait_until` node pauses the run until an absolute timestamp (e.g. "3 days from now"). BullMQ delayed jobs handle the actual wakeup. Output is a no-op `{}`. AC: workflow with `wait_until: { duration: "P3D" }` (ISO 8601 duration) checkpoints, the run goes `waiting`, and the BullMQ job fires after the delay; a manual resume short-circuits the wait. Closes the §9.2 churn save use case. **Summary:** New `wait_until` executor in `packages/engine/src/wait-until.ts` schedules a delayed BullMQ job (`enqueueWaitUntilResume` in `queue.ts`) and returns `{ status: "waiting", metadata: { wakeAt, durationMs } }`. The worker dispatches `job.name === "wait-resume"` to `handleWaitResume`, which checks node status before calling `resumeRun` — manual `POST /resume` and `cancelRun` short-circuit the wake-up via the idempotency guard, and early wake-ups retry briefly if the node is still transitioning from `running` to `waiting`. New `getRunNodeStatus` helper in `persistence.ts` backs that status guard. Reuses `parseIsoDuration` from the existing tool catalog (no new deps). Web inspector renders a `Duration (ISO 8601)` field with format examples. AI prompt grammar gains the `wait_until` line. Backward compat preserved: existing approval/webhook flow runs through the default `execute-node` job-name branch. Engine test count: 141 → 152. |
| ENG-035 | Tool catalog Layer 1 (pure-function tools) | Shipped | P1 | 2 | Five new tool families in `packages/engine/src/tool-registry.ts`: `text.*` (extend with lowercase, trim, replace, regex), `json.*` (extend with set, merge, jq), `csv.*` (parse, stringify, filter), `time.*` (now, parse, format, diff, add), `crypto.*` (sha256, hmac, uuid). All Zod-schemed inputs/outputs per the ENG-023 invariant. No I/O. AC: every new tool registered with `inputSchema` + `outputSchema`; tests cover the happy path + Zod rejection per family. **Summary:** Eighteen new pure-function tools registered: `text.{lowercase,trim,replace,regex}`, `json.{set,merge,jq}`, `csv.{parse,stringify,filter}`, `time.{now,parse,format,diff,add}`, `crypto.{sha256,hmac,uuid}`. Hand-rolled helpers in `csv.ts` (RFC 4180 subset state-machine parser; quoted fields, escaped `""`, CRLF/LF), `iso-duration.ts` (`P[Y/M/D]T[H/M/S]` regex; year=365d, month=30d approximations), and `json-jq.ts` (safe jq-style selector subset: `.`, paths, bracket keys, indexes, `[]` projections, `|` pipelines) — no new deps (csv-parse, date-fns, node-jq deliberately avoided). `time.format` exposes a closed enum (`iso`/`epoch`/`epochSeconds`/`utc`/`rfc2822`) instead of token-based formatters; `crypto.hmac` allowlist limited to `sha256`/`sha512`; `text.regex` capped at 1000 matches as defense-in-depth. AI prompt grammar extended so `/ai/generate-workflow` knows the new tool names. Zero risk to multi-tenant scope, engine atomicity, AI fallback, or worker lifecycle (pure functions, no I/O, no DB). Web surfaces auto via the existing `listTools()` JSON shape. |
| ENG-036 | `email.send` tool (Layer 2) | Shipped | P1 | 2 | New `email.send` tool driven by a pluggable mailer (Resend, SendGrid, or Noop). Safe tenant-level settings live in `org_configs` as `email.provider`, `email.from`, and `email.rateLimitPerMin` with env fallbacks; provider API keys stay in env/vault (`RESEND_API_KEY` / `SENDGRID_API_KEY`) and are never stored in `org_configs`. Multi-tenant scope: every send writes a `usage_events` row with `metric: "email.sent"`. AC: workflow with `email.send` to a configured address delivers (smoke against the mailer's sandbox); failures degrade to `{ ok: false, error }` rather than throwing; per-org rate limit enforced via the Redis-backed `email.send` bucket. **Summary:** New `email.send` tool registered with `writeSide: true` so sandbox/validation runs auto-skip through the shared `tool.dry_run.skipped` gate for both tool-node and agent-planned invocations. Real providers call out through `fetchHttpTarget`, preserving SSRF guards, pinned DNS, timeout/body/redirect bounds, and avoiding Resend/SendGrid SDK deps. `getMailer(providerOverride)` resolves tenant/provider override → env provider → Noop, and provider `send()` never throws. Rate limiting now uses the shared `packages/data/src/rate-limit.ts` primitive wired into both the API process and the worker process through `setEngineRateLimiter`; worker-side tool execution is the actual enforcement point for workflow sends. The email-usage recorder mirrors the LLM recorder and is registered at both API + worker boot to `recordEmailUsage`; success, provider failure, and rate-limit rejection all write one telemetry row with provider, providerMessageId, ok/error, latencyMs, nodeId, and workflowId. `executeTool` accepts an optional execution context carrying org/run/node/workflow/email config; existing tools ignore it. Tests cover mailer resolution/body shapes/failures, rate-limit DI, email telemetry DI, metadata bounds, tenant default sender/rate cap, write-side dry-run skips, and schema rejection. |
| ENG-037 | `pdf.generate` tool (Layer 2) | Shipped | P1 | 2 | New `pdf.generate` tool renders an HTML / Markdown template into a PDF and uploads to `s3` (or the configured object store). Driven by `@react-pdf/renderer` or `puppeteer-core`. AC: workflow rendering an invoice template produces a downloadable PDF URL with a per-org-scoped path. Object store config via `JANUSLY_OBJECT_STORE_*` envs. **Summary:** `pdf.generate` registered in `tool-registry.ts` with `writeSide: true` and the standard `{ ok, provider, url, key, contentLength, error?, latencyMs }` envelope. Object-store abstraction in `object-store.ts` with three providers: `S3Provider` (uses `@aws-sdk/client-s3` + presigned-URL, supports MinIO/R2 via `JANUSLY_OBJECT_STORE_ENDPOINT`), `LocalDirProvider` (dev/test), and `NoopProvider` (default). Per-org key prefix `orgs/<orgId>/pdfs/<runId>/<nodeId>/<filename>` enforces multi-tenant isolation. New `objectstore` category + `integrations.pdf.rateLimitPerMin` (default 30) added to `org_configs` catalog. `setPdfUsageRecorder` DI seam wired at api + worker boot to `recordPdfUsage` with `metric: "pdf.generated"`, `quantity: contentLength`. **HTML rendering continuation:** the original v1 was Markdown-only because the prior assumption was that Node-native HTML→PDF required Chromium or a hosted SaaS. v2 lifts that carve-out without either: new optional `format: "markdown" \| "html"` field (defaults to `"markdown"` so every existing call site keeps working) routes to a new `renderHtmlToPdf` in `pdf-renderer.ts` that parses a sanitized HTML subset via `htmlparser2` (~50KB pure-JS dep, the only new runtime dep) and emits the same `pdfkit`-backed `Block[]` intermediate as the Markdown side. Closed whitelist covers `<h1>`-`<h6>`, `<p>`/`<div>`, `<br>`/`<hr>`, `<ul>`/`<ol>`/`<li>`, `<pre>`/`<code>`, inline `<strong>`/`<b>`, `<em>`/`<i>`, `<span>`, `<a href>` (http/https only — `javascript:` rejected), and **`<table>`/`<thead>`/`<tbody>`/`<tr>`/`<th>`/`<td>` with bounded `colspan` capped at 16** (the killer feature missing from Markdown — invoices, refund letters, contract summaries all need tables). Closed deny-set drops tag AND children for `<script>`/`<style>`/`<iframe>`/`<object>`/`<form>`/`<svg>` and silently no-ops for void deny-tags (`<img>`/`<input>`/`<link>`/`<meta>`/`<base>`/`<embed>` have no body — adding them to the deny-set is harmless documentation but achieves no extra drop, the test acknowledges this asymmetry explicitly). All attributes dropped except `<a href>` (validated against `^https?://` after trim) and cell `colspan`. Inline `<a href>` inside `<h1>`-`<h6>` is preserved as a clickable link annotation — the Block model migrated `heading.text: string` to `heading.tokens: InlineToken[]` (also tokens emit at heading-bold + heading-size for non-link styles); Markdown's heading parser now feeds the same shape via the existing `tokenizeMarkdownInline`. Variable substitution `{{name}}` runs pre-parse identically across both dialects. **Three defense-in-depth caps** for DoS guards, all throwing into the existing tool-wrapper `{ ok: false, error }` envelope: 200KB template byte cap (same as v1), 32-deep nesting cap (parser-bomb / billion-laughs), and new `MAX_BLOCKS = 5000` + `MAX_TOKENS_PER_BLOCK = 2000` to bound the per-template Block / token count so a pathological 25000-`<p>` template can't stall the worker event loop. Tests grew from 8 → 35 in `pdf-renderer.test.ts` (kept the 8 Markdown / variable-substitution / size-cap cases; added parser AST assertions for sanitization since pdfkit Flate-compresses content streams — byte inspection is not viable for body content): h1-h6 shape, inline `<strong>` / `<em>` / `<code>` / `<a href>` tokens, list shapes, table-with-thead-and-cells, `colspan` clamp, non-void deny-tag content drop, void deny-tag inert, `on*` attribute strip, `javascript:` href rejection, valid https href preservation, whitespace collapse, `<br>` inside `<pre>` opens a new line (regression on a reviewer-found bug where `<br>` inside `<pre>` escaped the pre block via `pushInline` fallthrough), heading carries link/bold tokens (regression on a reviewer-found bug where `pendingHeading.text += token.text` lost the link/bold style), `MAX_BLOCKS` rejection, `MAX_TOKENS_PER_BLOCK` rejection, unknown tag passes children through, depth cap. `tool-registry.pdf-generate.test.ts` grew from 9 → 11 (HTML format dispatch happy path; `format` default = `"markdown"` back-compat + `listTools()` declares `format` as optional). Multi-tenant key prefix, rate-limit gate, usage recorder, AI-fallback envelope, S3/local/noop provider selection all unchanged. The Inspector / `/tools` JSON shape automatically surfaces `format` via `describeShape`; AI prompt grammar continues to advertise tool name only (operators / `/ai/patch-workflow` set `format` post-generation). **Remaining intentionally:** styled cells inside tables (bold / link inside a `<td>`) render as plain concatenated text — table layout is single-line per cell in v1; multi-line wrapping + per-token cell positioning is the obvious v3 if a customer demand surfaces. |
| ENG-038 | `db.query.*` tools (Layer 2) | Gated | P2 | 2 | Three new tools — `db.query.read` (`SELECT`), `db.query.write` (`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`), `db.query.transaction` (multi-statement) — against a per-org configured Postgres connection (separate from Janusly's own DB). Parameterised statements only — never string-concat user input. AC: workflow connecting to a configured per-org DB executes a parameterised SELECT; SQL injection is rejected at parse time; the connection pool is bounded (max 5 per org). Closes §9.1.2 (form → SQL → email) and §9.5 (article rewriter analytics). **Gate:** Scoped out during the ENG-070 planning discussion and kept gated until concrete customer pull. The generic `db.query.*` abstraction is the wrong primitive for sales demos because the workflow author can't realistically know the customer's schema (which tables / columns exist, which constraints, etc.) and the multi-DB landscape (Postgres / MySQL / MSSQL / etc.) multiplies driver work. ENG-070 instead delivered specific integration tools (`slack.post`, `github.create_issue`, `webhook.send`) whose input schemas are known at design time, plus the credentials + usage + rate-limit infrastructure any future per-customer DB tool would reuse. Revisit this ticket only when (a) a specific customer demand surfaces with a concrete Postgres they want to integrate AND (b) we have a story for schema discovery (a `db.schema.describe` tool that lets the AI / operator see the columns before generating SQL). |
| ENG-039 | Diff UX (v3 vs v4 + AI patch preview) | Shipped | P2 | 2 | Web side: a "Diff" panel that computes the structural diff between two workflow versions (added/removed nodes, modified edges, config changes) and renders side-by-side. Optional "Suggest patch" button calls the existing `/ai/explain-workflow` route extended to also produce a unified-diff suggestion. AC: opening a workflow with > 1 version shows the diff panel for any version pair selection; the AI patch preview button degrades to fallback per the AGENTS.md AI fallback contract. **Summary:** Closed by adding the AI improvement-suggestion surface — new `POST /ai/suggest-improvement` (editor role, "ai" rate-limit bucket) returns 1-3 suggested workflow improvements with rationale + approachLabel + confidence, mirroring the `/ai/patch-workflow` response shape. The new helper `packages/ai/src/suggest-improvement.ts` accepts `LlmClient + workflow + optional focus`, calls `llm.generateObject({ schema })` and returns `{ mode, suggestions: [{ patchedWorkflowJson, rationale, approachLabel, confidence }], aiError? }`. **Envelope shape decision:** the LLM emits the proposed workflow as a JSON-stringified blob (`patchedWorkflowJson: z.string()`) rather than a typed `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` payload — empirically Anthropic's compiled-grammar cap rejects the wrapper-around-typed-workflow shape ("`The compiled grammar is too large`") even though the bare `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` fits and `/ai/generate-workflow` works. The string-payload shape sidesteps the cap entirely and is also OpenAI-strict-mode safe. Validation moves from structured-output-time to server-time but the safety guarantee is identical: the route runs `JSON.parse` → `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` → `sanitizeAiWorkflow` on every suggestion, drops the suggestion if any step fails, and degrades to fallback when none survive. Audited as `ai.workflow.improvement_suggested` with `{ mode, model, provider, suggestionsCount, topApproachLabel, focusProvided }` on AI-mode AND fallback paths per the AGENTS.md AI-mutation contract. `VersionHistoryPanel.tsx` now exposes a "Suggest improvement" button below the Compare-mode diff (visible only when two versions are picked AND the operator has editor/admin role); clicking calls the new route against the newer of the two compared versions. The result mounts a second `<WorkflowDiffView>` with `aiPatchRationale` populated. The state machine includes a chip strip for ≥ 2 suggestions so operators can switch between validated alternatives without changing the baseline diff. Fallback responses surface as a yellow ribbon naming the AI error — the structural diff stays untouched. The state clears whenever the active workflow changes, compare mode toggles, or the version pair changes, so a stale suggestion can't apply-mismatch against a different baseline; a `useRef` cancel flag also bails the click handler out of setState after `await` so an in-flight request that resolves AFTER one of those invalidations doesn't overwrite the cleared state. **Deviation from speculative AC:** the AC named `/ai/explain-workflow` as the route to extend; actual implementation chose a sibling `/ai/suggest-improvement` route — the structured-suggestion shape doesn't fit explain-workflow's text-only contract, and a new sibling keeps both surfaces' response types stable. **Reviewer fix (collateral):** stale-fetch race in the click handler (in-flight request resolves AFTER pair-change → overwrites cleared state) was caught by review and pinned with a new test that resolves the AI promise after the pair changes and asserts the suggestion never mounts. **Cross-package reuse:** moved `packages/ai/src/suggest-improvement.ts`'s secret-shape scrubber to import `scrubSecretShapes` from `@janusly/shared/src/error-signature` instead of duplicating the closed regex set — added `@janusly/shared` to `@janusly/ai`'s workspace deps. New tests: helper unit tests (6 cases: null-llm fallback, throw fallback, valid envelope, focus forwarding + 200-char trim, secret-shape scrubbing, system-prompt pinning), envelope schema tests (6 cases: shape, confidence bounds, approachLabel enum closure, rationale length, JSON-payload size bounds, JSON-payload→engine-schema round-trip), and 6 new `VersionHistoryPanel.test.tsx` cases (button visibility gating across compare-mode + role + selection count, AI-mode chip strip + rationale render, fallback-mode ribbon rendering, API-throw fallback, stale-fetch race fix, version-pair-change clears stale state). Smoke confirmed `mode: "ai"` against Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5-20251001 with the focus parameter producing real retry-add suggestions. |
| ENG-040 | MCP server authoring helpers | Shipped | P1 | 2 | Extend `packages/mcp-server` with workflow authoring helpers. `workflows.validate({ workflow })` proxies to `POST /validate` and has no side effects. `workflows.save` remains unavailable until ENG-052 defines the consent/audit model for MCP writes. AC for the partial slice: Claude Desktop can validate a workflow via MCP; no MCP write tools are advertised; stale direct calls to `workflows.save` are rejected before the HTTP API is called. **Summary:** `packages/mcp-server/src/tools.ts` now advertises six tools (5 read-only + `workflows.validate`) and rejects direct `workflows.save` calls with a consent-policy error. The server still has zero DB access and zero audit writes of its own. AGENTS.md and `packages/mcp-server/README.md` document the no-write posture. MCP tests assert the six-tool catalog and that `workflows.save` never calls `/workflows/save`. The original save-through-MCP AC is deferred to ENG-052. **Continuation:** Catalog grew from 6 → 11 tools with five new read-only authoring helpers — `workflows.versions` (list every saved version), `workflows.health` (0-100 score + 6 sub-scores), `workflows.readiness` (POST pre-flight against safety / rollback / approval / secret gates, parallel to `workflows.validate`), `runs.list` (recent runs newest-first with optional `workflowId` filter), and `dlq.list` (DLQ entries with optional `status` filter). All five are pure proxies of existing read-only API routes — no DB access from MCP, no audit writes from the MCP side, no LLM cost. JSON Schema input descriptors mirror the upstream `limit` 1-200 caps so Claude can't pass invalid params. Test catalog assertion bumps to 11 tools alphabetised; per-tool happy path + required-arg validation + numeric-coercion tests added. The `workflows.save` rejection stays pinned. The original save-through-MCP scope remains tracked under ENG-052 (write consent/audit policy). |
| ENG-041 | Refactor api index to route registry | Shipped | P1 | 2 | `apps/api/src/index.ts` hand-rolled a 33-route `if (req.method === "X" && req.url === "Y") { ... }` chain. Phase 2 adds 12+ more routes (one per ENG-030..ENG-040 that exposes API surface). Linearly extending the chain is unmaintainable. Refactor to an Open/Closed route registry: `routes: Route[]` array of `{ method, match, role?, skipAuth?, handler }` entries; the `http.createServer` dispatcher iterates the registry (first-match-wins), runs `requireAuth` + `requireRole`, and calls the handler. AC: every existing route's external behaviour is preserved (gates green); adding a new route is a single `routes.push({...})` (or array literal append) — no edit to the dispatch loop. **Summary:** New `apps/api/src/routes.ts` carries the `Route` types + `matchesRoute` helper. `index.ts` registers all 33 existing routes plus the new ENG-029 cancel route as 34 entries; the dispatcher is ~15 lines (first-match-wins, runs `requireAuth` only when `skipAuth` is false, runs `requireRole` only when `role` is set). All multi-tenant scope guards stay inside their handlers. AGENTS.md gains a one-liner Open/Closed routing invariant; future Phase 2 tickets (ENG-030, ENG-038, ENG-040) plug in via `routes.push({...})`. |
| ENG-042 | Run input form in AI Studio | Shipped | P1 | 2 | ENG-028 adds the workflow input contract and API validation, but the Studio still needs a run-start form so users can provide values before pressing Run. AC: when the current workflow declares `inputs`, the Run action opens a schema-driven form, validates required fields client-side, and sends `POST /start` as `{ workflow, input }`; object, array, and primitive root inputs are supported; workflows without inputs keep the one-click run path. **Summary:** New `RunInputDialog` component (`apps/web/src/components/RunInputDialog.tsx`) renders a schema-driven form built from the workflow's declared `WorkflowInputSchemaShape` (string/number/boolean/object/array + enum + required). Local validation gates submit (required-empty + number-NaN + array-not-JSON); the engine's `validateInputs` stays the source of truth and re-validates on submit. Server errors come back from `POST /start` as a JSONPath envelope (`{ errors: ["$.invoiceId is required", …] }`); the dialog maps each error to the matching field by stripping `$.` and longest-prefix-matching against the schema's collected paths, with unmappable messages rendering in a top-of-form banner. Visual posture deliberately reads as a lifted-up `panel-card`: `--we-surface` background, `--we-line` border, 14px radius, soft fade-in + slide-up (honoring `prefers-reduced-motion`); cobalt-soft icon chip in the header anchors workflow identity; `command-button-primary` with `Play` lucide icon for submit; `AlertCircle` prefix on per-field error rows; `*` required marker in `--we-primary`. ESC + backdrop + close button all dismiss; first field focuses on mount; full ARIA wiring (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, `aria-required`, `aria-describedby` linking error spans). `apps/web/src/api.ts` got a `/start`-scoped 400 peek — when `POST /start` responds with `{ errors: string[] }`, the helper returns the parsed envelope instead of throwing, so the dialog can surface field-level errors while every other endpoint still throws on 400. `apps/web/src/App.tsx` split `startWorkflow` into a gate (open dialog when `currentWorkflowInputs` is declared) + a `startRunWith` fetcher both paths share, plus a `submitRunInput` handler. `apps/web/src/Layout.tsx` got an optional `overlay` slot so the dialog mounts above the workspace without portaling. 8 new jsdom + RTL dialog tests cover required marker, local validation gate, stale local-error clearing, number coercion, JSONPath server-error mapping, ESC + close button, boolean checkbox + enum select, and array root via JSON textarea. A 2-test `api.ts` unit guard pins that only `/start` resolves the field-error envelope; other endpoints still throw. Web workspace tests +10. **Collateral fix from manual smoke:** local field-level errors set on a failed submit now clear surgically as the operator types into a previously-empty field — the original implementation only reset the error map on the *next* submit, so a stale "X is required" lingered visibly even after the user typed a valid value. Threaded a `clearLocalError(path)` callback through `FieldProps` and call it in `setValue` whenever the field's state mutates; only the touched field's error drops, the rest stay until the user fixes them. |
| ENG-043 | Treat env templates as secrets | Shipped | P0 | — | `{{env.NAME}}` currently renders from `process.env` but does not add the resolved value to the redaction list. AC: `renderTemplateWithRedactions` records env-derived values; tests cover `{{env.OPENAI_API_KEY}}` and confirm no raw value reaches node state, event payloads, DLQ rows, or audit metadata. **Summary:** Three-line patch in `packages/engine/src/template.ts:env.*` branch — mirrors the existing secret branch's redaction-list bookkeeping, including the `length >= 4` floor that prevents over-redacting toy values like `"dev"`/`"prod"`. Env values that flow through the template renderer (`OPENAI_API_KEY`, `JANUSLY_API_SERVICE_TOKEN`, etc.) now end up in `redactValues`'s pass and never reach `run_nodes.state_json`, `run_events.payload`, `dead_letters.error_json`, or `audit_logs.metadata`. Backward compatible: `{{env.NODE_ENV}}` and similar short-value templates keep rendering verbatim. Five new tests in `template.test.ts` pin the capture, the length floor, the empty-env case, the OPENAI_API_KEY end-to-end scrub, and a combined env+secret scenario. No back-fill of pre-existing leaked rows (per the project's "don't back-fill" convention from ENG-001). |
| ENG-044 | Add runtime cancellation guards | Shipped | P0 | 2 | Enforce the documented cancellation semantics at runtime. `WorkflowRuntime.executeQueuedNode` must check `getRunStatus(runId)` before `markNodeRunning` and skip execution when the run is already `cancelled`; after a node succeeds, check again before `enqueueReadyNodes`. `markNodeRunning` should become a conditional `queued -> running` transition, not an unconditional update. AC: cancelling a run with queued jobs prevents those jobs from executing; cancelling while a node is running prevents downstream scheduling; regression tests cover queued, waiting, and just-finished nodes. **Summary:** Three layered guards in `core/runtime.ts`: (1) pre-execution `getRunStatus` check at the top of `executeQueuedNode` skips queued jobs against terminal runs and emits a `node.skipped` event; (2) `markNodeRunning` is now an atomic conditional `queued → running` UPDATE that returns `boolean` — mirrors `tryClaimNodeForQueue`'s pattern, catches the cancellation-mid-claim race; (3) post-success `getRunStatus` re-check skips downstream scheduling when cancellation landed while a node was running. `enqueueReadyNodes` also gets a head-of-method guard for any indirect caller (subworkflow notifier, future paths). Both `cancelled` and `failed` count as terminal. Interface bumped in `core/types.ts` + adapter passthrough. New `core/runtime.test.ts` (9 tests) using a hand-rolled mock `ExecutionStore` covers the cancelled-pre / failed-pre / claim-fail / happy-path / cancel-mid-running / retry / retry-after-cancel / direct-enqueueReadyNodes branches without touching Postgres. Engine test count: 157 → 167 (+10). Strengthens the existing engine-atomicity invariant; no regression to `startRun`, DLQ, or worker SIGTERM contracts. |
| ENG-045 | Share status constants | Shipped | P1 | — | Eliminate drift between API, engine, DB, docs, and web around statuses such as `cancelled` vs `canceled`.
