AC: `apps/web`, `apps/api`, and `packages/engine` import the same constants; no hard-coded `canceled` remains; tests fail if a status value diverges. **Summary:** New zero-dep `packages/shared/src/status.ts` exports `nodeOpenStatusValues` / `nodeCancellableStatusValues` / `nodeTerminalStatusValues` / `nodeStatusValues` + run-level analogs + three readonly Sets (`TERMINAL_NODE_STATUSES`, `TERMINAL_RUN_STATUSES`, `NODE_OPEN_STATUSES`) and type guards for persisted/API payloads. Engine `core/types.ts` re-exports `NodeStatus`/`RunStatus` types from shared; `persistence.ts` cancellation path uses `nodeCancellableStatusValues` (the `running`-excluded subset); `updateRunStatusFromNodes` uses the open-node type guard. API `/run/cancel`'s 409 guard uses the terminal-run type guard. Web added `@janusly/shared` as a workspace dep and imports the type guard directly from the subpath (`@janusly/shared/src/status`) to avoid pulling Zod into the browser bundle. **Discovered bug:** `apps/web/src/App.tsx:37` had `new Set(['succeeded', 'failed', 'canceled'])` (single-L typo) — the active-run polling loop never short-circuited on cancellation, so the web kept polling `/status` indefinitely after a cancel. Fixed via the shared constant. 16 shared tests pin the constants, guards, and type narrowing (any future single-L typo fails the build). |
| ENG-046 | Wire runtime learning metadata | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Make the learning/improvement path real by passing run metadata explicitly instead of expecting it inside `getRunContext`. Add `ExecutionStore.getRunMetadata(runId)` returning `orgId`, `workflowId?`, `workflowVersionId`, and `createdBy?`; use it in `WorkflowRuntime` for `updateRoutingStats`, `getRoutingStats`, and `evaluateImprovement`. AC: successful/failed router runs write `routing_stats` rows scoped by org; `decide()` receives live `rlStats`; `workflow_improvements` records are created only when the run has enough workflow metadata; unit tests verify no no-op path when `orgId` exists. **Summary:** Three previously-silent failure paths in the self-learning loop are now wired: (1) `routing_stats` writes happen on every healthy run (was no-op because `(context as any)?.orgId` was undefined and the repo's `if (!orgId) return` guard short-circuited); (2) `decide()` receives the org's prior reinforcement counters as `rlStats` (was always undefined → bandit term in `applyRlAdjustments` was dead code); (3) `recordWorkflowImprovement` writes use a structured `workflowId` from a join through `workflow_versions` (was missing or stringly-typed off the loose context bag). Added `getRunMetadata(runId)` to `packages/engine/src/persistence.ts` — single-row SELECT joining `runs` + `workflow_versions` returning `{ orgId, workflowVersionId, workflowId, createdBy }`. The `leftJoin` keeps the helper resilient when the version row is deleted mid-run (`workflowId: null`, improvement-eval no-ops, run still completes). New `getRunMetadata` method on `ExecutionStore` (`packages/engine/src/core/types.ts`) wired through `PostgresExecutionStore`. `WorkflowRuntime.executeQueuedNode` loads metadata once at entry and threads it into the router branch (`updateRoutingStats({ orgId: metadata?.orgId, ... })`, `getRoutingStats(metadata.orgId)` before `decide()`) and the improvement-evaluation helper (now takes `metadata` as a third argument; skips silently when `metadata?.workflowId` is null). When `getRunMetadata` returns null (run row deleted between scheduling and worker pickup), the executor still runs so node-status correctness and engine atomicity are preserved. **5 new tests** in `core/runtime.test.ts` cover: orgId forwarded into `updateRoutingStats`; live `rlStats` reach `decide()` with the org's counters; improvement records are written with structured workflowId; improvement is skipped on null workflowId; defensive null-metadata fall-through. **Reviewer fixes (collateral):** (a) the existing stale `vi.mock` for `'./improvementEngine'` and `'../routing'` (paths that didn't resolve to anything) were replaced with proper `@janusly/data/src/routingStatsRepo`, `@janusly/data/src/improvementsRepo`, and `@janusly/data/src/workflowRollbackRepo` mocks — without these, the new runtime tests would have hit Postgres on every router-decision call and ECONNREFUSED-failed in CI; (b) `getRunMetadata` is now loaded inside the try-block AFTER `markNodeRunning` wins the atomic claim, not before — keeps the cancellation race window between `getRunStatus` and the claim as tight as the original; (c) the rollback path's dead `typeof context.createdBy === "string"` check was dropped — `RunContext` is keyed by node id, so that branch only fired when a workflow had a node literally named "createdBy", which is never; the path now reads directly from `metadata.createdBy ?? "system"`. Multi-tenant scope is strengthened (every routing-stats / improvement write is now org-scoped from a structured source); engine atomicity is preserved (metadata reads are post-claim and read-only). |
| ENG-047 | Normalize router candidates | Shipped | P1 | — | Fix the mismatch between the AI prompt (`candidates: [{ id }]`) and `DecisionCandidate` (`nodeId`). Update the prompt to require `nodeId`, normalize legacy `{ id } -> { nodeId }` in runtime for backward compatibility, and validate router/router_llm config in `validateWorkflow`. AC: AI-generated router workflows validate and run; candidates missing both `id` and `nodeId` fail validation with a clear issue; tests cover `router`, `router_llm`, and legacy payloads. **Summary:** Three coordinated surfaces close a silent end-to-end break for AI-generated router workflows. (1) `GENERATE_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` now advertises `Array<{nodeId, avgCost?, avgLatencyMs?, successRate?}>` for `router` and `Array<{nodeId}>` for `router_llm`, replacing the bogus `{id, scoreFn?}` shape that didn't match `DecisionCandidate`. (2) New `normalizeRouterCandidates` boundary helper in `packages/engine/src/core/runtime.ts` runs before `decide()`: it accepts the canonical `nodeId` and the legacy `id` (preferring `nodeId`), strips the legacy `id` from the output, preserves `avgCost`/`avgLatencyMs`/`successRate`/`metadata` verbatim, and drops entries without any identifier so the engine never sees `nodeId: undefined`. Domain stays strict (`DecisionCandidate.nodeId` required); engine handles the loose input at the boundary. (3) `validateWorkflow` gained a router/router_llm block with three new stable issue codes — `router_missing_candidates`, `router_invalid_candidate`, `router_candidate_missing_node_id` — so malformed workflows surface a clear validation error instead of running with degenerate decisions. Seven new tests: 4 in `workflow-validation.test.ts` covering empty, canonical, legacy, and identifier-less candidates; 3 in `core/runtime.test.ts` proving legacy `{id}` produces a real `chosenNodeId`, that scoring fields survive the normaliser across mixed-shape candidates, and that `config.strategy` is actually plumbed into `decide()`. **Reviewer fixes (collateral):** the runtime now also forwards `node.config.strategy` (closed enum: `auto`/`cheapest`/`fastest`/`balanced`) to `decide()` — the prompt advertised this field but the runtime previously dropped it on the floor; a stale `vi.mock('../decisionEngine', ...)` block in `runtime.test.ts` was deleted (the path didn't resolve to anything and the symbols it intercepted live in `improvementEngine`, already mocked separately); and the two happy-path validator tests now assert `{ valid: true, issues: [] }` instead of a router-prefix filter so a future drift in any other rule fails loudly. Old runs persisted with `chosenNodeId: undefined` are not backfilled (forward-only fix per scope). |
| ENG-048 | Bound outbound HTTP execution | Shipped | P1 | — | Harden `http` node and `http.request` tool beyond SSRF/DNS-rebinding protection. Add default timeout, max response bytes, and redirect policy. Suggested defaults: `timeoutMs=30000`, `maxResponseBytes=1_000_000`, and redirects either disabled or revalidated through `fetchHttpTarget`. AC: slow upstreams time out; oversized responses fail before loading full body into memory; redirects to private IPs are blocked; tests cover slow server, large body, and public-to-private redirect. **Summary:** `fetchHttpTarget` now returns a fully-consumed `HttpResult` (`{ statusCode, ok, body, headers }`) instead of a half-read `Response`, so the byte cap can't be sidestepped by a caller asking for `.body` directly. Three runtime bounds default-on with env + per-call overrides: timeout (30000ms / `JANUSLY_HTTP_TIMEOUT_MS` / `init.timeoutMs`) via a single AbortController shared across redirect hops; body cap (1_000_000 bytes / `JANUSLY_HTTP_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` / `init.maxResponseBytes`) via a Content-Length pre-check plus a streaming byte counter that aborts mid-stream; redirect handling (5 hops / `JANUSLY_HTTP_MAX_REDIRECTS` / `init.maxRedirects`) is now `redirect: "manual"` with each Location re-resolved through the same SSRF pin — a 302 to e.g. `169.254.169.254` is rejected at the second resolve, closing the redirect-bypass hole the existing pin didn't catch. 301/302/303 coerce method to GET per spec; 307/308 preserve. Both callers (`http` node + `http.request` tool) updated to consume `HttpResult`; the tool's Zod schema gained the three optional override fields and `GENERATE_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` advertises them so AI-generated workflows can opt in. 8 new tests in `http-policy.test.ts` use a kernel-port-assigned in-process `node:http` server (cleaned up via tracked sockets) covering timeout, Content-Length precheck, streaming overflow, redirect-to-private revalidation (mid-flight env flip), redirect chain limit, default-limit happy path, per-call override raising the cap, and malformed workflow-provided override values falling back to safe defaults. |
| ENG-049 | Add safe persistence chokepoint | Shipped | P1 | — | Create a single persistence sanitizer for `run_events.payload`, `run_nodes.state_json`, `run_nodes.error_json`, DLQ rows, and audit metadata. It should combine value-based redaction, sensitive-key redaction, and optional size bounding. AC: `appendEvent`, node state writes, DLQ enqueue, and audit all call `safePersistPayload`; tests search the persisted JSON for known secret values and sensitive key values; payloads above a configured size are truncated with metadata preserving original byte count. Depends on ENG-042/ENG-043. **Summary:** New `packages/engine/src/safe-persist.ts` chokepoint stacks three sanitizers into one function: value-based redaction (delegated to existing `redactValues` from `template.ts` when the caller provides a `redactedValues` list), sensitive-key redaction via the closed regex `SENSITIVE_KEY_PATTERN` (matches `secret*`/`password*`/`token*`/`api*key`/`authorization`/`cookie`/`x-api-key`/`client*secret`/`private*key` whole keys, case-insensitive), and size bounding (default 256 KB / env `JANUSLY_PERSIST_MAX_BYTES` / per-call `maxBytes` override; over-cap payloads become a `{ __truncated: true, originalBytes, maxBytes, preview }` sentinel). Wired into 5 surfaces: `appendEvent` (run_events.payload — default cap), `markNodeWaiting`/`markNodeSkipped`/`markNodeSucceeded` (run_nodes.state_json — 1 MB cap because http body cap is 1 MB), `markNodeFailed` (run_nodes.error_json — 64 KB cap), `cancelRun` cancellation reason (state_json — 1 MB cap), DLQ adapter's `errorJson` (64 KB) + `workflowJson` and `nodeJson` (key-redacted but never truncated, since `/dlq/replay` reconstructs from these), and `apps/api/src/index.ts:audit()` (audit_logs.metadata — 64 KB cap, replacing the now-deleted inline `redactAuditMetadata` + `SENSITIVE_AUDIT_KEYS` regex that had the same shape). Engine-layer pre-redaction in `executeNode` stays as defense in depth — every persistence write now goes through both layers. **11 new tests:** 9 in `safe-persist.test.ts` covering value redaction, sensitive-key whole-key match (with `tokenizer` non-match assertion to pin the no-substring-match contract), truncation with sentinel, per-call/env cap overrides, primitives passthrough, and `undefined` handling; 2 in `persistence.test.ts` mocking `@janusly/db` so `appendEvent` and `markNodeFailed` capture the exact `db.insert`/`db.update` argument and assert `Authorization: "[redacted]"` plus that the literal secret token string never appears in the serialized JSON. **AGENTS.md** gained a one-line invariant: every jsonb write to the 5 surfaces must go through `safePersistPayload`; future callers that bypass the chokepoint are a wire-leak regression. Forward-only fix per the project's "don't back-fill" convention. |
| ENG-050 | Repair concurrent workflow saves | Shipped | P2 | — | Make `/workflows/save` resilient to simultaneous saves for the same `(orgId, workflowId)`. The current transaction computes `nextVersion` from latest version and relies on the unique index to protect integrity; concurrent writers can collide and produce a poor user-facing error. AC: two concurrent saves both complete with distinct versions, or one retries once and returns a clear conflict; implementation uses advisory lock, `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, or unique-constraint retry; tests simulate concurrent save attempts. **Summary:** Extracted the inline save block in `apps/api/src/index.ts:POST /workflows/save` into a new `saveWorkflowVersion` helper at `apps/api/src/workflows-save.ts` that wraps the existing `db.transaction` body in a bounded unique-constraint retry loop (`MAX_SAVE_ATTEMPTS = 3`). The `isRetryableSaveViolation` probe walks both the direct error and `err.cause` chain (Drizzle wraps the pg driver error) and matches Postgres `23505` violations on **either** of the two retryable constraints in this transaction: `workflow_versions_org_workflow_version_idx` (two writers compute the same `nextVersion`) **and** `workflows_pkey` (two first-time savers race on the caller-provided `workflowId`). On a hit, the loser re-reads the now-committed state, bumps `nextVersion`, and re-runs the transaction; the retry's fresh `existingWorkflow` lookup sees the winner's row and skips the `INSERT INTO workflows` branch on the second attempt. Pathological contention falls through to a typed `kind: "conflict"` result that the route maps to a clean `HTTP 409 Conflict — please retry` envelope (carrying `attempts`) instead of the generic 5xx the inline transaction used to emit. The audit row metadata now includes `attempts` so operators can see whether a save resolved on first try or after retry. New `apps/api/src/workflows-save.test.ts` covers 14 cases across three describes: helper happy paths (first-save inserts a workflow row + v1; subsequent saves bump version; name updates flow through; missing workflow id is generated), retry behaviour (single-retry on version-idx 23505, two-retry under rapid contention, exhausted → conflict, non-23505 rethrows without retry, 23505 on a different constraint rethrows, versionId regenerated per attempt, `workflows_pkey` 23505 also retries to cover the first-save race), and the `isRetryableSaveViolation` probe (version-idx match, workflows-pkey match, bare 23505 defensive default, `cause`-chain unwrap, mismatched constraint rejects, non-Error inputs reject). Mocks `@janusly/db` with a per-attempt-aware transaction stub so each retry sees the appropriate "latest version" snapshot, mirroring the rollback-test pattern. Live concurrent-save smoke against `pnpm dev` confirmed both the retry path (4-way burst landed v2/v3/v4/v5 with `attempts: 1/1/2/3`) and the 409 envelope (10-way burst landed v1/v2/v3 with `attempts: 1/2/3` and 7 clean 409s — zero 5xx). No schema/migration change — the existing unique indexes already protect integrity; retry is a pure UX layer on top. |
| ENG-051 | Add usage breakdown reporting | Shipped | P2 | 4 | Extend usage reporting beyond `metric -> quantity` token totals. Add breakdown by provider, model, workflow, node, mode, and day using either a new query endpoint or a pre-aggregated table. AC: `/billing/usage?breakdown=provider,model,workflow,node` returns token totals, call count, fallback count, latency aggregates, and costUsd; web Usage card can show provider/model cost; no unbounded scan is introduced. **Summary:** All six AC dimensions ship in v1 — `provider`, `model`, `mode`, `day`, `node`, `workflow`. New `getUsageBreakdown(orgId, dimensions, windowDays)` aggregator + closed `USAGE_BREAKDOWN_DIMENSIONS = ["provider", "model", "mode", "day", "node", "workflow"]` enum in `packages/engine/src/billing.ts`. Reads the same bounded 30-day / 10k-row slice that `getUsageSummary` already uses (no unbounded scan, no schema migration), groups in-process via the pure `aggregateUsageBreakdown(rows, dimensions)` helper. Per-bucket returns `quantity` (token total), `callCount`, `fallbackCount` (rows where `metadata.mode === "fallback"`), `costUsd` (sum, **`null` when every row has null cost** — never coerce-to-zero, otherwise unknown-model buckets falsely report `$0`), and `latency.{p50Ms, p95Ms, avgMs}`. **Workflow/node attribution plumbed end-to-end:** extended `UsageRecord` and the `LlmClient.generateText`/`generateObject` context types with `workflowId?: string`. The recorder writes `metadata.workflowId` whenever the field is populated. Every LLM caller plumbs the workflow id when it's in scope: `/ai/explain-workflow` reads it off the body, `/ai/review-workflow` and `/ai/suggest-improvement` read it after `WorkflowSchema.safeParse`, `/ai/patch-workflow` pulls it from `dlq.workflowJson.id` (the existing `failingWorkflowId`), `/ai/explain-run` resolves it via a small `workflow_versions` lookup that mirrors `getRunMetadata`, and the engine's `ai`/`agent` executors plumb it from `NodeContext.workflowId` (populated once per node execution by `executeNode` via `getRunMetadata` — replacing the older `getRunOrgId` lookup with a single join that returns both). `/ai/generate-workflow` leaves it absent (no workflow exists at LLM-call time); those rows naturally bucket under `workflow=unknown`. The `node` dimension piggybacks on `metadata.nodeId` which the recorder already wrote via the existing `NodeContext.nodeId` plumbing. The `/billing/usage` route accepts `?breakdown=provider,model,mode,day,node,workflow` (any combination). When unset or empty, returns the existing flat `Record<metric, quantity>` for back-compat; when set, returns `{ summary, breakdown, windowDays: 30 }`. Unknown dimensions return `400 Unknown breakdown dimension: <name>`. The web "Usage summary" panel-card in `RightPanel.tsx` exposes a chip strip ("Group by Provider · Model · Mode · Day · Node · Workflow") that, when one or more chips activate, fetches the breakdown and renders a top-10 bucket list sorted by `costUsd` desc with quantity/k-M, cost as `$X.XX` or `—`, and p95 latency formatted as `1.2s` / `320ms`. A `+ N more` affordance reveals the rest. Refetches on `platformVersion` bumps AND on a local refresh-nonce so the card's Refresh button updates the breakdown in lockstep with the flat summary even when the parent's `onRefreshPlatform` doesn't bump `platformVersion`. New tests: `packages/engine/src/billing.test.ts` covers the aggregator (27 cases including empty / single dim / multi dim / dedupe / missing-metadata→`unknown` / mode bucketing / fallbackCount / costUsd null/mixed/all-null / latency p50/p95/avg / day-bucket UTC boundary / **node grouping with `unknown` fallback** / **workflow grouping with same-workflow-across-nodes consolidation** / **workflow + node combined for per-workflow node hotspots** / **`/ai/generate-workflow`-style rows bucket under `workflow=unknown`** / six-axis enum + type guard); `apps/api/src/billing-route.test.ts` covers the parser (10 cases including the new node + workflow accept paths); `apps/web/src/components/UsageSummaryCard.test.tsx` covers the UI (10 cases). The recorder + LLM-client context plumbing pulls `packages/ai`, `packages/data`, `packages/engine`, and the API into the change set — but each individual edit is small (1-2 lines per caller) and behavior-preserving when the optional `workflowId` is absent. No schema/migration change. **Legacy backfill:** new `scripts/backfill-usage-workflowid.ts` (run via `pnpm backfill:usage-workflowid`) is an idempotent one-off that attributes pre-attribution `usage_events` rows. For each row missing `metadata.workflowId`, it tries the `runs → workflow_versions` join (mirroring `getRunMetadata`) — when the join resolves, the real workflow id is written; when it doesn't (no runId, deleted run, orphaned version), the row gets the sentinel `"_legacy_pre_attribution"`. The web's `bucketLabel` helper renders the sentinel as **"Legacy"** so operators see "Legacy / wf_real / unknown" instead of a single noisy "unknown" bucket conflating three different kinds of missing attribution. Cost / token / latency are preserved; only `metadata.workflowId` is added — no rows deleted. |
| ENG-052 | Define MCP write consent policy | Shipped | P1 | 2 | Block MCP write tools until an explicit consent/audit model exists. Define tool-level allowlist, dry-run mode, production consent requirements, service-principal actor identity, per-tool rate limits, and audit metadata. AC: `workflows.save`, `workflows.run`, `runs.cancel`, and `dlq.replay` remain unavailable over MCP until this policy is implemented; tests prove write tools reject without consent; every accepted write tool emits an audit row with `source: "mcp"`. Expands/guards ENG-040. **Summary:** Two-layer consent gate: process-wide `JANUSLY_MCP_WRITES_ENABLED=true` (default false) AND per-tenant `org_configs.mcp.writeConsent: boolean` (default false). Both must be true for any MCP write to proceed; either being false returns HTTP 403 with `code: "mcp_process_disabled"` / `code: "mcp_tenant_disabled"`. Source attribution rides on a self-declared `x-janusly-source: mcp` request header that the MCP client (`packages/mcp-server/src/api-client.ts`) sets on every request; the API auth layer honors it ONLY in service-token mode (so a browser-issued Supabase JWT can't claim MCP source) and stamps the resolved `AuthContext.source`. The new `apps/api/src/mcp-consent.ts` helper centralises three pieces: `isMcpWriteAllowed(orgId)` (env + tenant gate), `mcpAuditMetadata(auth)` (canonical `metadata.source: "mcp"` + actor block with `serviceTokenSuffix` for forensics), and `mcpRateLimitBucket(actionKey)` (stable per-tool bucket name). `POST /workflows/save` is the first accepted write — when `auth.source === "mcp"` the route runs the consent gate, enforces the `mcp.workflows.save` rate limit (60/min/org), then proceeds through the existing `saveWorkflowVersion` chain and merges `mcpAuditMetadata(auth)` into the audit row. The MCP catalog (`packages/mcp-server/src/tools.ts`) splits into `READ_TOOLS` (always) and `WRITE_TOOLS` (appended only when `mcpWritesEnabled()` returns true); `listTools(env)` is the pure variant. `workflows.save` accepts an optional `dryRun: true` argument that routes to `/validate` instead of `/workflows/save` so an MCP client can preview without persisting. The other three writes (`workflows.run`, `runs.cancel`, `dlq.replay`) stay absent from the catalog — adding any is a `WRITE_TOOLS` push + the same gate / rate-limit / audit wiring already in place. New tests: `apps/api/src/mcp-consent.test.ts`, `packages/mcp-server/src/tools.test.ts` extended (catalog flips on env, dry-run routes to `/validate`, env-off rejection clear error), `api-client.test.ts` extended (every request carries `x-janusly-source: mcp`). AGENTS.md gains the policy invariant; `packages/mcp-server/README.md` documents the env + tenant flow + audit-row contract; `.env.example` adds the new env. Expands ENG-040's MCP server with the write surface its AC asked for. |
| ENG-053 | Ship failure recovery MVP | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Turn Janusly’s strongest product wedge into an end-to-end loop: DLQ entry -> failure explanation -> suggested patch -> diff preview -> apply new workflow version -> replay/eval -> improvement confidence. AC: a failed node can be opened from the Recovery queue, the system explains root cause using run context, proposes a structured workflow patch, shows a version diff, saves an operator-approved new version, and replays or starts a validation run. This is the main “self-healing workflow operator” demo. Depends on ENG-039 and parts of ENG-025. **Summary:** New `suggestWorkflowPatch` helper in `packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts` accepts LlmClient + workflow + failed-node id + redacted errorJson + run events, calls `llm.generateObject({ schema })` with a structured-output schema owned by the route, returns `{ mode, suggestedWorkflow, rationale, aiError? }`. Pure function, never throws — LLM null / quota / network / schema-mismatch all degrade to `mode: "fallback"` returning the original workflow untouched plus a rationale telling the operator to fix manually. New `POST /ai/patch-workflow` route (`role: "editor"`, rate-limited in the shared `"ai"` bucket) loads the DLQ row + run events, calls the helper with `PatchEnvelopeSchema = { workflow: AiGenerationWorkflowSchema, rationale }` (reuses the existing 11-type union), runs `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow` on AI-mode output (any sanitize failure cascades to fallback), and audits both paths as `ai.workflow.patch_suggested`. New `RecoveryDialog.tsx` renders a 6-state machine (`idle/loading/review/applying/applied/error`): operator clicks "Generate suggestion" → spinner → `<WorkflowDiffView>` (ENG-072) renders the diff with the LLM rationale → "Apply & replay" chains `POST /workflows/save` then `POST /dlq/replay`. Apply is disabled when the suggestion came back as `mode: "fallback"`. Hooked into `DeadLettersPanel` via a new "Suggest fix" button on the selected-row action bar; reuses the `run-input-*` modal CSS tokens. 6 ai-workspace tests + 4 web jsdom tests cover the helper, the dialog state machine, the chained save+replay, and the fallback-disabled state. **ENG-025 dep deferred:** the MVP uses the existing `POST /dlq/replay` for validation (operator sees pass/fail in the next run row); eval-as-a-framework lands in ENG-025 (Phase 3, still gated). Follow-up tickets ENG-077..ENG-083 (added below) flesh out the eight limitations the MVP doesn't cover. |

The merger (`applyStructuralPatchToWorkflow`), the schema (`AiPatchStructuralEnvelope`), and the audit metadata field (`patchStyle: "structural"`) are all already type-agnostic so no schema / merger / audit-wiring changes needed. New nullable `rate_limit_per_min` column on `mcp_tool_descriptors` (migration `20260518024938_fearless_slyde`); `setToolFlags` in `packages/data/src/mcpConnectionsRepo.ts` now returns `{ before, after }` so the existing admin route can audit on-change. `executeMcpTool` (`packages/engine/src/mcp-tool-executor.ts`) computes `effectiveRateLimit = descriptor.rateLimitPerMin ?? input.rateLimitPerMin` before the limiter call — descriptor override (when set) wins over the org default, NULL keeps today's behaviour. Admin route `POST /mcp/connections/:alias/tools/:toolName` body schema extended with `rateLimitPerMin?: number | null` (integer in `[1, 10_000]` or `null` to clear); new audit action `mcp.tool.rate_limit_set` fires only on actual change with `metadata: { alias, toolName, before, after }`. AGENTS.md MCP client bullet updated to document the override + drop "per-tool rate-limit overrides" from the v2 carve-out + bump the audit-actions count from 6 to 7. 2 new executor tests (descriptor override wins; null falls back to caller default) + 3 new route tests (positive int persists + audits, null clears + audits, 10_001 rejected with 400). No web change (v1 API-only). **Status note (2026-05-18b):** `exposeToAi` per-connection flag with prompt-injection sanitisation shipped. New nullable `expose_to_ai boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` column on `mcp_connections` (migration `20260518173101_odd_proudstar`); `updateConnection` in `packages/data/src/mcpConnectionsRepo.ts` now returns `{ before, after }` so the existing admin route can audit on-change; new `listExposedMcpToolsForAi(orgId)` helper joins connections × descriptors scoped to `(enabled, exposeToAi, enabled)` and caps at 60 tools / 20 KB total with a `(N more truncated)` footer. New `sanitizeMcpToolDescription` in `packages/shared/src/error-signature.ts` strips ASCII control chars → reuses `scrubSecretShapes` → length-caps at 300 chars. New `composeGenerationSystemPrompt(base, exposedTools)` in `apps/api/src/ai-prompts.ts` returns `base` unchanged when no tools are exposed (zero behavior change for non-opt-in orgs) and otherwise appends a data-framed section with each tool prefixed by `- <alias>.<toolName>:` so injection attempts read as list items not commands. Admin route `POST /mcp/connections/:alias` gains `exposeToAi?: boolean` body field; new audit action `mcp.connection.expose_to_ai_set` fires only on actual change with `before`/`after` metadata. Awareness-only in v1 — the LLM is told to emit a `noop` placeholder with id `mcp_<alias>_<toolName>` (mirror of the `wait_*` / `schedule_*` convention) which the operator promotes manually in the Inspector. Automatic Pass-2 promotion (noop → `mcp_tool`) is a future ticket; the grammar cap of 11 branches stays intact. 5 new shared-package sanitiser tests + 3 new route tests (exposeToAi true persists+audits, reverse audits, omitted field stays silent) + 3 new prompt-composer tests (empty list = base unchanged, non-empty appends data-framed section, anti-pattern guidance present). AGENTS.md MCP client bullet rewritten: the "No LLM exposure in v1" carve-out is replaced with the new "Opt-in LLM exposure" paragraph; v2 carve-out collapses to just "HTTP transport". Audit-actions count bumped from 7 to 8. No web change (v1 API-only — Inspector dropdown for http appears in a future ticket). Closes ENG-057 entirely. |
| ENG-058 | Build Workflow Review Agent | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Add a second-pass AI reviewer that grades generated or edited workflows before save/run. The reviewer checks retries, timeouts, HTTP response caps, secret handling, missing approvals, unknown tools, malformed routers, missing outputs, ambiguous prompts, and PII/sensitive-action risk. AC: `/ai/review-workflow` returns structured findings with severity, affected node/edge, rationale, and suggested patch; Save/Run can surface blocking P0/P1 findings; tests cover safe workflow, missing timeout, secret risk, dangerous action without approval, and malformed router. **Summary:** New `POST /ai/review-workflow` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` mirrors the existing `/ai/generate-workflow` and `/ai/explain-workflow` posture (rate-limited via the shared `"ai"` bucket; wraps `llm.generateObject` in try/catch; audits both AI-mode and fallback paths as `ai.workflow.reviewed`). The structured-output schema `ReviewFindingsSchema` is a flat `{ status, issues: ReviewFindingSchema[] }` shape capped at 50 issues per response — concrete-typed throughout (no `z.unknown()`, no recursion) so it works on Anthropic + OpenAI strict mode. The new `REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` gives the model a severity rubric (info/warn/fail), a checklist tied to the AC (retries, bounds, hardcoded secrets, approval upstream of write-side, unknown tools, malformed routers, missing outputs, ambiguous prompts, PII risk), and per-finding format (code/severity/message/nodeId?/edgeId?/rationale/suggestion). New `packages/engine/src/workflow-review-fallback.ts` provides `buildReviewFallback(workflow)` that combines `validateWorkflow` structural issues with ENG-059's `checkWorkflowReadiness` and reshapes both into the review format with deterministic rationale strings, `mergeReviewFindings(primary, fallback)` that appends omitted deterministic blockers after AI sanitization, plus `sanitizeAiReview(review, workflow)` that drops findings whose `nodeId`/`edgeId` doesn't resolve (LLM hallucination guard) and re-rolls the status from the surviving issues — same defense-in-depth pattern as `sanitizeAiWorkflow` for the generation route. The fallback path fires when the LLM is unavailable (no key, quota, network) so the response shape stays consistent and `mode: "fallback"` flags the source per the AGENTS.md AI-fallback contract. AI Copilot panel (`apps/web/src/components/AiCopilotPanel.tsx`) gets a third "Review this flow" button alongside Generate and Explain; findings render inline using the same `we-readiness-issue--*` CSS tokens as the production-readiness badge so severity colors are consistent. 7 new engine tests cover the fallback (clean/missing-retry/raw-secret/malformed-router), deterministic merge, and the sanitizer (drops hallucinated nodeIds/edgeIds, re-rolls status). Save/Run is NOT gated on AI review — the AC's "surface blocking findings" is interpreted as UI-level surfacing; gating `/start` would add multi-second LLM latency to every run plus fallback-path risk. ENG-059's deterministic readiness gate already handles production-mode blocking. **Reviewer fixes (collateral):** (a) all three fallback paths in the route now write an audit row (`ai.workflow.reviewed` with `mode: "fallback"` + reason) — the original implementation skipped audit on the shape-invalid and no-LLM paths, which was consistent with the sibling routes' existing behavior but inconsistent with the documented "AI mutations write audit on success AND fallback" invariant; the route now records every AI surface call regardless of which fallback path fires; (b) `sanitizeAiReview`'s edge-id resolution no longer synthesizes `edge_${index}` placeholders for edges without an explicit `id` — the previous implementation would let an AI-hallucinated `edgeId: "edge_3"` survive sanitization because the index happened to land on a real but unnamed edge, surfacing a finding the operator couldn't locate in the canvas; only edges that carry a real `id` string contribute to the resolution set now; (c) the fallback and AI-success paths now append structural `validateWorkflow` blockers so a schema-valid but graph-invalid workflow (for example, a router candidate pointing at a missing node) cannot pass review just because the LLM omitted the finding. |
| ENG-059 | Add Production Readiness Check | Shipped | P1 | 2 | Add a deterministic readiness gate for workflows, separate from AI review. Checks include retries on external calls, timeout and response caps, declared outputs, approval/human gate for sensitive actions, credential references instead of raw secrets, eval coverage, and rollback availability. AC: `/workflows/readiness` returns pass/warn/fail with issue list; UI shows a "Production Ready" badge; workflows can still run in dev but production mode can block fail-level issues. **Summary:** New pure `checkWorkflowReadiness(workflow)` in `packages/engine/src/workflow-readiness.ts` runs 6 deterministic checks: `http_missing_bounds` (warn — http nodes without explicit `timeoutMs`/`maxResponseBytes`/`maxRedirects`), `external_node_missing_retry` (fail — http/tool/ai/agent without `config.retry.maxAttempts >= 2`), `workflow_missing_outputs` (warn), `sensitive_action_missing_approval` (warn — POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE http or write-side tool name without an `approval` ancestor in the DAG), `raw_secret_in_config` (fail — sensitive-keyed field values that aren't `{{secret.X}}` / `{{env.X}}` / `{{credential.X}}` templates), `workflow_missing_evals` (warn — placeholder until eval tracking lands). Reuses `SENSITIVE_KEY_PATTERN` from `safe-persist.ts` so the secret-key list stays in lockstep across the persistence chokepoint and the readiness gate. The status rolls up to `pass`/`warn`/`fail` (worst severity wins). New `POST /workflows/readiness` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` (next to `/validate`, same `role: "editor"` posture) returns the result, layering on a DB-aware `checkRollbackAvailability` helper that emits `workflow_missing_rollback_version` (warn) when `count(workflow_versions) < 2` for the saved id. Production-mode gate: when env `JANUSLY_PRODUCTION_MODE=true`, `POST /start` runs the readiness check before delegating to `startRun` and rejects fail-level workflows with HTTP 422 + the issue list. Dev mode (env unset) keeps the existing behavior unchanged. New `WorkflowReadinessBadge` component (`apps/web/src/components/WorkflowReadinessBadge.tsx`) renders in the App header next to the brand lockup — colored dot + label (`Production Ready` / `N warnings` / `N blockers`); click expands an inline list with `code`, message, suggestion. Refetches on the `platformVersion` tick (cross-panel reactivity hook). 13 new engine tests covering each check + the rollup logic. AGENTS.md note pins the new env flag so future maintainers know to extend the readiness check (not bypass it). Sister to (not overlapping with) ENG-058's future AI Workflow Review Agent — this is rules-only and never falls back. **Reviewer fixes (collateral):** (a) the `workflow_missing_evals` placeholder is now opt-in via env `JANUSLY_REQUIRE_EVAL_COVERAGE=true` so a clean workflow can actually reach `status: "pass"` and the badge can show a green "Production Ready" — without this, the always-on warn made the green state unreachable for any workflow today; (b) `WorkflowReadinessBadge` now selects `getWorkflowJson` from the Zustand store directly (`useWorkflowStore((s) => s.getWorkflowJson)`) instead of taking it as a prop — App.tsx's destructuring of the whole store produces a fresh local binding on every re-render (every node drag, every polling tick), which would have made the badge re-fetch on every tick. The selector returns the stable function reference. |
| ENG-060 | Build Replay Lab | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Create a run replay sandbox for failed and successful runs. Operators can replay with the same captured input, replay with a proposed patch, compare node outputs/cost/latency, and approve a new version only after replay passes. AC: UI exposes “Replay in Lab” from run details and DLQ; replay runs are tagged as sandbox/eval; production side effects are either disabled or require explicit tool-level replay policy; comparison report shows changed nodes, cost, latency, and pass/fail. **Summary:** Standalone surface assembled on top of ENG-077's sandbox infra. New `replayRunAsValidation` adapter in `packages/engine/src/adapters/replay-lab.ts` writes a fresh `runs.replayMode="validation"` row + seeds all nodes pending + enqueues root nodes in one transaction; mirrors `startRun`'s atomicity contract. Trigger input from the source run flows into the new run's `inputJson.input` so `{{input.*}}` references resolve to the same values the source saw. New `POST /runs/replay-lab` route (`editor`, `"ai"` rate-limit bucket) rejects nested labs (source `replayMode` already set), resolves the workflow snapshot from `workflow_versions.dagJson` → `runs.inputJson.workflow` (ad-hoc fallback) → caller-supplied `suggestedWorkflow` (validated through `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow`), and audits `replay_lab.started`. New `GET /runs/compare` route (`viewer`) delegates to `getRunComparison` in `packages/data/src/runComparisonRepo.ts`, which queries `runs` + `run_nodes` + `usage_events` (multi-tenant scoped) and aggregates per-node `{ base, replay }` bundles with status/latency/cost/tokens/output. The route registry's `GET /runs` prefix matcher excludes `/runs/compare` so first-match-wins routing works. Reuses `replayMode="validation"` (no new replay-mode value) so the existing dryRun gating, write-side skips, and `isNull(runs.replayMode)` rollup exclusions apply uniformly; audit action (`replay_lab.started` vs `recovery.validation_started`) distinguishes lab vs recovery intent. Web side: new `<ReplayLabDialog />` overlay modal with state machine `idle → starting → replaying → comparing → done | error` (polls `/run` at 1500ms, mirrors `RecoveryDialog`'s polling shape with `aliveRef` for unmount safety), new `<RunComparisonView />` per-node table with status pills + `Equal/Changed/Only base/Only replay` chips + latency/cost deltas (stable-stringify for key-order-tolerant output diff). Entry buttons in three surfaces: DLQ selected-entry action bar, RunsPanel active-run card (only for terminal non-validation runs), per-history-row compact Lab button. ESC + backdrop + close button are all blocked during `starting/replaying/comparing` so the operator can't accidentally toss an in-flight lab. The history-row layout switched from a single `<button>` to a `<div role="group">` with two interactive children; `.list-card-row:focus-visible` restores the focus ring lost to `all: unset`. New tests: 7 engine adapter cases pinning runs/runNodes/runEvents inserts + root-node discovery + trigger-input propagation; 7 RunComparisonView cases pinning equality classification + delta formatting + summary; 5 ReplayLabDialog cases pinning the state machine + error envelope. Web workspace tests +12 (131 → 143), engine +1 (471 → 472). |
| ENG-061 | Cluster failure patterns | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Group failures by normalized error signature, node type, tool, provider, and workflow. AC: Recovery Queue shows clusters such as “Missing secret: GITHUB_TOKEN”, “HTTP 401 on GitHub node”, and “OpenAI timeout on ai node”; each cluster shows frequency, affected workflows, first/last seen, impact, and suggested owner; clustering runs without leaking secret values. **Summary:** New pure normalizer `normalizeErrorSignature(error, context)` in `packages/engine/src/error-signature.ts` runs 7 first-match-wins rules in priority order: `secret_missing` (regex on message + explicit `code: "E_SECRET_MISSING"`), `http_error` (`error.statusCode` 4xx/5xx, then `HTTP 4xx` regex), `network_timeout` (`ECONNRESET`/`ETIMEDOUT`/`timeout`), `ai_provider` (`error.aiError` envelope + reason matchers — `quota exceeded` / `rate limit` / `context too long` / `model not found` — plus provider inference), `parse_error` (JSON / unexpected token), `tool_input` (invalid tool input + tool not found), `unknown` (fallback that scrubs + truncates to 80 chars). Defense-in-depth: every produced signature passes through `scrubSecretShapes` before return — closed regex set covers `sk-…` / `ghp_…` / `xox[baprs]-…` / `AKIA…` / `Bearer …` / 3-segment JWT `eyJ…` patterns and replaces matches with `[redacted]`. The persistence chokepoint already key-redacts the JSON we read from, but error MESSAGES are free-form strings; this scrub is the last line of defence. New pure aggregator `clusterFailureSamples(samples)` in `packages/engine/src/cluster-failures.ts` deduplicates by `(runId, nodeId)` preferring the DLQ-source sample (so a failed run that landed in DLQ counts once), groups by signature, aggregates affected-workflow counts via inner `Map<workflowId, ClusterWorkflow>`, tracks min/max `createdAt`, keeps up to 5 representative sample refs, sorts descending by frequency. New repo `collectFailureSamples(orgId, windowDays = 30, limit = 500)` in `packages/data/src/failureClusterRepo.ts` runs two parallel queries — one over `dead_letters` scoped by `dead_letters.org_id`, one over failed `run_nodes` scoped by `runs.org_id`; saved workflows left-join through `workflow_versions` for names and DAG metadata, while ad-hoc runs fall back to the persisted workflow snapshot. Pulls `nodeType` from the persisted DAG/`nodeJson` so the signatures get useful suffixes (`HTTP 401 on http node`). The DLQ writer now carries run `orgId` into `dead_letters` so non-default tenants show up in both DLQ lists and cluster rollups. New `GET /dlq/clusters?windowDays=…` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` (`role: "viewer"`) registered BEFORE the wildcard `/dlq` dispatcher (first-match-wins in the route registry — wildcard would otherwise swallow it). Web side: new `FailureClustersCard.tsx` mounted at the top of the Recovery panel inside `DeadLettersPanel.tsx`. Each cluster row shows the signature, category pill (`Secret`/`HTTP`/`Network`/`AI provider`/`Parse`/`Tool`/`Other`), frequency, affected-workflow count, last-seen relative time, and suggested-owner chip (`Ops`/`Workflow author`/`Platform`); click expands to show per-workflow occurrence counts + recent run ids. Severity bar on the left edge: `secret_missing`/`ai_provider` → red, everything else → amber. Reuses the cross-panel `platformVersion` selector for refetch and the existing `panel-card`/`field-label`/`mode-pill` design tokens. 24 new engine tests pin every category, the secret-shape scrubs (Bearer/sk-/ghp_/JWT), the non-empty unknown fallback, DLQ org propagation, the deduplication contract, and the descending-frequency sort. 3 web component tests cover loaded, empty, and error states for the cluster card. Engine workspace tests +24 (240 → 264). |
| ENG-062 | Add Workflow Health Score | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Score each workflow across reliability, safety, cost, latency, maintainability, and AI risk. Inputs include success rate, DLQ count, retries, approvals, secret hygiene, token/cost usage, p95 latency, missing outputs, and readiness/review findings. AC: workflow list and detail page show score + breakdown; score recalculates from recent runs and static checks; tests cover score changes after failed runs, missing timeouts, and added approval gates. **Summary:** New pure aggregator `computeWorkflowHealth({ workflow, readiness, signals })` in `packages/engine/src/workflow-health.ts` produces a 0–100 rollup with six per-category sub-scores: reliability (success rate − DLQ penalty − retry penalty, capped), safety (readiness fail/warn penalties), cost (band $0.01/run → 100, $1.00/run → 30), latency (band p95 5s → 100, 60s → 30), maintainability (declared outputs + multiple versions + approval node), AI risk (AI-node count + token volume + raw-secret findings). Weighted overall: 0.30 reliability + 0.25 safety + 0.15 latency + 0.10 cost + 0.10 maintainability + 0.10 aiRisk; status thresholds `≥80 healthy / ≥60 warn / <60 unhealthy`. Constants exported (`BREAKDOWN_WEIGHTS`, `NEUTRAL_DEFAULT`, `STATUS_THRESHOLDS`, per-category bands) so future tuning lives in one place. Neutral default 80 covers brand-new workflows with insufficient data (totalRuns < 5 → reliability/cost neutral; p95 null → latency neutral). New repo `collectHealthSignals(orgId, workflowId, windowDays = 30)` in `packages/data/src/workflowHealthRepo.ts` runs four batched queries across `runs`, `run_events` (retry count + p95 latency), `dead_letters` (open count), `usage_events` (cost + tokens), and `workflow_versions` (distinct version count). Multi-tenant scope via `eq(workflowVersions.orgId, orgId)` AND `eq(workflowVersions.workflowId, workflowId)` on the run query; subsequent queries gated on the `runIds` set. The `HealthSignals` type is duplicated structurally between `packages/data` and `packages/engine` to keep the dependency direction clean (engine → data, never the reverse). New `GET /workflows/health?workflowId=…` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` (`role: "viewer"` since health is post-save reporting, distinct from `/workflows/readiness` which is `editor`) confirms workflow ownership, loads the latest `workflow_versions` row, runs `checkWorkflowReadiness` on the parsed DSL, then composes `collectHealthSignals` + `computeWorkflowHealth` into the typed response. New `WorkflowHealthBadge` component (`apps/web/src/components/WorkflowHealthBadge.tsx`) renders a colored dot + "Health: 87 / 100"; click expands the six per-category sub-scores with rationale text. Refetches on the `platformVersion` tick (cross-panel reactivity). Mounted in two surfaces: App header next to `WorkflowReadinessBadge` (live workflow's score), and per-row in `WorkflowsDashboard` (compact mode without label). Skipped (`null`) when `workflowId` is undefined (ad-hoc unsaved workflows). Reuses `we-readiness-badge--*` and `we-readiness-issue--*` CSS tokens — same visual language as the readiness badge. 17 new engine tests covering each category band, the rollup math, and the AC's three assertions ("score changes after failed runs / missing timeouts / added approval gates"). Engine workspace lands at 240 (was 221). |
| ENG-063 | Export Run Explain Report | Shipped | P2 | 3 | Let operators export a failure/recovery report to Markdown/JSON and optionally send it to Slack/Linear/GitHub. Report includes timeline, root cause, failed node, attempts, redacted inputs/outputs, suggested fix, owner, and next action. AC: export never includes raw secrets; report is accessible from run detail and Recovery Queue; fixture test snapshots validate shape. **Summary:** Shipped Markdown + JSON download via a new `GET /reports/run-explain?runId=…&format=markdown\|json` route (`role: "viewer"`, multi-tenant scoped via `eq(runs.orgId, auth.orgId)`). New pure builder `buildRunExplainReport` in `packages/engine/src/run-explain-report.ts` consumes `{ run, runNodes, runEvents, recoveryAudit? }` snapshots and produces both formats from the same structured envelope: summary (id / status / workflow version / replayMode), root cause (via the shared `normalizeErrorSignature` taxonomy — category + `suggestedOwner` of `ops`/`workflow_author`/`platform`), failed node (id / status / attempts / redacted error summary), timeline (capped at 50 most-recent events with a truncation notice), Suggested fix (reads from the most recent `ai.workflow.patch_suggested` audit row — mode / topApproachLabel / envelopeKind / patchStyle / suggestionsCount), and next action (operator-actionable string keyed off the suggested owner). The route writes `report.run_explain.exported` audit rows tagged `{ format, recoveryAuditFound }` so there's a trail of who exported what. Defense-in-depth: every rendered free-form string passes through `scrubSecretShapes` even though source rows are already redacted at write time by `safePersistPayload` — Bearer tokens, `sk-…`/`ghp_…`/`xox[baprs]-…` shapes, and JWT segments are scrubbed. Web side: new `downloadFromApi(path, filename?)` helper in `apps/web/src/api.ts` mirrors `api()`'s auth resolution (Supabase JWT OR dev headers) but reads `res.blob()` + clicks a synthetic anchor so the browser downloads as a file rather than rendering text inline. Two Export entry points: the DLQ panel's selected-entry action bar next to "Suggest fix" / "Retry" / "Resolve" / "Replay in Lab", and per-row on RunsPanel history (next to the existing Lab button). Both buttons use the standard `small-command` token and a `lucide-react` `Download` icon. **v1 scope explicitly narrow:** download only — external delivery (Slack / Linear / GitHub) is documented as a clear future follow-up (would add a `POST /reports/run-explain/deliver` route + reuse the existing `slack.post` / `webhook.send` / `github.create_issue` integration tools). New tests: 10 builder cases (clean succeeded run skips root-cause / failed-node sections; failed run renders both with the correct suggested owner; multi-failure run picks the latest failed node; recovery-audit row renders the Suggested fix block; legacy audit without patchStyle surfaces "unknown"; secret-shape values in error messages are scrubbed to `[redacted]`; timeline truncation kicks in at the cap with a Markdown notice; empty timeline shows "No events recorded yet"; validation runs flag `replayMode` in the summary), and 6 route cases (Markdown happy path + Content-Disposition header + audit row, JSON variant, recovery audit auto-discovery, missing runId 400, unknown format 400, cross-org / missing run 404 with no enumeration leak). No AGENTS.md change — the report is read-only and reuses existing redaction. |
| ENG-064 | Add recovery metrics dashboard | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Add business-value metrics for selling and operating Janusly: workflow success rate, mean time to recovery, failed runs by pattern, cost by model/provider, p95 latency, approvals pending, auto-fixes suggested/applied, rollbacks triggered, and replay pass/fail rate. AC: dashboard reads bounded/preaggregated queries; web shows a Recovery/Operations overview; metrics can be used in demos without manual DB queries. **Summary:** New repo `collectRecoveryMetricsSignals(orgId, windowDays = 30)` in `packages/data/src/recoveryMetricsRepo.ts` runs six parallel windowed queries — run status counts, MTTR samples (replayed-DLQ `replayedAt - createdAt`), human approvals pending (`run_nodes.status='waiting'` with the approval waiting reason), cost by provider/model (grouped in JS), p95 latency (nearest-rank), replay outcomes from rows with `replayedAt` stamped — with multi-tenant scope on every query. New pure rollup `composeRecoveryMetrics` in `packages/engine/src/recovery-metrics.ts` produces six `RecoveryMetric` cards with severity bands exported as constants: success rate ≥95% healthy / ≥85% warn; MTTR ≤2m healthy / ≤15m warn; p95 latency ≤5s healthy / ≤30s warn; approvals 0 healthy / ≤5 neutral / >5 warn; replay rate ≥90% healthy / ≥70% warn; cost is `neutral`. New `GET /recovery/metrics?windowDays=N` route (`role: "viewer"`). Web side: new `'operations'` tab added to `ActiveTab`; `BuilderSidebar.tsx` gets a new "Operations" nav button with a `Gauge` lucide icon; `RightPanel.tsx` routes the tab to `OperationsPanel.tsx` which renders six metric cards in a responsive grid with severity-tinted left borders, a thin progress bar on the success-rate card, a per-provider cost breakdown table below the grid, and the existing `<FailureClustersCard />` embedded so operators get clusters in the same tab. Refetches on `platformVersion` tick. 14 new engine tests pin every severity band, MTTR formatting, cost rollup with provider canonicalisation, duration formatter, and null handling; 3 web tests cover the Operations panel loading/success/error states. **Out of scope:** "auto-fixes suggested/applied" + "rollbacks triggered" counters from the AC — both need new audit-log actions that don't exist yet; surface stays absent in v1, will plug in when the Recovery MVP ships the apply path. **Collateral fix from manual smoke:** `failureClusterRepo.queryFailedRunNodes` was passing the `since` Date inside a `sql` template literal — postgres-js renders Dates inside `sql` parameters via `String()` which yields the JS `Date.toString()` form ("Sat Apr 04 2026 …") instead of an ISO timestamp; postgres rejected the bind and the Operations panel + Runs tab cluster card both surfaced "Failed query: …" errors. Fixed by passing `since.toISOString()` with an explicit `::timestamptz` cast inside the same `sql` template. The `eq(workflowVersions.orgId, …)` predicate was also dropped to `eq(runs.orgId, …)` so org scope flows through `runs.orgId` (canonical) rather than the optional `workflow_versions` join (ad-hoc workflows have no version row). |

Expands ENG-039. **Summary:** New pure aggregator `computeWorkflowDiff(before, after)` in `packages/shared/src/workflow-diff.ts` produces `WorkflowDiff` with workflow-level field changes, node-level changes (added/removed/changed), and edge-level changes (added/removed/condition-changed). Lives in `@janusly/shared` (zero runtime deps) so the web bundle imports it via `@janusly/shared/src/workflow-diff` subpath without pulling the engine graph. Tag classifier marks AC-required signals: `secret_ref` (path's last segment matches `SENSITIVE_KEY_PATTERN` OR value uses `{{secret.X}}` / `{{credential.X}}` / `{{env.X}}` template), `retry` (`config.retry` / `config.retry.*`), `timeout` (`config.timeoutMs`), `bounds` (`config.maxResponseBytes` / `config.maxRedirects`), `approval` (added/removed `approval` node), `rationale` (workflow-level cosmetic — `name`, `metadata.*`). Deterministic sort: nodes by id within each kind bucket, edges by key, fields by path. Reruns produce deep-equal output so the UI never flickers. Also moved `SENSITIVE_KEY_PATTERN` from `packages/engine/src/safe-persist.ts` → `packages/shared/src/sensitive-keys.ts` so the diff classifier and the persistence chokepoint share a single source of truth — adding a new key shape there improves redaction at write time AND secret-ref tagging at diff time without one layer depending on the other. New `WorkflowDiffView.tsx` (~280 lines) renders the diff in three sections (workflow / nodes / edges) with severity-tinted rows: green (added), red (removed), amber (changed). Per-field tag pills (`Retry` amber, `Secret-ref` red with `ShieldAlert` lucide icon, `Approval` red, `Timeout`/`Bounds` amber, `Metadata` info-blue). Optional `aiPatchRationale` prop renders below the diff sections inside an info-toned helper-text block — Recovery MVP hookup point. `VersionHistoryPanel.tsx` got a "Compare" toggle: when off, click-a-version-to-hydrate works as before; when on, each row gets a checkbox (max 2 selectable; oldest selection drops on third click) and the inline `<WorkflowDiffView>` renders below the list with the older version always on the left regardless of click order. 17 new shared-workspace tests cover empty/identity, add/remove/change for nodes + edges, every tag-classifier branch (including the path-vs-template secret_ref distinction), workflow-level field changes, determinism. 6 new web jsdom tests cover the renderer's summary line, empty state, secret redaction, AI-rationale rendering, Compare-mode selection, and clearing stale compare state when the active workflow changes. No new API route — the existing `GET /workflows/versions` already returns `dagJson` per version, the diff is computed in the browser. |
| ENG-073 | Add brand narrative docs | Shipped | P2 | — | Create a concise brand narrative that makes Janusly feel unique and memorable: category, tagline, anti-positioning, product principles, demo story, and one-sentence pitch. AC: docs/marketing/narrative.md includes “not another automation builder,” “observable, recoverable, reviewable,” and “trust AI workflows in production”; README/PLAN can reference it. **Shipped:** new `docs/marketing/narrative.md` — seven scan-friendly sections (category, primary tagline + 4 alts by context, one-sentence pitch, anti-positioning, product principles, "3am moment" demo story, brand-voice notes for downstream contributors). Lands the three AC-required phrases verbatim. Cross-linked from README (after the positioning block) and PLAN §16.0 (closing tail line). Brand-voice notes encode discipline rules ("concrete over abstract", "honest about today vs destination", "never 'AI fixes everything'", "MTTR is the metric of record") so ENG-066/067/068/095 follow-ups stay in voice. |
| ENG-074 | Stream HTTP response bodies | Shipped | P2 | 3 | Today `fetchHttpTarget` returns `HttpResult.body: string` — the body cap (default 1 MB, configurable via `init.maxResponseBytes`) prevents OOM but limits use cases that legitimately need large fetches (multi-MB CSV, PDF, image processing). Add an opt-in streaming mode so consumers can iterate the body chunk-by-chunk without buffering the whole payload. AC: `fetchHttpTarget(url, { bodyMode: "stream" })` returns `HttpResult & { body: ReadableStream<Uint8Array> }`; the existing byte-counter still aborts the stream when the cap is exceeded; `transform`, `condition` evaluator, and persistence path (`run_nodes.state_json.output.body`) gain explicit handling for stream-typed bodies (truncate to a configurable preview for audit, full stream available downstream); recipes for "fetch a 5 MB CSV and process row-by-row" added to docs. Tracked as a follow-up to ENG-048 — the current bound-execution chokepoint is the right place for the new mode to plug in. **Summary:** `fetchHttpTarget` gains TypeScript overloads on a new `bodyMode?: "buffer" \| "stream"` option (default `"buffer"`, zero breaking change). Streaming returns `{ body: ReadableStream<Uint8Array>, ... }` backed by a TransformStream-style reader that shares the existing `AbortController` — `maxResponseBytes` aborts mid-flight with the same descriptive error string the buffered path emits, and the `Content-Length` pre-check rejects oversized declared bodies before opening the stream. The pinned `undici.Agent` + DNS-rebinding guard are untouched. New `consumeStreamToPreview(stream, previewBytes)` helper drains the stream into a UTF-8 preview + total byte count, propagating the cap-exceeded error if it fires during preview read. The `http` node executor and `http.request` tool both gain `bodyMode` + `streamPreviewBytes` knobs and auto-consume to a JSON-safe `{ body: <preview>, streamed: true, streamedBytes, streamTruncated }` envelope before returning so persistence stays clean. Defense-in-depth: `safePersistPayload` substitutes any leaked `ReadableStream` with `{ __stream: true, note }`; `template.getByPath` throws the new `StreamValueInTemplateError` (carrying the offending dotted path) when a `{{...}}` reference resolves to a stream; `expression.getBySafePath` throws an analogous error so `condition` nodes can't silently coerce a stream to a boolean. New `http.streamPreviewBytes` org-config key (range 1024..1_048_576, default 65536, env fallback `JANUSLY_HTTP_STREAM_PREVIEW_BYTES`) controls per-tenant preview size. New `docs/nodes.md` "Streaming HTTP responses (5 MB CSV recipe)" section. v1 carve-out: streams must be consumed within the same executor invocation; cross-node live streaming stays out of scope. **Status note (2026-05-18d.1):** Remaining work closed by ENG-105 — `csv.fetch` tool ships the row-by-row processor that consumes the streaming body through the shared RFC 4180 parser, bounded sample + counts persisted instead of the full payload. ENG-074 is now Shipped end-to-end. |
| ENG-105 | Add streaming CSV processor | Shipped | P2 | 2 | Follow-up to ENG-074's streaming primitive. Add a streaming-aware processor for large CSV payloads that fetches and parses row-by-row inside one executor/tool invocation, without persisting a live `ReadableStream` across run checkpoints. AC: operator can process a multi-MB CSV row-by-row without buffering the full body; SSRF, DNS pinning, timeout, redirect, and byte-cap guards still flow through `fetchHttpTarget`; persisted output is a bounded summary/sample rather than the full stream; tests cover malformed CSV, maxResponseBytes abort, preview/sample truncation, and no live stream leaking into templates or persistence; docs replace the ENG-074 v1 limitation with an end-to-end recipe. Depends on ENG-074. **Status note (2026-05-18d):** Shipped. New `csv.fetch` tool in `packages/engine/src/tool-registry.ts` calls `fetchHttpTarget(..., { bodyMode: "stream" })` and pipes the body through a new `streamCsvSummary` helper (`packages/engine/src/csv-stream.ts`) that consumes chunks via the shared RFC 4180 tokenizer. The hand-rolled parser in `csv.ts` was refactored to expose its character-by-character state machine as three primitives (`createCsvParseState`, `feedCsvChunk`, `finalizeCsvParse`); the existing `parseCsv` is now a thin wrapper that uses them — every pre-existing csv test stays green (behavior-neutral refactor pinned by 7-case csv.ts test suite). Input schema: `{ url, headers?, sampleRows? (1..500 default 50), filter?, maxBytes? (1KB..50MB default 10MB), timeoutMs?, maxRedirects? }`. Output schema: `{ ok, statusCode, totalRows, matchedRows, sampleRows, headers, streamedBytes, streamTruncated, malformedRows, error? }`. `writeSide: false` (pure read; dry-run runs exercise it identically). Memory bound: `O(sampleRows × avgRowSize) + O(longestLineSize)` — a 50MB CSV with 1M rows persists as ~50KB of `state_json` (sample × avgRowSize), not 50MB. Stream-leak chokepoints (`safe-persist.ts` sentinel, `template.ts` `StreamValueInTemplateError`) untouched — the tool consumes the stream BEFORE returning so the envelope is plain JSON; the chokepoints remain defense-in-depth for anti-pattern tool authors. Mid-stream byte-cap aborts surface as `{ ok: false, streamTruncated: true, ... }` with the partial sample so the operator sees what got through. Filter mode reuses the same exact-match `Record<string, string>` WHERE shape `csv.filter` already accepts, so operators get consistent behavior across buffered + streaming surfaces. 7 new csv-stream tests (happy path, chunk-boundary reconstruction, filter, malformed rows, trailing partial line, JSON-safe envelope, mid-stream abort) + 3 new tool-registry tests (happy path, byte-cap mid-stream abort, SSRF pre-stream rejection). AGENTS.md HTTP/SSRF bullet extended with a `csv.fetch` cross-reference sentence. No new dep (RFC 4180 parser was already hand-rolled in `csv.ts`), no new audit action, no migration, no web change. Closes ENG-074's Remaining line — that row flips to Shipped concurrently. |
| ENG-075 | Fix AI workflow generation schema for OpenAI strict mode and Anthropic | Shipped | P0 | — | `/ai/generate-workflow` was broken on every provider in five stacked layers, all of which had to be peeled before AI Studio actually worked. (1) OpenAI strict mode rejected `propertyNames` emitted by every `z.record(z.string(), X)`. (2) Anthropic via the AI SDK rejected the circular `$ref` from `WorkflowInputSchema`'s `z.lazy(() => ...)` self-reference. (3) Once schema rejection was bypassed, the model itself returned semantically broken workflows (router with empty `candidates`, http with no `url`) because `NodeSchema.config` is `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())` and gave zero per-node-type guidance. (4) Anthropic structured output rejected `z.unknown()` / `z.any()` fields with "Empty schema that accepts any JSON value is not supported"; every free-form field had to be typed concretely or dropped. (5) Anthropic's grammar compiler caps schema size — empirically the limit is identical across Haiku/Sonnet/Opus 4.5+ (all three accept up to 11 discriminated-union branches with the full envelope; 12+ rejects with "compiled grammar is too large"), so the AI side covers a deliberate 11-type subset rather than the then-current engine's full 16; ENG-033 later kept the cap but changed the selected 11 after the engine grew to 20. Bonus: a stale `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://api.anthropic.com` shell var (set by Claude Desktop) silently routed to `/messages` instead of `/v1/messages` and 404ed every call — the SDK respects the env override as-is. Pre-existing since ENG-028 introduced the recursive `inputs` schema. AC: `/ai/generate-workflow` produces a real AI-authored workflow for representative prompts; the fix preserves `WorkflowSchema` as the engine's strict parse target. **Summary:** Replaced the omit-based slim schema with a typed `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` in `apps/api/src/index.ts` — a `z.discriminatedUnion("type", [...])` over 11 node types (`noop`, `http`, `transform`, `condition`, `ai`, `tool`, `agent`, `router`, `approval`, `multi_agent`, `loop`), each with its required config fields explicit and free-form `z.unknown()` either typed (`z.record(z.string(), z.string())` for mappings) or omitted (headers, body, tool input — operator fills in Inspector). At the time, the remaining 5 types (`wait_until`, `webhook`, `agent_reflection`, `router_llm`, `subworkflow`) stayed operator-only — AI emitted a `noop` placeholder named after the requested step and the operator promoted it. **Updated by ENG-033:** the current 11-type set is `noop`, `http`, `transform`, `condition`, `ai`, `tool`, `agent`, `router`, `approval`, `human_form`, `loop`; `multi_agent` is now operator-only/noop-promotion. The system prompt gained a SUPPORTED-TYPES rule, a PLACEHOLDER rule, a ROUTER rule, and a worked-example JSON of a smart-router workflow. Defensive `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` handling in `packages/ai/src/llm-client.ts` auto-appends `/v1` when the env var points at the canonical host without it — fixes Claude Desktop's stale shell injection without disturbing intentional proxy overrides. `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` default updated to `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` (the dated suffix; the bare `claude-haiku-4-5` doesn't resolve). The post-generation `sanitizeAiWorkflow` step still re-validates against the strict engine `WorkflowSchema`, so the engine's parse contract is unchanged. Cross-provider unblock without weakening any per-provider strict mode. End-to-end smoke verified 5 representative prompts (smart router, approval gate, multi-agent crew, loop batch, wait-until placeholder) all return `mode: "ai"` with sensibly-shaped workflows. |
| ENG-076 | Expand AI workflow generation to all node types | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Today `/ai/generate-workflow` is capped at 11 of the engine's 20 node types because Anthropic's structured-output grammar compiler rejects schemas with 12+ discriminated-union branches under the full envelope ("The compiled grammar is too large"). The 9 operator-only types (`multi_agent`, `wait_until`, `webhook`, `agent_reflection`, `router_llm`, `subworkflow`, `parallel_fork`, `join`, `schedule`) currently land as `noop` placeholders that the operator promotes in the Inspector. ENG-033 intentionally reorganized the 11 direct-generation slots to include `human_form` and demote `multi_agent`: structured human input is common enough and schema-light enough to generate directly, while multi-agent crews have a large nested shape and usually need operator intent. Empirically the Anthropic limit is identical across Haiku/Sonnet/Opus 4.5+ (it's an API-level grammar cap, not per-model), so just upgrading the default model doesn't help. Investigate paths to recover the missing operator-only types where direct generation is actually worth the complexity: (a) wait for Anthropic to raise the grammar cap and re-test; (b) two-pass generation — first pass scaffolds with the 11-type union, second pass promotes specific noop placeholders to a tighter single-type schema; (c) intent-specific generation surfaces such as `/ai/generate-multi-agent` or `/ai/generate-scheduled-flow`; (d) provider-specific expansion only after OpenAI or another provider is re-opened with a current verification plan. AC: prompts that explicitly mention wait/schedule/subworkflow/fork-join or multi-agent intent get a high-quality promoted workflow path without degrading Anthropic `mode: "ai"` reliability; the engine's strict `WorkflowSchema` remains the runtime source of truth; tests cover at least one recovered type per strategy. Depends on ENG-075. **Summary:** Shipped strategy (b) — two-pass generation — with `wait_until` as the first recovered type. New `promoteNoopPlaceholders` helper in `packages/ai/src/promote-noop.ts` scans Pass-1's slim-schema output for noop nodes whose id starts with `wait_`, `sleep_`, `pause_`, or `delay_` (case-insensitive), then calls Pass-2 against `AiWaitUntilConfigSchema` (single-shape, provider-strict-safe — no `oneOf`, no `propertyNames`, no open `additionalProperties`) with the operator's verbatim prompt as the hint. The LLM extracts the ISO 8601 duration relevant to the detected noop's id and the helper flips the noop's `type` + `config` in place. **Detection is id-prefix, not LLM-emitted metadata:** an initial design asked the model to set `config.promote: "wait_until"` + `config.hint` on placeholders; live-LLM smoke against Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5 showed the model consistently ignored the instruction and emitted empty configs (the path of least resistance). Operators consistently see descriptive ids for time-pause placeholders, the existing prompt already encourages snake_case descriptive ids, so the id-prefix heuristic is reliable in practice. Per-noop failures (LLM throws, schema rejects, whitespace-only duration, empty operator prompt) degrade silently — the offending noop stays unpromoted and the rest of the workflow is preserved, so the response stays `mode: "ai"` with `promotionsSucceeded < promotionAttempts` surfaced in the audit row. `GENERATE_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` gains a "WAIT-INTENT NAMING" paragraph and a worked wait-intent example pinning the id convention. Audit metadata on `ai.workflow.generated` extends with `promotionAttempts` + `promotionsSucceeded`; existing readers unaffected. Rate-limit posture documented in the helper's file header: the route's single `enforceRateLimit("ai", ...)` gate fires at entry only; Pass-2 calls bill against the same Redis bucket window but aren't individually capped. AI-fallback contract preserved: Pass-1 failure → existing fallback envelope; Pass-2 catastrophic throw → existing fallback; Pass-2 per-noop failure → `mode: "ai"` with partial-degrade signal. **Strategy-coverage scope:** v1 ships strategy (b) only; the other 3 strategies (a/c/d) stay unimplemented and `wait_until` is the only recovered type. New tests: 11 helper cases (happy path on `wait_3_days`, every documented prefix family recognised case-insensitively with non-alphanumeric separators, edges/workflow-level preservation, no-op passthrough on workflows without wait-intent noops, non-noop with wait-prefixed id ignored, ids that merely contain `wait` mid-string correctly skipped, per-noop LLM-throws isolation, empty operator prompt short-circuit, whitespace-only duration rejection, context + Pass-2 prompt plumbing); 1 prompt-pin case asserting the new "WAIT-INTENT NAMING" paragraph + worked example are in the system prompt body. AGENTS.md AI integration entry extended with the two-pass + partial-degrade contract. **Live-LLM smoke verified:** Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5 against `pnpm dev`: prompt "wait 3 days then call https://example.com/webhook" lands a `wait_until` node with `config.duration: "P3D"` and audit row `promotionAttempts: 1, promotionsSucceeded: 1`. |
| ENG-077 | Sandbox replay before commit (Recovery v2) | Shipped | P1 | 3 | The Recovery MVP (ENG-053) saves the AI-suggested workflow version BEFORE replaying — if the replay re-fails, the regression is persisted and the operator must rollback manually. Tighten the loop: after the operator approves the patch, the recovery dialog runs the replay in a **sandboxed** mode (the `replayDeadLetter` adapter accepts a new `dryRun: true` flag) that re-executes the failed entry against the suggested workflow WITHOUT writing the version row, gates on terminal status, and only commits the save when the sandbox replay reaches `succeeded`. Sandbox runs are tagged `runs.replayMode: "validation"` (new column) so they're excluded from the cluster + health rollups. Write-side tools (`http POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE`, write-side tool registry entries) are gated in sandbox mode — they emit a `dry-run skipped` event instead of executing. AC: Recovery dialog gains a "Validating fix…" step between Review and Apply; the operator only sees "Apply" enabled after sandbox passes; failed sandbox replays surface the new run's errorJson inline so the operator can iterate. **Subsumes parts of ENG-060 (Replay Lab)** — ENG-060 becomes the standalone surface; ENG-077 is the tight Recovery integration. **Summary:** Schema gains `runs.replay_mode` (text, nullable) + `(orgId, replayMode)` index. New engine helper `getRunReplayMode` plus `NodeContext.dryRun` flag plumbed by `executeNode` so node executors can gate writes per run. HTTP node skips POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE in dryRun and emits `node.dry_run.skipped`; safe methods (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS) still execute. `ToolDefinition` gains optional `writeSide` flag (mark `http.request`); tool node executor checks `isToolWriteSide(name)` + refines on `input.method` for http.request, emitting `tool.dry_run.skipped` when gated. New `DLQReplayAdapter.replayDeadLetterAsValidation` writes a fresh run (synthetic `workflowVersionId`, `replayMode: "validation"`, suggested workflow snapshot in `inputJson`, `parentRunId` linkage) in one transaction, then enqueues only the failing node. New `POST /dlq/validate-fix` (`role: "editor"`, "ai" rate-limit bucket) validates the proposed workflow through the same `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow` chain as `/ai/patch-workflow`, calls the adapter, audits `recovery.validation_started`, returns `{ runId }`. `RecoveryDialog` state machine extended with `validating` (polls `GET /run` at 1.5s until terminal) and `validation-failed` (renders the failed node's `errorJson` plus an "Iterate" button) states; the polling effect claims the terminal-status path before yielding so concurrent in-flight polls don't double-fire save+replay. Apply button labeled "Apply & validate"; production save+replay only runs after sandbox `succeeded`. `workflowHealthRepo` and `failureClusterRepo` queries add `isNull(runs.replayMode)` filters so validation runs don't pollute production signals. New tests: dryRun gating on http + tool executors, validation-adapter shape pin, RecoveryDialog success + failure flows, `isToolWriteSide` helper. |
| ENG-078 | Cluster-level Recover All (Recovery v2) | Shipped | P1 | 2 | When 50 DLQ rows fail with the same signature ("Missing secret: GITHUB_TOKEN") the operator currently has to click Suggest fix 50 times. The dialog still runs the ENG-077 sandbox validation once on the representative before saving; after save it calls cluster apply instead of single replay, surfaces "replayed N of M" plus per-row errors, and warns when the batch is capped. Reviewer fixes keep closed DLQ rows out of the bulk list and fall back to a still-open member when the original representative is stale. |
| ENG-079 | One-click workflow rollback | Shipped | P2 | 1 | Every saved workflow has a complete version history in `workflow_versions` but the only path to "go back to v3" is to manually re-save the older `dagJson`. Add a "Rollback to this version" button per row in `VersionHistoryPanel` (visible to editors) that opens a confirmation dialog rendering the existing `WorkflowDiffView` (current → target) and, on confirm, saves the target row's `dagJson` as a new version (preserves history forwards) plus an audit row tagged `workflow.rolled_back` with the source version id.
