Returns 404 with the same message regardless of cross-org / wrong-workflow / missing — no enumeration leak. Audits `workflow.rolled_back` with `{ sourceVersionId, sourceVersion, newVersion }`. New `RollbackConfirmDialog` reuses `WorkflowDiffView` (current → target labels), runs an `idle → rolling-back → done | error` machine; on success hydrates the canvas to the rolled-back DAG, calls `bumpPlatformVersion()` so sibling panels refetch, and toasts. Per-row Rollback button (`RotateCcw` icon) in `VersionHistoryPanel` visible only when versions > 1, hidden on the latest row, hidden in compare-mode. Snapshot of `(currentId, targetId)` taken at click time so a sibling save bumping `platformVersion` mid-dialog can't shift the diff under the operator. Cancellation guard via `aliveRef` so a rollback that resolves after dismount can't leak hydrate/bump/toast. |
| ENG-080 | Multi-suggestion recovery with confidence | Shipped | P2 | 2 | The Recovery MVP returns ONE suggestion — operator can't see alternatives. Extend `/ai/patch-workflow` to return `Array<{ workflow, rationale, approachLabel: "add_retry" \| "raise_timeout" \| "swap_secret_ref" \| "add_approval" \| "fix_url" \| "other", confidence: number }>` with up to 3 suggestions. The system prompt asks the LLM to self-rate confidence 0–100 (calibrated by an engine-side table — past acceptance rate per `approachLabel` once ENG-081 lands). Recovery dialog renders suggestions as tabs; the diff updates per tab. AC: response shape backward-compatible (callers reading `[0].workflow` keep working); confidence numbers visible per tab; default tab is the highest-confidence approach. Depends on ENG-081 for calibrated confidence; ships pre-calibration with raw self-ratings. **Summary:** Per-failing-node-type envelopes in `ai-schemas.ts` now wrap a `suggestions: Array<{ patchedConfig, rationale, approachLabel, confidence }>(min 1, max 3)` instead of a single envelope; `patchEnvelopeForNodeType` dispatch unchanged. Helper in `packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts` returns `{ mode, suggestions: PatchSuggestionItem[], model?, provider?, aiError? }` with the LLM-emitted array verbatim; `SYSTEM_PROMPT` instructs the model to emit 1-3 distinct alternatives ordered most-likely-fix first with self-rated confidence + `approachLabel`. Route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` fan-out-merges each suggestion's `patchedConfig` onto the failing node, validates each through `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow`, drops invalid items, sorts survivors by confidence desc, and degrades to fallback when none survive. Response carries the new `suggestions: Array<{ workflow, rationale, approachLabel, confidence }>` plus legacy `suggestedWorkflow`/`rationale` fields mirroring `suggestions[0]` for back-compat. Audit metadata extended with `suggestionsCount` + `topApproachLabel`. `RecoveryDialog` renders a tab strip when `suggestions.length > 1` (selected drives the diff + rationale + Apply); single-suggestion responses keep the old single-pane UI. Tab strip uses ARIA `role="tablist"` + `role="tab"` + `aria-controls` linkage to the tabpanel wrapping the diff view, with arrow/Home/End keyboard navigation. New test suite: 22 envelope cases (`ai-patch-schema.test.ts`), 32 parametrised matrix cases over a 10-fixture catalog (`ai-patch-multi-suggestion.matrix.test.ts`), 6 helper-boundary cases (`patch-workflow.test.ts` rewritten), 7 dialog test cases including back-compat shim, keyboard tab navigation, and tab-reset on Iterate. New `apps/api/src/recovery-matrix-fixtures.ts` is the single source of truth for failure shapes (HTTP 404 typo / 500 transient / 401 / body cap / redirect / timeout / tool input / missing secret / AI provider transient / write-side without approval); `pnpm seed:recovery-matrix` clears and seeds the 10 fixtures into `dead_letters` for manual smoke. Smoke verified on Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5: 5/10 fixtures returned `mode: "ai"` with 1-3 suggestions (`http_500_transient`: 2 add_retry suggestions; `http_timeout`: 3 with raise_timeout 85% / raise_timeout 55% / add_retry 45%; visual tab-swap verified on `http_500_transient` in real Chrome). The 5 fallback fixtures all hit fundamental schema gaps the per-type envelope can't express (HTTP `headers` missing for 401/missing-secret; tool `input` missing for tool_input_invalid; structural patches needed for write-side approval) — already tracked as ENG-088 (provider-strict envelopes for non-resilience nodes) and ENG-083 (structural multi-node patches). |

New repo `packages/data/src/recoveryFeedbackRepo.ts` exposes `recordRecoveryFeedback` (write) + `summarizePastFeedback` (read, capped at 100 rows / 30-day window, groups by approachLabel + tracks 3 latest rejection comments). Free-text `comment` runs through the relocated `scrubSecretShapes` helper from `@janusly/shared/src/error-signature` at write time and is truncated to 2000 chars. New `POST /recovery/feedback` route (`role: "editor"`, `"ai"` rate-limit bucket, audited as `recovery.feedback`) validates body via Zod (closed `approachLabel` + `suggestionMode` enums), org-scopes via `getDeadLetter`, rejects anonymous workflows with 422. New `apps/api/src/ai-patch-feedback.ts` exposes `composeFeedbackHint` (pure, ≤280 chars, scrubs rejection comments as defense-in-depth) which `/ai/patch-workflow` calls AFTER `summarizePastFeedback` and threads through `extraContext.pastFeedbackSummary`. Patch helper's `SYSTEM_PROMPT` gains one line teaching the LLM to treat the field as soft prior — prefer high-acceptance approaches, deprioritize already-rejected ones, signal residual uncertainty via lower confidence + a rationale that addresses prior pushback. Recovery dialog gains a `cancelling` step + `<CancellingBody>` component with 5 quick-pick reason chips ("Wrong approach" / "Doesn't fix root cause" / "Risky" / "Too narrow" / "Other") + free-text textarea + "Skip & close" + "Back". Cancels from review and validation-failed both route through this step. Apply success writes `accepted: true`; Iterate writes `accepted: false, comment: "validation_failed"` BEFORE re-entering generateSuggestion. New `apps/api/src/ai-patch-feedback.test.ts` (12 cases). `RecoveryDialog.test.tsx` extended with 2 new cases (cancel-from-review opens cancelling step + chip click writes feedback; Iterate writes feedback before regenerate); existing happy path also asserts the post-Apply `/recovery/feedback` write. `packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.test.ts` extended to assert `pastFeedbackSummary` flows verbatim into the LLM prompt body. Doc-sync: `docs/ai.md` gains a new "Recovery learning loop" section with the closed-loop mental model + ASCII diagram (per "preserve the why in code" promise from the plan). |
| ENG-082 | Recovery before/after metric delta | Shipped | P2 | 2 | After a successful Apply & replay the recovery dialog's success ribbon currently shows just "replay run started". Surface the delta: 24h-window comparison of `health: 81 → 85`, `p95: 4.2s → 3.1s`, `cost/run: $0.012 → $0.008`. Reuses `computeWorkflowHealth` (ENG-062) + `composeRecoveryMetrics` (ENG-064) — the dialog calls both at the moment of apply and again 24h later (or on next dialog render via `platformVersion`). AC: success ribbon expands to show the metric delta when at least 5 runs against the new version exist; per-metric severity-tinted arrows (green up / red down); falls back gracefully to "still gathering data" when the post-apply window has too few runs. **Summary:** New `GET /workflows/health/delta` route (`role: "viewer"`) reuses `collectHealthSignals` + `computeWorkflowHealth` with a per-call version cutoff to split the same time window into before/after sides — `collectHealthSignals` extended with an optional discriminated-union `versionFilter: { side: "before" \| "after"; cutoffVersion }` 4th argument that flows a single `lt`/`gte` predicate into the runs query. Route also returns three new fields the operator-feedback UX needs: `recentRunsAgainstAfter` (always-populated counter even for in-flight runs), `sameFailureSinceApply` (re-normalizes new DLQ entries against the caller-supplied prior signature; defense-in-depth — the response only echoes back the supplied signature, never freshly-derived ones), and `priorVersion` (drives the regression-rollback affordance). New `MIN_RUNS_FOR_DELTA = 5` constant exported from `@janusly/engine/src/workflow-health` so the route + dialog agree. `normalizeErrorSignature` relocated from `@janusly/engine/src/error-signature` to `@janusly/shared/src/error-signature` so the web bundle can derive a signature without pulling the engine; engine module becomes a thin re-export for back-compat (referential-equality test pins). New `<RecoveryDeltaCard>` component (~310 lines, `apps/web/src/components/RecoveryDeltaCard.tsx`) mounted inside `RecoveryDialog`'s `applied` step alongside the existing success ribbon. Renders 5 pills with severity-tinted arrows and plain-language sentences ("Health improved 4 points", "38% faster", "$0.004 cheaper per run"): run counter (always shown), same-failure check (when prior signature supplied), health, p95 latency, cost-per-run. The three delta pills are gated on `hasEnoughData` (≥5 runs against v{N}); below the threshold, the run counter + same-failure pill still render plus a progress bar showing how many of 5 runs are collected. When the delta surfaces a regression (`delta.score ≤ -3` AND v{N-1} exists), surfaces a role-gated "Roll back to v{N-1}" button for editors/admins that lazy-fetches both versions via the existing `/workflows/versions` endpoint and mounts the existing `<RollbackConfirmDialog>` — closing the loop in one click instead of forcing the operator to navigate to the version-history panel. `RecoveryDialog`'s `applyAfterValidation` extended to (a) derive the original failure's signature once via `normalizeErrorSignature`, (b) capture a pre-save `/workflows/health` snapshot at the moment Apply is clicked so the card renders the "before" pills statically without a loading flash, (c) capture the save-response's `{ workflowId, version }` and thread all three through to the `applied` step. Refetches on `platformVersion` bump (same hook the version-history panel uses) so the card animates as runs accumulate. New CSS classes `.we-recovery-delta-*` use existing `--we-success` / `--we-danger` / `--we-warn` / `--we-text-2` tokens — no inline hex. New tests: 12 `RecoveryDeltaCard.test.tsx` cases covering loading-with-snapshot, gathering-state, ready-state with all 5 pills + plain-language sentences, regression rollback affordance gated on threshold + priorVersion + effective role, error state with Retry, platformVersion-bump refetch, and the rollback fetch chain mounting `<RollbackConfirmDialog>`. Existing `RecoveryDialog.test.tsx` happy-path extended to assert the card mounts with `afterVersion=2`, the right `priorFailureSignature` derived from the seeded DLQ, and threads through to cluster mode. Engine-side back-compat pinned by 4 referential-equality cases. |
| ENG-083 | Structural patches (multi-node) | Shipped | P2 | 3 | The Recovery MVP's prompt explicitly tells the LLM to keep node ids stable and modify only the failing node's config — works for retry/timeout/secret-ref fixes but blocks structurally larger fixes (add an `approval` upstream of a write-side tool, split a flaky `http` into a `retry-loop + http` pair, insert an `error_handler` edge). Loosen the prompt to allow adding nodes / adding edges / partitioning a single node into a small subgraph; the engine-side `validateWorkflow` + `checkWorkflowReadiness` already gate the result. AC: at least one prompt per fix-class produces the multi-node patch (sample: "secret leaks through a public webhook → add an approval node before the http write"); the diff renderer (ENG-072) already handles add/remove rows so no UI changes needed; tests cover an "add approval upstream" patch suggestion. **Summary:** Shipped the `add_approval` structural fix-class as the first wired strategy. New `AiPatchStructuralEnvelope` in `apps/api/src/ai-schemas.ts` — single non-union object shape (provider-strict-safe: no `oneOf`, no `propertyNames`, no open `additionalProperties`) wrapping a `suggestions: Array<{ action: "insert_approval_upstream", approvalNodeId, approvalMessage, insertBeforeNodeId, rationale, approachLabel: "add_approval", confidence }>` bounded `min(1).max(3)`. The `action` literal is the extensibility hook — future structural fixes (`split_for_retry`, `insert_error_handler`) add a new literal value + a new applier case without changing the outer envelope. New `applyStructuralPatchToWorkflow` in `apps/api/src/patch-workflow-merge.ts` is a pure function alongside `applyConfigPatchToWorkflow`: validates the target node exists, rejects collisions with existing node ids, inserts the new `approval` node, and rewrites every edge whose `to` is the failing node so all predecessors flow through the new approval before reaching the target (multi-predecessor rewiring is comprehensive — no parallel-path bypass). Route dispatch in `apps/api/src/routes/ai-routes.ts:/ai/patch-workflow`: BEFORE the existing `patchEnvelopeForNodeType` call, compute `useStructural = failingNode.type === "http" && isSensitiveAction(failingNode) && !hasApprovalAncestor(...)`. When true, dispatch to the structural envelope + `STRUCTURAL_PATCH_SYSTEM_PROMPT` + `applyStructuralPatchToWorkflow` in the merge loop. When false, existing config path unchanged. Both `isSensitiveAction` and `hasApprovalAncestor` exported (newly) from `packages/engine/src/workflow-readiness.ts` so dispatch reuses the SAME rule that drives the production-readiness badge — drift between the readiness check and the recovery dispatcher is impossible. The structural envelope flows through the same per-suggestion `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow` chain the config envelope uses; suggestions whose merged workflow fails validation drop silently; all-invalid degrades to fallback. Audit metadata on `ai.workflow.patch_suggested` extends with `patchStyle: "structural" \| "config_only"` — existing readers ignore the new field; the metric distinguishes "how often did the LLM produce a structural fix vs a config tweak." **v1 scope explicitly narrow:** ONE structural action — `insert_approval_upstream`. Only http POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE failing nodes (tool write-side stays config-only). Other fix-classes are explicit future slots. New tests: 14 structural-applier cases (happy path with single-edge rewire, schema-clean re-parse, multi-predecessor rewiring, root-write-side edge case, missing-target rejection, collision rejection, unknown-action defensive guard, envelope schema acceptance of 1/2/3 suggestions, rejection of empty/over-cap suggestions, rejection of wrong approachLabel/action literals, confidence bound rejection, empty-string field rejection); 2 prompt-pin cases asserting `STRUCTURAL_PATCH_SYSTEM_PROMPT` is shipped when override is set and the default config-only prompt is used otherwise. AGENTS.md "Failure recovery patch surface" entry extended with the structural envelope contract + the dispatch rule. Recovery-matrix smoke target: `write_side_without_approval` fixture now reaches `mode: "ai"` with `topApproachLabel: "add_approval"` (Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5-20251001) instead of the previous `fallback` posture. |
| ENG-084 | Extend AI patch schema with retry/timeout/bounds config | Shipped | P1 | 1 | The Recovery MVP (ENG-053) reuses `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` for structured output. That schema's `AiHttpNode.config` only allows `url` + `method`; `AiToolNode.config` only allows `tool`; `AiAgentNode.config` only allows `goal`. The LLM regularly generates patches that say "added retry maxAttempts=3" in the rationale but the strict schema strips the actual `retry`/`timeoutMs`/`maxResponseBytes`/`maxRedirects` fields from `config` before the response leaves the API — operator sees the rationale but the structural diff shows nothing actionable on the failing node. AC: the Recovery dialog's diff actually shows `config.retry`/`config.timeoutMs`/`config.maxResponseBytes` changes when the LLM proposes them, not just metadata-level changes; tests cover a 502 → retry patch surface and pin that the patched workflow saves successfully and replays through the engine; engine-side `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` continues to be the source of truth. Spotted during ENG-053 manual smoke. **Summary:** Path (b) shipped. Both AI Zod schemas now live in `apps/api/src/ai-schemas.ts` (extracted from `index.ts` for testability). New `AiPatchGenerationWorkflowSchema` is a parallel 11-branch discriminated union with richer `AiPatchHttpNode` / `AiPatchToolNode` / `AiPatchAgentNode` configs that allow optional `retry: { maxAttempts }` (2-10), `timeoutMs`, `maxResponseBytes`, `maxRedirects`. The 8 non-richer branches reuse the slim node schemas verbatim. `/ai/patch-workflow`'s `PatchEnvelopeSchema` swapped to the new schema; `/ai/generate-workflow` keeps the slim one. `SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts` now instructs the LLM to emit resilience knobs in `config` directly when the failure is transient (5xx / ECONNRESET / timeout / body-cap), with concrete value guidance. New `apps/api/src/ai-patch-schema.test.ts` pins (a) the patch schema preserves retry/timeout/bounds on http/tool/agent, (b) the slim generation schema strips them (regression pin), (c) the round-trip through engine `WorkflowSchema` carries the resilience fields untouched. **Superseded by ENG-086:** provider-safe per-type config envelopes replaced the parallel union while preserving the same retry/timeout/bounds AC. |

| ENG-086 | Fix `/ai/patch-workflow` schema for both providers | Shipped | P1 | 1 | ENG-084's `AiPatchGenerationWorkflowSchema` (11-branch discriminated union with resilience optionals) failed on every registered LLM provider when actually compiled at the structured-output boundary: Anthropic rejected with "compiled grammar is too large", OpenAI strict mode rejected with "'oneOf' is not permitted". Result: `/ai/patch-workflow` always degraded to `mode: "fallback"` and the entire Recovery dialog (ENG-053 / ENG-077 / ENG-078) was a no-op end-to-end. Surfaced during ENG-078's UI smoke test. **Summary:** Replaced the full-workflow union with per-failing-node-type config-only envelopes (`AiPatchHttpConfigEnvelope` / `AiPatchToolConfigEnvelope` / `AiPatchAgentConfigEnvelope` / `AiPatchGenericConfigEnvelope`). Each envelope is a single non-union `{ patchedConfig, rationale }` shape — no `oneOf` keyword in the compiled JSON Schema. Route at `/ai/patch-workflow` reads `dlq.nodeJson.type`, picks the envelope via `patchEnvelopeForNodeType`, and merges the patched config into the original workflow server-side (shallow object spread; nulls filtered out). Leaf fields use `.nullable()` instead of `.optional()` so the schema satisfies OpenAI strict mode's "every property in `properties` must be in `required`" rule while remaining within Anthropic's grammar cap. The helper `suggestWorkflowPatch` stays schema-agnostic; only the `SYSTEM_PROMPT` and `PatchEnvelopeSchemaResult` shape change ("emit patched config for the failing node" instead of "emit a full workflow"). Real-provider smoke verified the typed resilience path end-to-end on both Anthropic (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`) and OpenAI (`gpt-4o-mini`) — both produce `mode: "ai"` with structural patches landing on the failing node. `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow` post-validation chain unchanged. Audits include the new `envelopeKind` field. Recovery v2 (ENG-077 sandbox gate, ENG-078 cluster apply) is now reachable end-to-end through the UI for both providers on the typed http/tool/agent patch path; generic non-resilience node patches remain best-effort until they get concrete provider-strict envelopes. |
| ENG-087 | Fix `/ai/generate-workflow` schema for OpenAI strict mode | Deferred | P2 | 2 | The same `oneOf` rejection that broke `/ai/patch-workflow` against OpenAI strict mode (fixed in ENG-086) also affects `/ai/generate-workflow`. `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` is an 11-branch discriminated union; OpenAI strict-mode validator rejects `oneOf` outright. Today AI Studio works against Anthropic (default tenant provider) but degrades to `mode: "fallback"` against OpenAI. **Deferred to post-MVP — MVP support posture is Anthropic-only for AI surfaces** (see the "AI integration" bullet in AGENTS.md). The provider abstraction in `packages/ai/src/llm-client.ts` stays intact for future expansion; this ticket comes back when multi-provider is on the roadmap. **What landed during the deferred-investigation pass:** attempted path (b) — relax `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` to a single open node shape `{ id, type, config: Record<string, unknown> }`. Real-provider smoke surfaced two failures the schema-level tests missed: (1) on Anthropic, claude-haiku-4-5-20251001 actively relies on the strict schema to enforce required fields; once the schema is loose, the model emits http nodes without `url` / ai nodes without `prompt`, post-validation rejects the whole workflow, and the route degrades to `mode: "fallback"` — net regression. (2) on OpenAI, `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())` itself trips a different strict-mode rejection: `'propertyNames' is not permitted`. So a one-line schema swap doesn't work — both providers degrade differently. **Reverted the schema relaxation; kept the collateral validator hardening** (`validateWorkflow` now rejects `ai_missing_prompt` / `agent_missing_goal` / `transform_missing_mapping` — these were soft gaps before, and the new checks make the post-validation chain a more honest strict gate for any future schema strategy AND for direct operator input). **Post-MVP candidate paths** (when this comes off Deferred): (a) intent-dispatch UI where the operator picks a flow style first (single agent / smart router / multi-agent crew / approval gate, etc.) and each style has a single non-union schema — high quality, requires UI work; (b) two-pass generation — first call emits a loose draft, second call fills missing required fields per type — moderate cost, no UI work; (c) wrap the AI SDK's `generateObject` with a provider-specific mode toggle — pass `mode: "json"` (non-strict) to OpenAI so it accepts the union but doesn't enforce — simplest but loses OpenAI structured-output guarantees. Cost re-estimated at 2 (was 1 — open-shape design didn't survive contact with reality). |
| ENG-088 | Provider-strict recovery envelopes for non-resilience nodes | Shipped | P2 | 2 | ENG-086 makes the primary Recovery patch path provider-safe for `http` / `tool` / `agent` resilience fixes. The fallback `AiPatchGenericConfigEnvelope` still uses an open `Record<string, unknown>` shape for transform/condition/ai/router/approval/multi_agent/loop/noop patches. That keeps local Zod parsing flexible but is not a good strict-provider contract: OpenAI requires concrete object keys with `additionalProperties: false`, and Anthropic has already rejected empty/open schemas on adjacent AI surfaces. AC: replace the generic record with concrete envelopes for each supported non-resilience node type; convert any model-friendly array pair shape back into engine config where needed (for example transform mappings); add JSON-schema shape tests that fail on root composition, `propertyNames`, and open `additionalProperties`; smoke at least one non-http patch on Anthropic and OpenAI with `mode: "ai"`. **Summary:** Added 6 concrete envelopes — `AiPatchTransformConfigEnvelope` (mapping array-of-pairs, max 50), `AiPatchConditionConfigEnvelope` (expression scalar), `AiPatchAiConfigEnvelope` (prompt + model + retry), `AiPatchRouterConfigEnvelope` (candidates verbatim-replace array, max 10, + strategy enum), `AiPatchApprovalConfigEnvelope` (message scalar), `AiPatchLoopConfigEnvelope` (items scalar + mapping array-of-pairs). All use the same nullable-required leaf-field pattern as ENG-086/ENG-089 — provider-safe shape, no `oneOf`, no `propertyNames`, no open `additionalProperties`. `patchEnvelopeForNodeType` dispatcher grows from 4 → 9 cases; `router_llm` shares the router envelope. `multi_agent` and `noop` intentionally stay on the generic envelope (multi_agent: agents-array shape too complex for v1; noop: no actionable runtime config) — documented in the dispatcher. `PAIR_ARRAY_FIELDS_BY_NODE_TYPE` extended with `transform: ['mapping']` and `loop: ['mapping']` so the per-node-type fold semantics extend to those types without bleeding into other generic-envelope nodes. SYSTEM_PROMPT teaches per-type guidance for all 6 (TRANSFORM / CONDITION / AI / ROUTER / APPROVAL / LOOP) with concrete examples + grammar pointers. **Smoke matrix expanded to 15 fixtures** — added `transform_invalid_mapping`, `condition_invalid_expression`, `router_unknown_candidate`, `loop_invalid_items`, `ai_prompt_missing_grounding` to exercise each new envelope end-to-end. Verified on Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5: matrix lift from 8/10 → **14/15 (93%)** ai-mode rate; all 5 new envelope kinds appear in `audit_logs.metadata.envelopeKind` (`transform`, `condition`, `ai`, `router`, `loop`). `ai_provider_transient` flipped from fallback to ai with `add_retry 92` + 2 alternatives. The remaining fallback is `write_side_without_approval` (structural multi-node patch, ENG-083 territory). **Real-browser smoke** verified the dialog renders the per-envelope diff correctly for transform (`config.mapping.summary` swap), condition (`config.expression` swap; engine grammar passes post-merge), and router (`config.candidates[0].nodeId` verbatim-replace) — all three flow through the same multi-tab dialog with no UI changes. New tests: 6 envelope test suites (~30 cases covering positive / null sentinel / negative bounds / grammar checks per envelope) + dispatch tests asserting concrete-envelope routing for the 7 new dispatch entries and intentional fallback for multi_agent/noop/unknown. |
| ENG-089 | Resilience envelopes: HTTP `headers` + tool `input` | Shipped | P1 | 2 | ENG-080 smoke matrix on Anthropic showed the multi-suggestion patch route lands in `mode: "fallback"` whenever the natural fix lives in a field the per-failing-node-type resilience envelope doesn't expose. The `AiPatchHttpConfig` shape today exposes `url / method / retry / timeoutMs / maxResponseBytes / maxRedirects` only — so a 401 with a literal `Authorization` token (the operator should swap it to `{{secret.NAME}}`) and a missing-secret fixture both degrade. The `AiPatchToolConfig` exposes `tool / retry / timeoutMs` only — so a malformed-tool-input failure (`text.replace` missing `pattern`/`replacement`) also degrades. AC: add `headers` to the http envelope and `input` to the tool envelope as bounded `Array<{ name, value: string \| null }>` patch forms (provider-safe — single repeated `items` shape, no `propertyNames`, no `oneOf`); merge layer in `apps/api/src/patch-workflow-merge.ts` folds each item against the existing record (null value removes the key); SYSTEM_PROMPT in `packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts` teaches the array form and clarifies `swap_secret_ref` covers it; the recovery-matrix smoke lifts from 5/10 to ≥ 8/10 ai-mode rate on Anthropic with structurally-valid merges for `http_401_literal_token`, `missing_secret_template`, `tool_input_invalid`. Stays inside Anthropic's compiled-grammar cap (each new field adds one nullable wrapper + one nested 2-field shape × bounded array) and OpenAI's strict-mode rules. No UI change — `WorkflowDiffView` already renders arbitrary config keys. No DB migration. **Summary:** Added `AiPatchHttpHeader` + `AiPatchToolInputItem` schemas; extended the per-failing-node-type envelopes (`headers` on http, `input` on tool) as bounded `Array<{name, value}>(.max(20)/.max(30))` patch forms with `value: string \| null` (null = remove the key on merge). The merge layer (`apps/api/src/patch-workflow-merge.ts`) gained a per-node-type whitelist (`PAIR_ARRAY_FIELDS_BY_NODE_TYPE = { http: ['headers'], tool: ['input'] }`) so the array-of-pairs fold only fires for the resilience envelopes — a `subworkflow.config.input` (forwarded object, not flat record) flows through verbatim through the generic envelope. Empty arrays drop as no-op (preserve existing); malformed pair items also drop. SYSTEM_PROMPT teaches the array form with `{{secret.NAME}}` examples for both surfaces and notes that `swap_secret_ref` is the right approachLabel for header/input template swaps. Smoke verified on Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5: matrix lift from 5/10 → 8/10 ai-mode rate (+30%), with `http_401_literal_token` (swap_secret_ref 95 + alternatives), `missing_secret_template` (3 swap_secret_ref suggestions), and `tool_input_invalid` (after the toolInputContract enrichment was added) all flipping from fallback to AI mode. The remaining 2 fallbacks have known causes outside this ticket: `ai_provider_transient` is a generic-envelope LLM-output edge case (the model emits an empty patchedConfig for the `ai` node type); `write_side_without_approval` needs structural multi-node patches. **Tool input enrichment:** the route now plucks the failing tool's `listTools()` entry (`name` / `description` / `required` / `optional` / `inputExample`) and threads it through `extraContext.toolInputContract` so the LLM has the exact required-field names instead of inferring them from a possibly-misleading runtime error. The helper helper takes a new optional `extraContext: Record<string, unknown>` parameter; SYSTEM_PROMPT teaches the LLM to use the contract's field names verbatim. Additional unit test pins the contract flows through to the prompt body. New tests: 5 schema cases for the headers/input array shape (length bounds, empty-name reject, multi-knob cohabit), 6 merge-layer cases including the per-node-type scoping regression test, and updated matrix synthesizers for the 3 affected fixtures. Closes the largest single quality gap surfaced in the ENG-080 smoke matrix. |
| ENG-090 | Promote remaining operator-only AI generation placeholders | Shipped | P1 | 3 | ENG-076 ships the two-pass strategy with `wait_until` only; the remaining operator-only generation intents (`multi_agent`, `schedule`, `webhook`, `subworkflow`, `parallel_fork`, `join`, `router_llm`, `agent_reflection`) still require manual Inspector promotion. Extend the Pass-2 promotion pattern only where it improves generated-workflow quality without expanding the 11-branch Anthropic grammar. AC: add a typed single-shape schema and dispatcher branch for at least one additional intent family; keep `/ai/generate-workflow` direct output capped at the current 11 node types; per-placeholder failures keep `mode: "ai"` and leave the original noop intact; audit metadata reports attempts/successes per family; tests cover false-positive id prefixes, schema rejection, and successful promotion. Reason: outside ENG-076's shipped `wait_until` scope but needed so "all node types" remains tracked after the umbrella row closes. **Summary:** Shipped `schedule` as the second wired Pass-2 promotion family alongside `wait_until`. New `AiScheduleConfigSchema` + `CRON_EXPRESSION_PATTERN` + `SCHEDULE_PROMOTE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `packages/ai/src/promoted-schemas.ts` — single non-union object shape `{ cronExpression: string }` constrained by the same 5-field cron shape the engine accepts. Aliases such as `@daily` are intentionally rejected so promotion failures stay local to the placeholder instead of reaching `sanitizeAiWorkflow` and forcing a full `/ai/generate-workflow` fallback. Refactored `WAIT_ID_PREFIXES` (single-family flat list) → `PROMOTE_FAMILIES: ReadonlyArray<{ prefixes, target }>` (multi-family). Schedule family triggers on `schedule_*` / `cron_*` / `every_*` / `daily_*` / `weekly_*` / `monthly_*` / `hourly_*` id prefixes (case-insensitive, non-alphanumeric separator required to dodge false-positives like `analyze_schedule_data`). New `promoteToSchedule` helper mirrors `promoteToWaitUntil`: single LLM call with `SCHEDULE_PROMOTE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` + the operator's verbatim prompt + the noop's id, regex pre-filter on the output, silent degrade on every failure path. Per-family audit metadata: `audit_logs.metadata.promotionsByFamily: { wait_until: { attempts, succeeded }, schedule: { attempts, succeeded } }` additively layered next to the existing aggregate `promotionAttempts` + `promotionsSucceeded` fields — existing readers ignore the new key. Pass-1 `GENERATE_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` extended with a SCHEDULE-INTENT NAMING paragraph + a worked-example JSON; the prompt explicitly calls out "schedule a meeting" style on-demand intents as NOT cron so the LLM doesn't false-flag a one-off step. **v1 scope explicitly narrow:** ONE additional family (`schedule`); the other 7 operator-only types (`multi_agent` / `webhook` / `subworkflow` / `parallel_fork` / `join` / `router_llm` / `agent_reflection`) stay as `noop` placeholders that the operator promotes manually. The framework primes the remaining families — each new one is a new entry in `PROMOTE_FAMILIES`, a new schema in `promoted-schemas.ts`, a new helper, and a new dispatcher branch. **Anthropic 11-branch grammar cap stays intact** — `schedule` does not enter `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema`; it emerges via Pass-2 promotion of a `noop` id, mirroring the `wait_until` posture. New tests: 12 Pass-2 cases (schedule happy path with 5-field cron, every documented schedule prefix recognised case-insensitively + non-alphanumeric separators, `@daily` alias rejected because the engine requires five fields, out-of-range cron fields rejected before sanitize, non-cron string rejection — `monday morning` regex-failed, empty cron string short-circuit, mid-string `schedule` ignored — `analyze_schedule_data`, multi-family co-existence with per-family count assertions, per-family failure isolation — schedule throws, sibling wait still promotes, zero-noop workflow populates empty per-family counts) plus 1 new prompt-pin case (SCHEDULE-INTENT NAMING paragraph + worked example + false-positive guard). Existing 11 wait-intent cases stay green — regression-protected. AGENTS.md AI integration "Two-pass promotion" entry extended with the schedule family + the `promotionsByFamily` audit-metadata extension. |

The helper is fail-soft: audit write throws never break the route. The 6 `/ai/*` routes (`generate-workflow`, `explain-workflow`, `review-workflow`, `patch-workflow`, `suggest-improvement`, `explain-run`) call `gateBudget` BEFORE `enforceRateLimit`; block path returns HTTP 402 with `{ error: "budget_exceeded", budget: <envelope> }`. Engine paths gated: `ai` node executor degrades to `{ mode: "fallback", aiError: "budget_exceeded", response: <deterministic content> }`; `agent` planner returns a `done` terminate decision with the budget reason. AI fallback contract intact. Web surfaces: `BudgetSettingsPanel` (mounted inside Operations) edits org + per-workflow budgets via `POST /org/config` + the new route; `BudgetTile` (5th tile in `RecoveryCenterPanel`) shows MTD spend bar with cobalt / amber / red severity bands; AI Studio CTAs (Draft / Explain / Review) gain a "(~$X.XX)" chip via the zero-dep `@janusly/shared/src/llm-pricing` helper; `BudgetBlockedBanner` (mounted in `App.tsx` overlay) surfaces the 402 envelope on every AI 402 — captured by `apps/web/src/api.ts`, kept in `useWorkflowStore.budgetBlocked`. 7 new tests pin the gate's clean / blocked / warn / de-dup / fail-soft / response-shape behavior; existing OperationsPanel + RecoveryCenter tests updated to stub `/billing/budget` so the new tile doesn't trip strict mocks. **AC fully met.** |

| ENG-094 | Build MCP client v1 | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Let Janusly consume external MCP tools as safe workflow steps through a new `mcp_tool` path instead of only exposing Janusly as an MCP server. AC: tool discovery imports descriptors into a bounded allowlist; execution uses tenant-scoped credentials, dry-run/write-side gating, SSRF/time/body guards where applicable, rate limits, audit metadata, and usage events; operators can promote an allowed MCP tool into a workflow step without raw arbitrary execution. Depends on ENG-057/ENG-071 safety decisions. **Summary:** New `mcp_tool` node type + admin CRUD lets an admin register external MCP servers per org (alias + transport + envRefs) and per-tool enable specific descriptors. Two transports: `stdio` (gated by `JANUSLY_MCP_ALLOWED_COMMANDS` env allowlist + `mcp.clientCommandAllowlist` tenant override; fail-closed by default; spawn env whitelisted to `{ PATH, ...envRefs }` only so a child server can't read `DATABASE_URL` / `JANUSLY_RESUME_TOKEN_SECRET`) and `sse` (URL routed through `fetchHttpTarget`'s SSRF / DNS-pin / body-cap / timeout chokepoint so a metadata endpoint like `169.254.169.254` is rejected up-front). Two new tables (migration `20260514205432_worthless_millenium_guard`): `mcp_connections` (unique on `(orgId, alias)`, closed-enum `status` of `pending` / `active` / `failed` / `disabled`) and `mcp_tool_descriptors` (unique on `(connectionId, name)`, per-tool `enabled` flag operator-opt-in, `writeSide` flag operator-assigned with fail-safe default `true`). New `mcpConnectionsRepo` with multi-tenant-scoped CRUD; new transport-agnostic `mcp-client` wrapper around `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk@^1.29.0` (already a dep via `packages/mcp-server`) with `listTools` + `callTool({timeoutMs})` + `close` + a `withMcpClient` convenience that ensures close runs in a finally. New `mcp-tool-executor` chokepoint: refuses on unknown alias / disabled tool / inactive connection, honours dry-run gate (sandbox replay skips write-side), enforces two-flag write consent (process env `JANUSLY_MCP_CLIENT_WRITES_ENABLED` + tenant `org_configs.mcp.clientWriteConsent` — both must be true), resolves env-refs from `process.env` with generic `credential secret missing for <key>` error (never echoes the env-var name), enforces per-tool Redis-backed rate-limit bucket `mcp_client.<alias>.<toolname>` (default 60/min, fail-open on Redis blips), wraps the SDK call in try/catch + timeout race, returns `{ ok, error?, output?, latencyMs, connectionAlias, toolName, transport, writeSide }` envelope. Tool never throws — every failure becomes `ok: false` (matches the integration-tool contract). New `mcp-usage` DI seam mirrors `integration-usage`: registered at api + worker boot with `recordMcpUsage` writing `usage_events.metric = "tool.mcp.<alias>.<name>"` on success AND failure (fire-and-forget try/catch so telemetry never breaks a tool path). Permission catalog grows from 17 → 19 keys: `mcp.connections.read` (viewer+) + `mcp.connections.write` (admin only); new `mcp` category. Three new `org_configs.mcp.*` keys: `clientWriteConsent` (boolean, no env fallback by design), `clientRateLimitPerMin` (number, env fallback `JANUSLY_MCP_CLIENT_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN`), `clientCommandAllowlist` (CSV with `allowEmpty: true` flag; env fallback `JANUSLY_MCP_ALLOWED_COMMANDS`). Seven new admin routes (`apps/api/src/routes/mcp-routes.ts`): list / create-and-discover / update / delete / rediscover / list-tools / toggle-tool — every mutating route gated by `role: "admin"` + `permission: "mcp.connections.write"`. Six new `audit_logs` actions (one row per API-level mutation): `mcp.connection.created` / `_updated` / `_deleted` / `_rediscovered`, `mcp.tool.enabled` / `_disabled`. Per-invocation telemetry rides on `run_events` (`mcp_tool.started` + `mcp_tool.completed`) plus the `usage_events` recorder; the executor itself doesn't write audit rows because the audit table is scoped to API-level mutations. Web ships `McpConnectionsPanel.tsx` mounted in `OperationsPanel` (admin-only) — connection list with status badges, add-connection form with transport-conditional fields + env-refs key-value editor, per-connection accordion with per-tool enable + writeSide toggles, Re-discover + Delete buttons. New `mcp_tool` node-type tile in the canvas palette + dedicated Inspector branch (`McpToolConfigField`) with dependent connection→tool dropdowns sourced from `/mcp/connections` + auto-generated input JSON editor + timeout numeric. **No LLM exposure in v1** — `apps/api/src/ai-prompts.ts`'s `GENERATE_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` tool list is UNCHANGED so prompt-injection from third-party tool descriptions can't reach the system prompt; operators promote MCP tools manually via the Inspector. AI generation's grammar still caps at the 11 closed branches + 8 builtin tools. **v1 carve-out:** ENG-057 (MCP safety model standalone doc) + HTTP transport + per-connection `exposeToAi` flag + per-tool rate-limit overrides remain v2 follow-ups; the safety model lands inline with this ticket via the env-allowlist, env-whitelist spawn, SSRF-guarded SSE, two-flag write consent, and operator-only Inspector promotion. ENG-071 (operator UX via MCP) stays unchanged — that's a separate inbound surface. Tests: `mcp-tool-executor.test.ts` (19 cases — happy path, no-org, missing connection, disabled connection, inactive status, disabled descriptor, write-consent process flag off, write-consent tenant flag off, dry-run skip, missing env-ref no-leak, rate-limit reject, isError surface, timeout, audit fires on both paths, env env-vars helper, rate-limit helper), `mcp-client.test.ts` (5 cases — stdio env strict whitelist, SSE SSRF reject pre-construct, SSE headers pass-through, withMcpClient close-on-throw, listTools normalisation), `mcp-routes.test.ts` (12 cases — alias regex, transport validation, command allowlist reject, 409 duplicate alias, happy create + discovery + status=active, discovery failure flips status=failed, DELETE 404, DELETE happy path, tool toggle happy, tool toggle 404, tool toggle 400 empty body, role + permission gates on every mutating route), `node-registry.mcp-tool.test.ts` (3 cases — completed + output, throws on failed envelope for retry/DLQ, missing config rejection), `McpConnectionsPanel.test.tsx` (4 cases — empty state, existing connection render with status badge + tool counts, accordion expand + tool render, create-form happy path + platformVersion bump), `permission-catalog.test.ts` extension (2 cases — 19-key floor + MCP keys present). API test count grows by ~22; engine grows by ~27; web grows by 4. |

"default"` fallback for Supabase is gone. `POST /members/invite` now writes to `invitations` instead of seeding `org_members` with `userId = email`; two new admin routes (`GET /members/invitations`, `POST /members/invitations/:id/revoke`). | ENG-098 | Add WorkOS enterprise SSO | Shipped | P1 | 4 | Add WorkOS as the enterprise identity layer for SAML/OIDC SSO without rewriting Janusly authorization. AC: each org can attach a WorkOS connection, login callback validates state and org binding, JIT provisioning creates/updates Janusly users and memberships only under explicit policy, enforced-SSO orgs block password/dev-style login in production, and every SSO login writes `auth.sso.login` audit metadata. Do not build SAML/OIDC yourself in v1. Depends on ENG-096/ENG-097. **Summary:** WorkOS plugged into the SSO JIT seam ENG-097 left behind. New schema column `sso_connections.enforced_sso` + new table `sso_state_nonces` (one-time-use replay protection). `apps/api/src/workos.ts` is the REST client (no `@workos-inc/node` SDK so the `fetchHttpTarget` SSRF / body / redirect / timeout chokepoint applies; pins `WorkOS-Version: 2024-07-01`). New 4th auth mode `janusly-session` (HMAC-signed via the generalized `signSignedToken` / `verifySignedToken` in `packages/engine/src/secrets.ts` with `purpose: "sso_session"`; runs first in `PROVIDER_CHAIN` so a fresh SSO login beats stale Supabase cookies). New `AuthSource = "sso"`. New routes: 4 admin CRUD (`/org/sso/connections`) + 2 flow routes (`/auth/sso/start`, `/auth/sso/callback`). State token signs `{orgId, nonce, callbackUrl}` with `purpose: "sso_state"` and 10-min TTL; callback verifies HMAC + DELETE-and-checks the nonce + binds callback URL + verifies `profile.connectionId === sso_connection.providerConnectionId` (defense in depth against cross-org code reuse) before JIT-upserting the membership. Session token (8-hour TTL) delivered via `#janusly_session=<token>` URL fragment; web client (`consumeSsoSessionFragment` + `getSessionToken` in `apps/web/src/auth.ts`) extracts on boot, persists to `localStorage["janusly:sessionToken"]`, strips fragment via `history.replaceState`. `apps/web/src/api.ts` sends `x-janusly-session` when set (instead of Bearer). `Login.tsx` gains a "Continue with SSO" form. Enforced-SSO check in the resolver rejects supabase/dev-headers for orgs with `enforced_sso: true`; service-token + janusly-session pass; `ALLOW_DEV_SSO_BYPASS=true` is the dev escape hatch (inverts the `NODE_ENV` polarity so a staging-on-prod misconfig fails closed instead of silently bypassing). Five new audit actions: `org.sso.connection_added` / `_updated` / `_revoked` / `auth.sso.start` / `auth.sso.login` / `auth.sso.state_invalid` / `auth.sso.callback_failed`. `.env.example` documents the four operator-supplied env vars (`WORKOS_CLIENT_ID`, `WORKOS_API_KEY`, `JANUSLY_SSO_CALLBACK_URL`, `JANUSLY_WEB_BASE_URL`) + `ALLOW_DEV_SSO_BYPASS`. 9 new sso-routes tests + 4 new auth-test scenarios + 1 new web api-test. 322 api tests pass. **Live smoke against real WorkOS project + Test Connection:** confirmed end-to-end up to the WorkOS hosted UI — admin CRUD round-trips through psql, `/auth/sso/start` 302s to the real WorkOS authorize URL with a valid HMAC state token carrying the real connection id, nonce persists in `sso_state_nonces` with TTL, `auth.sso.start` audit row written; tampered state on `/auth/sso/callback` 400s with `auth.sso.state_invalid` audit row carrying `reason: "invalid_signature_or_expiry"`. The final WorkOS-callback-with-real-code leg is bounded by AuthKit (the WorkOS hosted signup/login UX that intercepts the redirect when AuthKit is enabled on the project); completing it requires either a one-time signup of a verifiable email or disabling AuthKit on the project. Both are operator-side decisions and the callback contract is fully pinned by the 9 unit tests in `sso-routes.test.ts` (state HMAC, nonce DELETE-and-check, callback URL binding, connectionId mismatch fail-closed, JIT upsert + session token issuance, audit on every branch). Known limitations: WorkOS connectionIds are environment-scoped (sandbox ≠ prod); no SSO logout/SLO in v1; `sso_state_nonces` not pruned automatically (rows are harmless past TTL); AuthKit-enabled WorkOS projects route the first login through their hosted signup UX. **Live verification note:** AuthKit-enabled WorkOS projects intercept `/sso/authorize` regardless of the `connection` param, routing the user through AuthKit's hosted sign-in UI instead of going directly to the configured IdP. The "Test SSO connection" attached to the built-in Test Organization is part of AuthKit's demo experience, not a standalone SSO connection — it's bound to AuthKit by design. Confirmed live against a real WorkOS project + Test Connection: admin CRUD round-trips, `/auth/sso/start` 302s with a valid HMAC state, nonce persists with TTL, audit rows fire, tampered state correctly 400s with `auth.sso.state_invalid`, WorkOS issues real auth codes via Test SSO, and `exchangeCode` parses WorkOS' `invalid_grant` envelope cleanly through `WorkOsExchangeError`. The final happy-path leg (fresh code arriving at our callback within its ~60s TTL → JIT upsert + session token + 302 to the web with `#janusly_session=`) is not exercised live with the built-in Test Connection because AuthKit owns the post-auth redirect; verifying that leg end-to-end requires a real IdP (Okta / Azure AD / Google Workspace / Generic SAML 2.0). Every branch of that leg is line-by-line covered by the 9 `sso-routes.test.ts` cases that mock the `exchangeCode` boundary. ENG-099 (SCIM) has a clean JIT-provisioning seam to plug into. |
| ENG-099 | Add SCIM directory sync | Shipped | P1 | 4 | Add WorkOS-backed SCIM lifecycle sync for enterprise tenants. AC: create/update/deactivate users, map directory groups to Janusly memberships/roles or future permissions, revoke access on deprovision, record idempotent audit events, and test replayed/out-of-order events so SCIM cannot elevate or resurrect users unexpectedly. Depends on ENG-098. **Summary:** Four new tables in `packages/db/src/schema.ts` (migration `20260514180928_stormy_dragon_man`): `scim_directories` (one per org, admin-attached with default role), `scim_user_state` (per-`(directory, providerUserId)` mirror of IdP state, `email` is the join key into `org_members`), `scim_group_state` (group state captured for v2 role mapping), `scim_processed_events` (PK = event id; `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` for replay idempotency). Four new repos in `packages/data/src/scim*Repo.ts` plus a `deleteMembership` helper on `orgMembersRepo`. The webhook receiver `POST /webhooks/workos/directory` (skipAuth, in new `apps/api/src/routes/scim-routes.ts`) verifies WorkOS' Stripe-style `WorkOS-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex>` HMAC-SHA256 via the new pure `apps/api/src/workos-webhook.ts` (±5 min tolerance, constant-time hex compare, missing-secret rejects every webhook). Org binding goes through `getScimDirectoryByProviderDirectoryId(directory_id)` — the ONLY un-scoped DB read in the SCIM module by design; the webhook payload's tenant claim is never trusted. Pure dispatcher `apps/api/src/scim-event-handler.ts` runs three guards in deterministic order: (1) replay via `scim_processed_events`, (2) out-of-order via `scim_user_state.lastEventTimestamp`, (3) resurrection — a `user.created` for a deactivated user with `created_at <= last_event_timestamp` is blocked + audited; a genuine reactivation with newer timestamp is allowed and audited `metadata.reactivated: true`. SCIM provisioning runs `getAuthPolicyConfig(orgId)` for the `allowedEmailDomains` gate so out-of-policy users never get an `org_members` row (audit `scim.user.provision_rejected`). SCIM-provisioned `org_members` rows go in with `userId = lower(email)` — the legacy-orphan placeholder ENG-097 already bridges to the real SSO profile id on first SSO login via the resolver's step-2 backfill, no SCIM-specific resolver code needed. Lookups always use `(orgId, lower(email))`, stable across the SSO rewrite. v1 ships per-directory `defaultRole`; per-group role mapping deferred to v2 (`scim_group_state` is populated but not consulted for role yet). Webhook ALWAYS 200s on signature-pass + parseable JSON (guard rejections don't trigger WorkOS retries for hours); real I/O failures bubble to 5xx so WorkOS retries those. Three new admin routes (`GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE` on `/org/scim/directories[/:id]`) plus a minimal `ScimDirectorySettingsPanel.tsx` mounted in `OperationsPanel`. 12+ new audit actions catalogued in AGENTS.md. New env var `WORKOS_WEBHOOK_SECRET` documented in `.env.example`. Tests: `workos-webhook.test.ts` (10 cases — valid sig, missing/malformed/expired/future header, signature mismatch, missing-secret fails closed, tolerance boundary), `scim-event-handler.test.ts` (25 cases — replay guard, out-of-order guard, resurrection guard, reactivation, all 3 user event types, group events, allowed-email-domains, multi-tenant scope, malformed payloads, sync timestamp), `scim-routes.test.ts` (10 cases — admin CRUD with role gates + audit, webhook signature 401 + malformed JSON 400 + valid dispatch + unknown/revoked directory 200), `ScimDirectorySettingsPanel.test.tsx` (4 cases). API test count: 344 → 392; web grows by 4. Zero changes to the membership resolver path; ENG-097's legacy-orphan-backfill carries the SCIM→SSO bridge. ENG-101 (fine-grained permissions) has a clean per-org-config seam to plug into — group-role mapping arrives at the same time. |

| ENG-101 | Add fine-grained permissions | Shipped | P2 | 4 | Evolve `viewer` / `editor` / `admin` into explicit permissions without breaking existing role checks. AC: define a permission catalog, map current roles to permissions, update route guards through a compatibility layer, expose permissions in audit/operator UI where useful, and test that existing role behavior remains backward-compatible while enterprise orgs can adopt narrower grants. Depends on ENG-096/ENG-100. **Summary:** Hand-rolled (no library dep — CASL.js evaluated and rejected since Janusly's data model is uniformly org-scoped today with no record-level ownership; the `requirePermission(perm)` shape maps 1:1 to `ability.can(action, subject)` if record-level ever lands). New `org_roles` table (migration `20260514194530_thick_zombie`) stores both per-built-in overrides and admin-created custom roles. Closed 17-key catalog in `apps/api/src/permission-catalog.ts` grouped into 9 categories; `defaultRoles` per entry derives `DEFAULT_ROLE_PERMISSIONS` for the three built-ins. Built-ins are VIRTUAL — the table starts empty per org and grows only when an admin overrides a built-in's permissions or creates a custom role. Custom roles always have a row with `inheritsFrom` (closed enum: viewer / editor / admin) for rank inheritance + `grantedPermissions` JSONB. **Custom roles default to `inheritsFrom: "viewer"`** (fail-closed) — fails the 37 unmigrated `requireRole("editor")` gates by rank, requiring explicit opt-in to higher ranks. **Permission gate**: new `Route.permission?:` sibling field next to `role?:`. Dispatcher in `apps/api/src/server.ts` runs `requireRole` then `requirePermission` in that order — when both are present, BOTH must pass (defense in depth). `getMemberRole` is now custom-role-aware: it consults `org_roles` when the literal name isn't one of the 3 built-ins and propagates the inherited rank to `requireRole`. **5 high-value routes migrated** to declare `permission:` so per-org overrides actually bite: `POST /workflows/save` (`workflows.write`), `POST /dlq/replay` (`dlq.replay`), `POST /members/role` (`members.role_set`), `POST /org/config` (`org.config.write`), `POST /run/cancel` (`runs.cancel`). The other 37 routes stay role-only in v1 (documented as the v1 carve-out; mechanical migration is a v2 follow-up). **Admin self-lockout guard**: when the built-in `admin` override is saved, the server force-includes `org.permissions.write` + `members.write` (from `mandatoryAdminPermissions()`) and audits the coerced floor in `metadata.coerced`. Custom roles with `inheritsFrom: "admin"` are NOT auto-coerced — operator can create narrower admin-rank roles. **Delete-with-members rejection**: deleting a custom role with active members returns 409 with `{membersAffected, code: "role_in_use"}`. Built-ins return 400 on delete; DELETE on a built-in NAME means "revert the override" (cleared via `org.permissions.override_cleared` audit). Five new admin routes (`GET /org/permissions/catalog`, `GET /org/roles`, `POST /org/roles`, `POST /org/roles/:name`, `DELETE /org/roles/:name`). `/members/invite` + `/members/role` updated to validate role name against the org's role set (built-ins OR custom). New web admin panel `PermissionGrantsPanel.tsx` mounted in `OperationsPanel` — renders per-role accordions with checkbox grids grouped by permission category, an "Add custom role" form with inheritsFrom dropdown + permission checkboxes, and a "Revert to defaults" button for built-in overrides. `MembersPanel.tsx` now sources the role dropdown from `/org/roles` (dynamic list) so admins can invite users directly into custom roles. Five new audit actions: `org.permissions.override_set` / `_cleared`, `org.role.created` / `_updated` / `_deleted`. Tests: `permission-catalog.test.ts` (8 cases — closure, defaults coverage, unique keys, mandatory-admin floor, isPermission + assertPermissionsValid), `permissions-helpers.test.ts` (13 cases — resolveMemberRole built-in + custom + stale + dev-headers fallback, getEffectivePermissions override + default + filter, requirePermission happy + reject paths, requireRole with custom rank inheritance), `roles-routes.test.ts` (15 cases — catalog list / list roles default + with override + custom / create with name validation / create defaults-to-viewer / create-invalid-permission / create-rejects-builtin-name / coerce admin floor on override / inheritsFrom immutable on built-ins / 404 on missing custom / delete custom + audit / delete-with-members 409 / DELETE on built-in reverts override / DELETE on built-in with no override 404), `PermissionGrantsPanel.test.tsx` (6 cases — render built-ins, render custom, mandatory-admin checkbox disabled, create form POSTs, save button POSTs, revert with confirm), `MembersPanel.test.tsx` (2 cases — dynamic role dropdown, invite into custom role). API test count: 394 → 430 (+36); web: +8. |

(1) **Stable error-code envelope:** new `apps/api/src/error-codes.ts` exports a closed `ApiErrorCode` union (15 codes — `email_required`, `email_invalid`, `invitation_pending_exists`, `member_exists`, `member_not_found`, `role_in_use`, `role_already_exists`, `role_not_found`, `mcp_connection_not_found`, `mcp_tool_not_found`, `mcp_connection_duplicate`, `dlq_not_found`, `dlq_field_required`, `workflow_not_found`, `workflow_name_required`) + `errorEnvelope(code, message, params?)` helper returning the canonical `{ error, code, params? }` shape. 15 highest-impact 4xx envelopes migrated across `members-routes.ts` / `roles-routes.ts` / `mcp-routes.ts` / `dlq-routes.ts` / `workflows-routes.ts`. (3) **Date / number locale sweep:** 8 sites that called `.toLocaleString()` / `.toLocaleDateString()` without an explicit locale now pass `getResolvedLocale()` so a Spanish user sees `5 nov 2026, 14:32` instead of `Nov 5, 2026, 2:32 PM`. Sites: `OperationsPanel.tsx` (token / call counts), `ScimDirectorySettingsPanel.tsx` (lastSyncedAt), `DeadLettersPanel.tsx` (createdAt), `FailureClustersCard.tsx` (cluster timestamps), `RightPanel.tsx` (run createdAt), `WorkflowsDashboard.tsx` (workflow updatedAt), `VersionHistoryPanel.tsx` (version createdAt). `getResolvedLocale` re-exported from `apps/web/src/i18n/index.ts`. AGENTS.md i18n bullet extended to document `tApiError` + `apiErrors.<code>` namespace + date/number locale invariant. Server-side stays locale-blind for error responses (curl / CI callers still get the EN fallback in `error`; the new `code` lets sophisticated clients translate themselves). New tests: `apps/api/src/error-codes.test.ts` (4 cases — envelope shape, params round-trip, primitive union, closed-union narrow check), `server-events.test.ts` extended with 5 `tApiError` cases (translates ES with interpolation, falls back on unknown code, falls back on no code, reads `message` for Error-like, returns empty for null). Parity test stays green (15 new EN keys, 15 new ES keys, matched). 167 → 15 v1 cut of API envelopes is intentional — the other ~150 lower-traffic endpoints stay free-form English in v1 (covers ~95% of toast-visible errors); v2 picks up the long tail. MCP server tool descriptions stay English (protocol convention). Email / PDF / webhook body content stays operator-supplied. AI generation output follows the LLM's response language (already plumbed via `localeFromRequest`). |

Tests cover missing credentials, failed upstream delivery, no raw secret leakage, and successful audit rows. **Summary:** new `POST /reports/run-explain/deliver` route (`editor` role, `"reports.deliver"` rate-limit bucket, default 60/min/org) reuses `buildRunExplainReport` and dispatches through `executeTool` to one of `slack.post` / `github.create_issue` / `webhook.send`. Slack gets a header + summary text truncated below the 3000-char per-block cap (with a Full-report footer when `JANUSLY_PUBLIC_APP_URL` is set); GitHub gets the full Markdown as the issue body; webhook receives the structured `RunExplainReportJson` signed via the existing HMAC-SHA256 path. Audit row `report.run_explain.delivered` is written on success AND failure with `{ destination, format, ok, statusCode, error, latencyMs, runId, workflowId, workflowName, runStatus, credentialName }` (`markdown` for Slack/GitHub, `json` for webhook). The web Send button on each run-history card opens `ReportDeliveryDialog` (destination radio + per-kind args, gated submit, success surfaces the GitHub issue URL).
