Scanner cron `system:auto-healing-scanner` (env `JANUSLY_AUTO_HEALING_SCAN_CRON` default `0 */6 * * *`) walks every org with the tenant flag on, groups recent DLQ rows by signature, and for each cluster with frequency >= 2: runs loop-breaker + budget + idempotency gates, calls `suggestWorkflowPatch` with the type-specific envelope from `patchEnvelopeForNodeType`, merges via `applyConfigPatchToWorkflow` (or the structural variant), enqueues sandbox validation via `replayDeadLetterAsValidation`. Watcher cron `system:auto-healing-watcher` (env `JANUSLY_AUTO_HEALING_WATCH_CRON` default `* * * * *`) polls validating rows, computes the signature-changed defense via `normalizeErrorSignature`, records outcome, and (if `isAutoApplyAllowed` + loop count still in range) replays the failing run against the patched workflow with `decisionActor: "auto"`. Two-hour stale-row sweep auto-declines stuck `validating` rows. Both crons NEVER throw. Four new HTTP routes (`apps/api/src/routes/auto-healing-routes.ts`): `GET /auto-healing/pending`, `GET /auto-healing/:id`, `POST /auto-healing/:id/decide` (CAS-style; surfaces 409 `signature_already_resolved` when racing the watcher's auto-apply; also writes a `recovery_feedback` mirror row so the LLM learns from auto-healing decisions), `POST /auto-healing/scan` (admin-only ad-hoc trigger, rate-limited via the existing `"ai"` bucket). Two new permission keys (`autohealing.read` viewer+, `autohealing.decide` editor+). Web card `apps/web/src/components/AutoHealingPendingCard.tsx` mounted inside the existing DLQ panel alongside `FailureClustersCard`; Apply / Decline buttons call `bumpPlatformVersion()` on mutation. EN+ES i18n parity for 16 new keys. ~30 new tests across engine repo, api scanner (13), api watcher (5), and api routes (7). **Honest non-goals:** no new top-level UI panel (one card inside the existing DLQ panel); no auto-apply by default (zero behavior change for orgs that don't flip both flag pairs on); no retroactive scan (forward-only); no cross-org scan parallelism (sequential walk); no alerting on auto-apply (the `auto_healing.applied` audit row with `decisionActor: "auto"` IS the signal); no new LLM grammar / structured-output schema (reuses `suggestWorkflowPatch`); no shadow production replay (sandbox validation only proves the signature is gone — operator review is the safety net by default). |
| ENG-118 | Harden stdio MCP sandboxing | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Strengthen stdio MCP subprocess isolation without changing URL transport semantics. AC: keep the existing `JANUSLY_MCP_ALLOWED_COMMANDS` plus tenant allowlist and clean-env rebuild; add a production stdio sandbox profile that constrains cwd, filesystem write access, CPU, memory, process lifetime, stdout/stderr byte limits, and log redaction before telemetry; do not route `sse` or `http` transports through this subprocess sandbox; hosted MCP tools that require network continue to use the existing URL transport policy. Tests cover host env values being absent, disallowed commands failing closed, allowed command receiving only resolved env refs, timeout/resource-limit termination, stdout/stderr truncation, secret-shaped log redaction, and URL transports remaining unchanged. **Summary:** new pure module `packages/engine/src/mcp-sandbox.ts` exposes `buildSandboxProfile` + `captureRedactedStderr` plus typed errors `McpSandboxCommandNotAllowedError` / `McpSandboxLifetimeExceededError` / `McpSandboxStderrExceededError`. Five composable layers, applied in order: (a) **allowlist re-check at SPAWN time** — defense in depth over the existing registration-time gate, fails closed when the env CSV ∪ tenant CSV union is empty or rejects the command; (b) **ephemeral cwd** via `fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'janusly-mcp-'))` per spawn, removed on `close()`; (c) **lifetime cap** — `setTimeout` arms on first connect (default 600s, range 60s..1h), fires `transport.close()` (which SIGTERMs the child) and after 500ms grace escalates to `process.kill(pid, 'SIGKILL')`; (d) **stderr byte cap + secret-shape redaction** — `captureRedactedStderr` consumes the child's `stderr` stream (SDK exposes it via `stderr: 'pipe'`), buffers raw bytes capped at 64KB default, runs `scrubSecretShapes` at snapshot time so secrets split across chunk boundaries are still redacted, and triggers a child kill when the cap is exceeded; (e) **Linux `ulimit -v` wrap** — only on `process.platform === 'linux'` AND `enforceLinuxUlimit === true`, wraps the original command via `/bin/sh -c 'ulimit -v "$1"; shift; exec "$@"' janusly-mcp-sandbox <vmKb> <orig-cmd> <orig-args...>` (portable POSIX shell, the tag becomes `$0` and never reaches the executed command). The `createStdioMcpClient` factory accepts a new REQUIRED `sandbox` block — typed param change is intentional so dropping the config is a TS error; the `McpClient` interface gains `getSandboxSnapshot()` returning `{ capturedStderr, stderrTruncated, stderrByteCount, lifetimeExceeded, stderrExceeded, enforced }` (null for URL transports). The `mcp_tool` executor (`packages/engine/src/mcp-tool-executor.ts`) gains `stdioSandbox` on the input shape + a new `resolveStdioSandboxConfig(mcpConfig)` helper that resolves the allowlist (tenant ∪ env, tenant-overrides-env precedence matches registration-time), the three caps from `org_configs.mcp.stdio*` with env fallbacks, and the `enforceLinuxUlimit` flag (default `process.platform === 'linux' && NODE_ENV === 'production'`, override via `JANUSLY_MCP_SANDBOX_ENFORCE_LINUX`). The executor maps typed sandbox errors to stable envelope codes `mcp_sandbox_command_rejected` / `mcp_sandbox_lifetime_exceeded` / `mcp_sandbox_stderr_exceeded` exported as `SANDBOX_ERROR_CODES`, surfaces the redacted stderr tail (last 1KB) to the audit callback via new `sandboxFailureCode` + `capturedStderrTail` fields, and the `mcp_tool` node executor writes both the existing `mcp_tool.completed` event AND a new `mcp.sandbox.terminated` event on kill paths so the run timeline distinguishes operator-driven errors from sandbox kills. Three new safe `org_configs` catalog entries (`mcp.stdioMaxLifetimeMs` default 600_000 range 60_000..3_600_000, `mcp.stdioMaxStderrBytes` default 65_536 range 1024..1_048_576, `mcp.stdioMaxVmKb` default 524_288 range 131_072..4_194_304) with env overlays (`JANUSLY_MCP_STDIO_MAX_LIFETIME_MS` / `_STDERR_BYTES` / `_VM_KB`). Discovery flow (`apps/api/src/routes/mcp-routes.ts:runDiscovery`) also resolves the sandbox config via the same helper so registration + re-discovery + per-call invocation all evaluate equivalent allowlists. 13 new test cases in `mcp-sandbox.test.ts` (allowlist re-check, ulimit wrap shape by platform, clamping, byte-accurate stderr cap with secret-shape redaction across chunk boundaries) + 5 new in `mcp-client.test.ts` (sandbox snapshot exposed on stdio transports, spawn-time allowlist drift fails closed, `cwd` + `stderr: 'pipe'` propagated to the SDK constructor, stderr-cap transport closure mapped to `McpSandboxStderrExceededError`) + 9 new in `mcp-tool-executor.test.ts` (`parseCommandAllowlist` + `resolveStdioSandboxConfig` env/tenant precedence + Linux-only enforce default + the executor mapping `McpSandboxCommandNotAllowedError` to `COMMAND_REJECTED`, `McpSandboxLifetimeExceededError` to `LIFETIME_EXCEEDED`, and `McpSandboxStderrExceededError` to `STDERR_EXCEEDED` with redacted stderr tail flowing through to the audit callback). URL transports (`sse` / `http`) untouched — same `validateHttpTarget` chokepoint, no new ulimit / cwd / stderr layers on them. **v1 carve-outs (natural follow-ups):** native `prlimit(2)` / `setrlimit(2)` binding to avoid the `sh` wrapper, CPU time cap (`ulimit -t`), per-connection sandbox profile JSONB column on `mcp_connections`, macOS `sandbox-exec` profile, Windows job-object resource limits, `firejail` / `bubblewrap` integration for chroot-level isolation, network-namespace isolation, real-time stderr streaming to the run timeline. |
| ENG-119 | Add targeted Replay Lab forks | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Extend the shipped Replay Lab from whole-run sandbox replay to selected-node forks. AC: add `POST /runs/replay-lab/fork` or extend the existing route with a closed `mode` field; request includes `sourceRunId`, `forkNodeId`, and optional bounded input/context overrides; route verifies source run ownership, rejects nested lab runs, validates `forkNodeId` exists in the source workflow, creates a `replayMode="validation"` run, clones only completed predecessor state needed by the selected node, starts the selected node pending with the override, marks downstream nodes pending, and records a distinct `replay_lab.fork_started` audit/event payload; `GET /runs/compare` can compare base vs fork. Web UI adds an "Open selected node in Replay Lab" path with override editor and EN/ES copy. Tests cover predecessor-only cloning, no successor-state leakage, dry-run write skips, override size limits, invalid node 422, cross-org 404/403 behavior, and comparison rendering. **Summary:** v1 substrate (engine adapter + route + adapter tests) shipped first, v1.1 UI follow-up closed the carve-out. Engine adapter `replayRunAsValidationFork` in `packages/engine/src/adapters/replay-lab.ts` (new export alongside existing `replayRunAsValidation`); pure helpers `computePredecessors` + `computeSuccessors` compute the ancestor and downstream sets through the workflow's edges; the adapter validates `forkNodeId` exists, validates every predecessor terminated `succeeded` in the source run (strict v1 policy — v1.1 may relax with an opt-in), and writes the new `runs` row + N `run_nodes` rows + a `run.started.replay-lab-fork` event in one DB transaction (predecessors cloned as `status='succeeded'` with `stateJson` copied from the source, fork node `pending` with `{ input: inputOverride }` seeded, downstream closure `pending` with empty state, sibling/disconnected nodes `skipped` with `reason: "outside_replay_fork"` so the runtime never executes branches outside the targeted fork). New route `POST /runs/replay-lab/fork` (editor + `"ai"` rate-limit bucket) in `apps/api/src/routes/runs-routes.ts` validates `sourceRunId` + `forkNodeId` + `inputOverride` (64 KiB cap), org-scopes the source run via `eq(runs.id) AND eq(runs.orgId, auth.orgId)` (404 on cross-org), rejects nested labs (400 `nested_replay_lab`), resolves the workflow snapshot from `workflow_versions.dagJson` or `runs.inputJson.workflow`, calls the adapter, maps discriminated errors (`fork_node_not_found`, `predecessor_not_succeeded`) to HTTP 422, and audits `replay_lab.fork_started` with `{ replayRunId, forkNodeId, predecessorCount, hasOverride }`. The `parentRunId` and `parentNodeId` columns on the `runs` table (already shipped) start being populated together for forks — `parentNodeId` was previously always null. The existing `GET /runs/compare` endpoint requires no changes — it generically compares two runs by id regardless of whether the second is a whole-run replay or a fork. Comprehensive adapter test suite in `packages/engine/src/adapters/replay-lab-fork.test.ts` covering: `computePredecessors` + `computeSuccessors` on linear/diamond/isolated graphs, happy-path predecessor cloning (linear + diamond), override seeding, empty-state seeding when no override, disconnected fork node with out-of-scope nodes skipped, `fork_node_not_found` rejection, `predecessor_not_succeeded` rejection (both failed-predecessor and missing-predecessor variants), no-DB-writes-on-validation-failure invariant, only-fork-node-queued invariant. **v1.1 UI close:** added `apps/web/src/components/ReplayLabForkDialog.tsx` — a textarea-based JSON override modal that mirrors `ReplayLabDialog`'s frame, POSTs `/runs/replay-lab/fork`, and on success calls `setRunId` + `bumpPlatformVersion` so the run timeline switches to the new fork run automatically. `RunsPanel` in `RightPanel.tsx` gains a "Fork in Replay Lab" section listing succeeded + failed terminal nodes when the active run is non-replay + terminal; each row opens the dialog with the chosen `forkNodeId`. 22 new i18n keys (16 `replayLab.fork.*` + 6 `apiErrors.*` for the route's typed codes — `nested_replay_lab`, `fork_node_not_found`, `predecessor_not_succeeded`, `override_too_large`, `no_workflow_snapshot`, `invalid_snapshot`) ship in both EN and ES locales with Spanish translations honouring the AGENTS.md no-Spanglish rule. Vitest jsdom tests in `apps/web/src/components/ReplayLabForkDialog.test.tsx` cover idle render, submit without override (POST body without `inputOverride` field), submit with valid JSON override, submit with invalid JSON (inline error, no POST), typed-error path (`ApiError` with `code: fork_node_not_found` resolves through `tApiError` catalog), and unmount-mid-POST safety (the `aliveRef` guard prevents stale `setRunId` after the dialog tears down). Reuses existing `run-input-dialog*` CSS classes — no `index.css` additions. **Still-deferred follow-ups (natural future tickets, not gaps in this slice):** polished schema-aware override form (per-input fields driven by the node's input schema, similar to the `human_form` renderer), `contextOverrides` surface (override upstream node outputs that downstream `{{nodeId.X}}` references resolve to), DAG fork-aware visualization in the comparison panel, and cluster-fork (one fork point with N replays of N override variants). **Spanglish cleanup colateral** also landed in this commit per a new project-wide rule (no Spanglish in Spanish content; the AGENTS.md "No Spanglish in Spanish content" bullet documents the canonical replacement table): ~14 occurrences across `docs/marketing/narrative.md` (`bindeado`/`bindear`/`patched` → `asignado`/`asignar`/`parchado` + Spanish "ya shipped" → "ya en producción"), `docs/marketing/icp.md` (`shippe*` × 5 + `chequear` × 1 → `lanzar`/`publicar`/`revisar`), `docs/marketing/pricing.md` (`shippe*` × 3 + `trackea` × 1 → `publicar`/`lanzar`/`se mide`), `docs/marketing/competitive-positioning.md` (1 preamble fix), `docs/marketing/private-beta-playbook.md` (12 fixes — `shippe*`/`loggea*`/`bindea*`/`parsear`/`sávalo`/`workflow patched`), `docs/marketing/landing-page.md` (`replayearse` → `reproducirse`). All replacements per the canonical table in the plan; English `(shipped)` tags + English code comments stay intact (those are correct English, not Spanglish). |
| ENG-120 | Add MTTR and value dashboard | Shipped | P2 | 4 | Turn the private-beta/value thesis into an operator-facing dashboard without overclaiming financial precision. Depends on ENG-093 for real customer baseline data when possible. AC: add a Recovery/value dashboard that shows MTTR before/after recovery, recovery success rate, replay rate, failed-run clusters resolved, AI spend from `usage_events`, and estimated manual triage hours saved; dollar savings are configurable assumptions (for example hourly cost and minutes saved per recovered failure) and labeled as estimates, not hard accounting; every query is tenant-scoped and bounded by a time window; exports use existing report/download helpers where practical. Tests cover aggregation windows, null cost handling, estimate formula labels, org isolation, 1M-row-style query bounds via indexes or query shape, EN/ES UI parity, and private-beta data absence fallback. **Summary:** ships the visualization layer over the existing recovery-metrics rollup — no new server-side collection. **3 new `org_configs` keys** under a new `value` category (`value.hourlyCost` default 50 range 0..2000, `value.minutesSavedPerRecovery` default 30 range 0..480, `value.baselineMttrSeconds` default 0 range 0..86400) + the matching `OrgConfigSnapshot.value` branch. **One new repo query** `queryFailureClustersResolved(orgId, since)` in `packages/data/src/recoveryMetricsRepo.ts` joins `dead_letters` with status in `{replayed, resolved}`, derives the signature via `normalizeErrorSignature`, returns distinct cluster count + total entries. Same 10k-row cap as other metrics queries. **Engine `composeRecoveryMetrics` extended** with `clustersResolved` metric + `valueEstimate { hoursSaved, dollarSaved, mttrDeltaSeconds, assumptions }` block + a pure `estimateValueSavings` helper exported for the export route. The formula `clustersResolved × minutesSavedPerRecovery / 60 × hourlyCost = dollarSaved` is computed server-side; `baselineMttrSeconds === 0` is the sentinel for "operator has not measured a pre-Janusly baseline yet" → mttrDelta returns null and the UI renders "Awaiting private-beta data". **`/recovery/metrics` extended** — pulls the org-config value snapshot in parallel with the metrics signals and passes it into the composer. Same URL, same role gate, additive fields. Clients that don't read the new fields keep working byte-for-byte. **NEW `GET /reports/value-dashboard?windowDays=N&format=markdown|json`** route mirrors `/reports/run-explain` (same `Content-Disposition` + `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` chokepoint, same audit shape `report.value_dashboard.exported`). Markdown export header carries "Estimate based on operator-supplied assumptions" + writes the formula out plainly so a CFO reading the artefact knows exactly how every number was derived. **Web `ValueDashboardSection.tsx`** mounted inside the existing `RecoveryCenterPanel` grid — NOT a new top-level panel, NOT a new sidebar entry, same additive-mount pattern auto-healing's pending card used. MTTR before/after pair + clusters card + hours-saved + dollar-savings (Intl.NumberFormat USD, locale-aware via `getResolvedLocale`). Every dollar/hour surface carries an "estimate" badge + tooltip linking to the assumptions config. Export buttons trigger `downloadFromApi`. **16 EN+ES i18n keys** under `recoveryCenter.value.*` (parity-gated). **~25 new test cases** across data orgConfig (8), engine recovery-metrics (10), web component (5), plus updated mcp-routes fixtures with the new `value: {} as never` snapshot branch. **Honest non-goals:** no charting library (no recharts / visx / d3 — the lockdown stays), no multi-currency (USD only, locale-aware formatting), no time-series buckets (per-window aggregates only), no automated baseline-MTTR capture (operator-supplied via config; ENG-093 may surface real numbers), no CSV export, no Slack/email delivery, no retroactive metric backfill, no new audit chokepoint. |
| ENG-121 | Surface rate-limit degradation | Shipped | P2 | — | Address Redis limiter outage visibility while preserving the documented fail-open posture. AC: keep `apps/api/src/rate-limit.ts` behavior fail-open on Redis errors; add structured warning telemetry/counters for limiter degradation by bucket and org, a health/readiness signal consumed by Operations or AI health UI, and operator copy that distinguishes "traffic is allowed under degraded limiter" from "AI budget exceeded"; AI budget guards remain the cost-control layer, not a hidden fail-closed route policy, and Redis outages must not become route-level 429/503 failures. Tests cover Redis error path still allowing requests, warn metadata shape, health signal clearing after recovery, no duplicate noisy events in tight loops, and no regression to in-memory enforcement as the primary limiter. **Summary:** closes the observability gap on the limiter's fail-open path WITHOUT touching the fail-open posture. **`createRateLimiter` gains an optional second `hooks` arg** (`onRedisError` / `onRedisSuccess`) wrapped in a `fireHook` try/catch that catches both sync throws and async rejections so a tracker bug NEVER breaks the fail-open invariant. **NEW `packages/data/src/rate-limit-degradation.ts`** owns the in-memory per-process tracker (`Map<string, DegradationState>`) + the audit chokepoint. `recordRateLimiterError(bucket, key, error)` increments the counter and writes a `(bucket, day)`-deduped `rate_limit.degraded` audit row with `orgId: "system"` sentinel (mirrors the established global-cron convention from memory-retention + auto-healing). `recordRateLimiterRecovery(bucket)` clears the in-memory state on the next successful round-trip and writes a one-shot `rate_limit.recovered` audit. DB-level dedup query (`SELECT … WHERE action='rate_limit.degraded' AND metadata->>'bucket' = ? AND created_at >= today_utc`) guards against multi-replica spam — the in-memory dedup already covers the single-replica case. **Two-tier health snapshot:** `getRateLimiterPublicHealth()` (public-safe bucket label / errorCount / firstObservedAt / lastObservedAt only; tenant-chosen MCP aliases/tool names, Redis errors, and org keys withheld) for the open `GET /health` block; `getRateLimiterAdminHealth()` (full — raw bucket plus `lastError` + `lastObservedKey`) for the new admin `GET /system/rate-limiter` route. The public truncation is the load-bearing reason the snapshot can ride on the unauthenticated `/health` route — Redis error strings can leak hostnames / connection metadata. **Production hooks wired** at both `apps/api/src/rate-limit.ts` and `packages/engine/src/rate-limit-redis.ts` so API + worker replicas feed their own process-local snapshots. **`GET /health` extended additively** with the `rateLimiter` block — `body.ok: true` still anchors any orchestrator probe that reads only that field; clients reading the new field gain the degraded signal. **NEW `GET /system/rate-limiter` (admin)** exposes the full snapshot. **NEW `RateLimiterStatusChip` mounted inside `OperationsPanel`** — green chip when healthy, amber chip with bucket-count tooltip when degraded. Copy distinguishes explicitly: "Rate limiter degraded — N bucket(s) failing open. Traffic still allowed." vs `BudgetBlockedBanner`'s "AI workflow budget exceeded — blocked." Operators reading both surfaces should never confuse a Redis blip with a hard budget block. **6 new EN+ES i18n keys** under `operations.rateLimiter.*` (parity-gated; Spanish honours the no-Spanglish rule via `dejando pasar el tráfico sin aplicar el límite`). **2 new audit actions** (`rate_limit.degraded` / `rate_limit.recovered`). **~21 new test cases** across data tracker (9), data limiter hook wiring (7), api health-routes (5), web Operations chip (2). **Honest non-goals:** no fail-closed variant, no per-org dedup on the audit (Redis outages are global infrastructure events), no in-memory fallback limiter, no Prometheus exporter (counter is in-memory; a future ticket can plug `packages/engine/src/observability/prometheus.ts`), no top-level banner (a fail-open degradation is non-blocking), no alert routing, no reset endpoint. |
| ENG-126 | Add credential health preflight | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2 as the first Wave A starter with no upstream Wave A dependency. AC: surface credential and tool issues BEFORE a run fails. `getCredentialHealth(orgId)` data helper returns each credential's `{ secretRefPresent, lastUsedAt, lastErrorAt, lastErrorMessage (scrubbed via scrubSecretShapes), kind }` plus the workflows that reference it (latest version only); helper joins `usage_events` for last-use via `metadata.credentialName`; new route `GET /credentials/health` (viewer, `credentials.read` permission); `checkWorkflowReadiness` gains a `credential_missing` warn rule for every referenced credential whose env-var resolves to undefined; Operations UI gets a "Credential health" card listing problems with explicit "Used by N workflow(s)" labels — the env-var name is NEVER echoed (mirrors `executeTool`'s `credential secret missing for <name>` envelope); MCP connections show `status: pending|active|failed|disabled` plus per-tool enabled count plus a stale-discovery sentinel (`staleDays`). Tests cover env-name redaction, multi-tenant scope, disabled MCP connection visibility, the readiness rule firing on each ref class (tool / MCP), and the empty-state fallback. **Summary:** ships observability without rotation. **NEW `packages/data/src/credentialHealthRepo.ts`** with `getCredentialHealth(orgId, resolver)` running three bulk queries (credentials list, `usage_events` aggregate filtered on `metadata ? 'credentialName'`, latest-version-per-workflow scan with defensive `LIMIT 500`). `lastErrorMessage` passes through `scrubSecretShapes` before return. `SecretRefResolver` is the SOLE chokepoint that touches `process.env`; production wires `apps/api/src/readiness-helpers.ts:productionSecretRefResolver`. MCP block reuses `listConnections` + `listToolDescriptors` (mirrors `GET /mcp/connections` enrichment); surfaces `envRefsMissingKeys` as the operator-chosen KEYS (NOT env var names). **`apps/api/src/readiness-helpers.ts`** extended with `getCredentialReadinessIssues(orgId, workflow, resolver)` sidecar — engine's pure-sync `checkWorkflowReadiness` stays unchanged; the sidecar pattern mirrors `checkRollbackAvailability`. Wired into all four call sites (`/workflows/readiness`, `/workflows/save`, `/workflows/health/delta`, `/start`). New permission keys `credentials.read` (viewer-default) + `credentials.write` (admin-default) under a new `credentials` category; `POST /credentials` gains the new permission alongside its existing `role: admin` (defense-in-depth). **`apps/web/src/components/CredentialHealthCard.tsx`** mounted between `PermissionGrantsPanel` and `McpConnectionsPanel` in `OperationsPanel`. Severity-tinted rows (red on missing secret / failed MCP, amber on recent error / stale discovery / missing env refs, green otherwise). v1 carries no admin actions — copy points to the existing MCP panel for rediscover. 20 new EN+ES i18n keys under `operations.credentialHealth.*` + `readiness.credential_missing` (parity-gated; Spanish honours the no-Spanglish rule). ~17 new test cases (repo 8 + readiness sidecar 5 + route 3 + web card 4). **Honest non-goals:** no automatic credential rotation; no automatic MCP rediscover (operator triggers via existing panel); no live `listTools()` for descriptor drift (staleness sentinel only); no new credential kind; no alert delivery (that's ENG-122). |
| ENG-125 | Add workflow SLO policy engine | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2 as the foundational upstream blocker for ENG-122 (recovery alerting). AC: workflows can declare reliability SLOs that drive future alerts. Extend `workflow_versions` with an `slo_json` column (nullable); SLO evaluation reuses `computeWorkflowHealth` signals (already shipped) without duplicate queries — health math returns SLO breach booleans alongside scores; new route `POST /workflows/:id/slo` (admin) writes the SLO and audits `workflow.slo.set`; UI surfaces an SLO breach pill on the health badge; the save chokepoint carries the SLO forward to every subsequent version so the contract is automatic; rollback carries the SLO forward too (the latest reliability contract isn't undone by a DAG rollback). Tests cover window bounds, null SLO equals no-policy, threshold changes, breach detection, and tenant scope. **Summary:** zero-overhead contract layer over the existing health rollup. **NEW `packages/shared/src/workflow-slo.ts`** (zero-runtime-dep) exports `WorkflowSloSchema` (closed-key Zod 4 — `successRatePercent` 0..100 nullable / `mttrSeconds` int ≥0 nullable / `p95DurationMs` int ≥0 nullable / `budgetBlocksPerWindow` int ≥0 nullable / `stuckWaitingNodesMax` int ≥0 nullable / `windowDays` literal union 7\|14\|30) and the pure `evaluateWorkflowSlo(slo, signals)` helper. Sample-size guard: success-rate + p95 breaches do not fire when `totalRuns < MIN_SAMPLE_SIZE = 5` (mirrors `computeWorkflowHealth`'s neutral-default rule). **v1 evaluates `successRatePercent` + `p95DurationMs` against the already-shipped `HealthSignals` shape;** the other three threshold fields (`mttrSeconds`, `budgetBlocksPerWindow`, `stuckWaitingNodesMax`) persist in the schema for forward-compat but their breach flags always return `false` in v1. This keeps the column stable so a future signal-wiring change does not need a migration. **Migration** `packages/db/migrations/20260522132352_round_natasha_romanoff/migration.sql` adds the nullable `slo_json` jsonb column on `workflow_versions`. **NEW `packages/data/src/workflowSloRepo.ts`** is the data chokepoint — `getWorkflowSlo(orgId, workflowId)` reads the latest version's `slo_json`, `setWorkflowSlo(orgId, workflowId, slo)` updates the latest version in place and returns the previous SLO for the audit row. Multi-tenant scope on every query via `eq(workflow_versions.orgId, orgId)`. **Save chokepoint** in `apps/api/src/workflows-save.ts` now reads the prior latest's `sloJson` BEFORE inserting the new version row and copies it forward — operators don't re-declare the SLO on every workflow edit. **Rollback chokepoint** in `packages/data/src/workflowRollbackRepo.ts` mirrors the same carry-forward — rolling back the DAG does not roll back the reliability contract. **Engine integration** in `packages/engine/src/workflow-health.ts` accepts an optional `slo?: WorkflowSlo | null` input and attaches the resolved `{ slo, breaches }` block as `HealthScore.slo`. The block is null when no SLO is declared; the numeric score is unchanged whether or not SLO is supplied (the contract is orthogonal to the rollup). **NEW route `POST /workflows/:id/slo`** in `apps/api/src/routes/workflows-routes.ts` is admin-only via the route registry's declarative `role: "admin"` gate; the matcher accepts `/workflows/<id>/slo` and explicitly rejects unrelated suffixes. Body validated via `z.object({ slo: WorkflowSloSchema.nullable() })` — `null` clears the declaration. Audits `workflow.slo.set` with `{ slo, previousSlo, versionId }` metadata via the existing `audit` chokepoint (which routes through `safePersistPayload` for the standard redaction posture). `/workflows/health` extended additively to load the SLO via `getWorkflowSlo` in parallel with `collectHealthSignals` and pass it into `computeWorkflowHealth`; the delta route does the same on both the before-side and after-side computations (same SLO applies to both sides — the operator's current contract is what they expect the workflow to satisfy regardless of the historical window). **Web UI** extends `apps/web/src/components/WorkflowHealthBadge.tsx` with a red "SLO breach" pill rendered when `slo?.breaches.anyBreach === true`. The expanded badge panel adds an SLO section listing each declared metric (null thresholds are skipped) with a per-metric pass/breach color tint. **NEW `apps/web/src/components/WorkflowSloPanel.tsx`** mounted inside the Inspector right panel next to `VersionHistoryPanel` — three bounded inputs (success-rate %, p95 duration ms, evaluation window 7\|14\|30) plus a Clear button. The two unsupported-in-v1 metrics intentionally do NOT render input fields; the helper-text under the form documents `Accepted but not yet evaluated in v1.` Submit calls `POST /workflows/:id/slo` and bumps `platformVersion` on success so the badge refetches without a reload. Non-admins see the form but the server's `role: "admin"` gate rejects the write through the existing 403 toast envelope. **EN+ES i18n** ships ~22 new parity-gated keys under `badges.slo.*` + `workflowSlo.*`; the parity test in `apps/web/src/i18n/parity.test.ts` gates the build. **~26 new test cases** across shared (14 — `WorkflowSloSchema` + `evaluateWorkflowSlo`), data (6 — repo unit), engine (3 — health integration), api (7 — route shape + body validation + audit emission + null-clear), web (4 — pill render + breakdown expansion). **Honest non-goals:** no breach event emission to a subscriber bus (ENG-122 will land the alerting plumbing — v1 just returns breach booleans + the audit row as the persistence trail); no automatic SLO recommendations from history (that's ENG-141 anomaly detection); no dedicated "SLO change log" — the `workflow.slo.set` audit rows ARE the change log; no multi-window simultaneous evaluation (one `windowDays` at a time); no org-default SLO template (operator declares per workflow); no MTTR / budget-block / stuck-waiting-node breach evaluation in v1 (the threshold fields persist for forward-compat, but the v1 evaluator returns false on all three until the corresponding signals are wired); the breach booleans do NOT change the numeric health score — they are a separate orthogonal contract surface. |
| ENG-122 | Add recovery alerting policies | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2. Both upstream blockers (ENG-125 SLO, ENG-126 credential health) shipped before this row; ENG-125's Summary cites the handoff verbatim ("ENG-122 will land the alerting plumbing"). AC: org-scoped alerting policies fan events into Slack / webhook / email / GitHub via the existing tool chokepoints. New `alert_policies` table keyed by `(orgId, name)` with closed-enum `trigger` (`dlq.entry_created` \| `failure_cluster.threshold` \| `budget.blocked` \| `limiter.degraded` \| `workflow.slo_breach` \| `approval.stalled`), bounded `parameters` jsonb (per-trigger Zod schemas), `channels` jsonb (array of `{ destination, credentialName, params? }` referencing existing credentials — no new credential kind), `cooldownSeconds`, `enabled`. New `alert_dispatches` table backs both cooldown lookup and the `GET /alerts/recent` operator feed. Five new audit actions (`alert.policy.created/updated/deleted/triggered/suppressed`). Two new permission keys (`alerts.read` viewer+, `alerts.write` admin). Five new routes (`POST/GET/POST :id/DELETE /alerts/policies`, `GET /alerts/recent`). Three event-driven hooks (DLQ adapter, budget-gate, rate-limit-degradation) + a state-driven scanner (failure clusters, SLO breaches, stalled approvals) registered as `system:alerts-scanner` BullMQ cron (default `*/2 * * * *`, env-tunable). Dedupe per `(orgId, policyId, dedupeKey)` within `cooldownSeconds`; cooldown skips only fire the suppressed audit row (no `alert_dispatches` row, keeps the table compact). The dispatcher lives in `packages/engine/src/alerts/` (NOT `apps/api`) so both API and worker processes can register it — DLQ inserts in the worker fire alerts immediately via the DI seam. Tests cover Zod schemas, glob matcher, message composer, dedupe-key per trigger, matchesPolicyFilter per trigger, DI seam swallow-on-error, web list + form render + recent-card severity tint. **Summary:** zero-LLM alerting layer over the existing tool chokepoints. **NEW shared schemas** at `packages/shared/src/alert-policy.ts` + `alert-pattern.ts` (glob matcher, NO regex / NO ReDoS surface) + `alert-message.ts` (deterministic Markdown template — NOT a prompt). **NEW DB schema** adds `alert_policies` + `alert_dispatches` via `packages/db/migrations/20260522182937_mighty_jetstream`. **NEW data repos** `packages/data/src/alertPoliciesRepo.ts` (CRUD + `getEnabledPoliciesByTrigger`), `alertDispatchesRepo.ts` (cooldown lookup + cursor-paginated feed), `alert-dispatch.ts` (DI seam mirroring `setUsageRecorder`). **NEW engine dispatcher** `packages/engine/src/alerts/dispatcher.ts` matches policies, applies per-trigger filter (`errorSignaturePattern` glob, `workflowIds` whitelist, `minFrequency` threshold, `metricNames` overlap, `buckets` filter, `scope` selector), checks cooldown, fans across channels via `executeTool` (same chokepoint as `/reports/run-explain/deliver` — SSRF / body-cap / usage / audit / safe-persist all apply for free), writes dispatch row + `alert.policy.triggered` audit. Cooldown skips write `alert.policy.suppressed` audit only. Never throws — per-channel failures land as `{ ok: false, error, latencyMs }`. **NEW api scanner** `apps/api/src/alerts/scanner.ts` + bootstrap registered behind `JANUSLY_ALERTS_ENABLED=true` env gate. **NEW routes** `apps/api/src/routes/alerts-routes.ts` with body validation through `AlertPolicyConfigSchema` + `validateAlertPolicyConfig`; 422 envelope carries structured per-channel errors for the web's inline form messages. **Three line-insertion hooks**: DLQ adapter writes `recordAlertEvent({ trigger: "dlq.entry_created", ... })` after `db.insert(deadLetters)`; `budget-gate.ts` after the `billing.budget.exceeded` audit; `rate-limit-degradation.ts` after `rate_limit.degraded` audit (uses `orgId: "system"` sentinel — operator-side only). **NEW web** `AlertPoliciesPanel.tsx` (admin CRUD with trigger-swap parameters subform + credential dropdown filtered by destination kind + inline Edit button per row that opens the form prefilled via `policyToForm` and POSTs to `:id` instead of creating; Save button copy swaps to "Save changes" when editing; the tenant-org filter hides the `limiter.degraded` trigger from the trigger dropdown for non-`system` orgs since alerts on that trigger only fire for the SaaS operator) + `RecentAlertsCard.tsx` (50-row feed with cobalt/amber severity tint, expand for channel results) mounted inside `OperationsPanel`. **~52 new EN+ES i18n keys** under `alerts.*` (parity-gated, no Spanglish). **~30 new tests** across shared (alert-policy + alert-pattern + alert-message), data (DI seam), engine (matchesPolicyFilter + dedupe-key), web (panel + recent card). Total: 2017+ tests still green. **Honest non-goals:** no alert message templates beyond the Markdown default (operator-customisable templates are v2); no escalation chains; no Slack interactive buttons (ENG-143 owns that); no native PagerDuty / Opsgenie (use `webhook.send`); no snooze (toggle `enabled: false`); no `recovery_item.created` trigger (ENG-123 doesn't exist yet — one-line add when it lands); no preview / test-send button; no PATCH HTTP verb (the codebase's `Route` type is closed to GET/POST/DELETE — `POST /alerts/policies/:id` does updates); 14 dedicated `*.browser.test.tsx` Vitest browser-mode scenarios + 7 Claude_Preview screenshots are a follow-up step before the human commits (jsdom + parity tests pass; the operator runs `pnpm dev` and captures screenshots for the human review pass). |
| ENG-123 | Add recovery ownership workflow | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2. Continúa naturalmente a ENG-122 — convierte cada DLQ row abierto en un incident trackeable con owner / severity p1-p4 / SLA timer / status lifecycle / comments append-only / resolutionReason. Cierra el honest non-goal de ENG-122 ("no `recovery_item.created` trigger") añadiendo dos triggers nuevos al contrato cerrado: `recovery_item.created` (event-driven, dispara cuando se crea un item) y `recovery_item.sla_breached` (state-driven, el scanner periódico de ENG-122 lo evalúa cada 2 min sobre `recovery_items WHERE status != 'resolved' AND sla_target_at <= now()`). AC: new `recovery_items` table keyed por `(orgId, deadLetterId)` único — un incident por DLQ row, incluso cuando un cluster tiene varios miembros. Lifecycle status closed-enum (`open` → `acknowledged` → `in_progress` → `waiting_external` → `resolved`, con `reopened` para vueltas). Severity closed-enum (`p1`/`p2`/`p3`/`p4`) con SLA defaults por nivel (1h/4h/1d/7d) overridable por-item al asignar. ResolutionReason closed-enum (`fixed_by_patch`/`rolled_back`/`upstream_fixed`/`accepted_loss`/`not_a_bug`/`sandbox_replay_succeeded`). 10 rutas nuevas en el route registry (`GET /recovery/items` paginado + `GET /recovery/items/:id` + 8 transition POSTs: `acknowledge`/`in-progress`/`waiting-external`/`escalate`/`assign`/`resolve`/`reopen`/`comment`), todas `permission: "recovery.write"` (ya existía, descripción ampliada). 9 audit actions nuevas (`recovery.item.created/acknowledged/in_progress/waiting_external/assigned/escalated/resolved/reopened/commented`); cada mutación lleva `{before, after}` para traceability. CAS-style transitions (mirror de `recordDecision` en `autoHealingRepo.ts`) — concurrent operator-click vs auto-apply pierde con 409 + `{code: "recovery_item_transition_invalid", currentStatus}`. Cluster-recover-all hace fan-out: cada DLQ row aceptado crea (idempotentemente) un recovery_item antes del replay y lo auto-cierra después con `resolutionReason: sandbox_replay_succeeded`. `/dlq/replay` + `/dlq/resolve` hacen el mismo auto-close. **Summary:** columna vertebral operacional sobre Recovery Center. **NEW shared schemas** en `packages/shared/src/recovery-item.ts` (zero-runtime-dep) — closed enums, SLA defaults (`SLA_SECONDS_BY_SEVERITY`), Zod body schemas por route, helper `defaultSlaTargetAt`, helper `isSeverityEscalation`. **NEW DB schema** añade `recovery_items` table vía `packages/db/migrations/20260522213531_bizarre_zombie/` con 3 índices: unique `(orgId, deadLetterId)` para idempotencia del cluster-apply, `(orgId, status, sla_target_at)` para el SLA-breach scanner, `(orgId, owner)` para "mis incidents". **NEW data repo** `packages/data/src/recoveryItemsRepo.ts` con CRUD + 6 transition functions CAS-style + `listOpenItemsWithBreachedSla` para el scanner + `appendCommentToRecoveryItem` read-modify-write con cap MAX_COMMENTS_PER_ITEM=200. **NEW DI seam** `packages/data/src/recovery-item-creator.ts` mirror de `alert-dispatch.ts` — la creación se dispara desde la DLQ adapter via seam (NO import directo a apps/api desde engine, sigue el patrón cross-process establecido por el alert dispatcher). **NEW engine helper** `packages/engine/src/recovery/recovery-item-hook.ts` con `createRecoveryItemForDeadLetter` (idempotente, honora `org_configs.recovery.autoCreateItems` default true, audit inline via `db.insert(auditLogs)` + safePersistPayload chokepoint, dispatch `recovery_item.created` alert) y `autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay` (CAS-loser-safe). Vive en engine para que worker process pueda registrar el mismo creator. **NEW api routes** `apps/api/src/routes/recovery-items-routes.ts` con 10 rutas + body validation via Zod schemas en `@janusly/shared` + 409 con `currentStatus` en CAS-loser + audit con before/after por transition. **DLQ hooks** insertados en 3 sitios: `/dlq/cluster-apply` (createRecoveryItemForDeadLetter + autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay por cada accepted row), `/dlq/replay` single (autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay), `/dlq/resolve` (autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay). **Alert trigger extension** extiende `ALERT_TRIGGERS` con dos valores nuevos cierra el contrato declarado en ENG-122; `matchesPolicyFilter` + `buildDedupeKey` + scanner cada uno con su branch nuevo (scanner reutiliza el cron `*/2 * * * *` existente añadiendo `scanRecoveryItemSlaBreaches`). **Engine event hook** en `packages/engine/src/adapters/dead-letter-queue.ts` captura el `deadLetterId` generado y dispara `recordRecoveryItemCreationEvent` paralelo al alert event. Worker process registra el creator en `worker.ts` para que worker-side DLQ inserts también disparen item creation. **NEW web** `RecoveryItemBadge.tsx` (avatar owner + severity pill cobalt→red por p4→p1 + status chip + SLA timer con bandas verde/amber/red por % de tiempo restante) + `RecoveryItemDrawer.tsx` (transition buttons habilitados según current status + resolve sub-form con select del closed-enum + comments append con textarea + escalate sub-buttons por severity restante) montados dentro de `DeadLettersPanel.tsx` (load recovery items en paralelo via `GET /recovery/items?limit=200`, lookup por deadLetterId, badge inyectado en `we-list-row__meta`). **~50 nuevas EN+ES i18n keys** bajo `recoveryItems.*` (parity-gated, no Spanglish — "Acuse de recibo", "En curso", "Esperando externos", "Resuelto" en español, no "shippear/loggear"). **~30 new tests** across shared (recovery-item Zod coverage), data (DI seam swallow + last-write-wins), engine (alert dispatcher branches + dedupe-key + scanner), web (RecoveryItemBadge severity tones + SLA color bands + null-item no-op). Total: 2024+ tests verde. **Honest non-goals (v1 scope):** no team-level owner (owner es userId — asignación grupal queda para v2); no email-on-assign (operador opt-in vía alert policy con `recovery_item.created` + filtro severities); no edit/delete de comments individuales (append-only por diseño); no bulk-assign/bulk-resolve UI fuera del cluster-apply existente; no `runbookMarkdown` per workflow (eso es ENG-139); no mobile-optimized drawer; no SLA auto-escalation (scanner solo emite el evento `recovery_item.sla_breached`, no sube severity automáticamente); no analytics dashboard ("how long do p1s take to resolve on average" es ENG-120); no "Mis incidents abiertos" widget en RecoveryCenterPanel (queda como follow-up — el filter `?owner=me` ya existe en la ruta `GET /recovery/items`, solo falta el componente); no UI validation de que el owner sea un userId real del org (operator-trusted input, 1-line follow-up). |

ES en "tú" register sin Spanglish ("responsables", "guardar", "configúrala"; runbook/Slack/Linear/webhook stay English como anglicismos NOMINALES per AGENTS.md). **~50 nuevos tests** across shared (parseMarkdownBlocks port + nueva cobertura de link tokens + WorkflowMetadataSchema 19 cases), data (workflowMetadataRepo + DI seam swallow), engine (recovery-item-hook severity default applied + fallback + workflow-id-null skip), api (workflow-metadata-routes 10 cases — role/permission, 404, 422 por slack regex + 33-KiB runbook, audit shape), web (SafeMarkdown link safety + WorkflowAboutCard render variants). | ENG-162 | Gate DLQ list endpoint behind viewer role | Shipped | P0 | — | The generic `GET /dlq` route in `apps/api/src/routes/dlq-routes.ts:69` is registered without a `role:` or `permission:` field, so it falls through to the dispatcher's authenticated-only check — a service-token caller (or any logged-in user in dev mode) can read every open dead letter for their org regardless of intent. Sibling routes `GET /dlq/clusters` (line 43) and `GET /dlq/cluster-members` (line 56) already carry `role: "viewer"`. AC: add `role: "viewer"` to the registry entry for `GET /dlq`; an unauthenticated request still returns 401, an authenticated request below `viewer` returns 403, viewer/editor/admin keep working byte-for-byte; one new api-layer test covers each of the three role rejections plus the viewer success path; no other route's role posture changes. **Non-goals:** no rework of the DLQ permission catalog, no rename of existing roles, no migration to `permission:` form (that's the broader follow-up tracked in the v2 permission-migration work). **Summary:** Added `role: "viewer"` to the `GET /dlq` registry entry in `apps/api/src/routes/dlq-routes.ts` so the dispatcher invokes `requireRole(...)` symmetric with the two sibling list routes. New `apps/api/src/routes/dlq-routes.test.ts` (3 declarative pins on the registry shape + 4 dispatcher integration cases via `createApiServer`: 401 unauth, 403 below viewer, 200 viewer, 200 editor byte-for-byte). No permission catalog entry added; no other route's role posture changes. |
| ENG-163 | Block admin self-role-change and self-removal | Shipped | P0 | — | `POST /members/role` and `DELETE /members` in `apps/api/src/routes/members-routes.ts:99-127` let an admin pass their own `userId` in the body and either demote themselves to viewer or remove themselves from the org entirely. Both produce an unrecoverable lock-out for single-admin orgs (no other admin to escalate the role back) and a cross-admin foot-gun in multi-admin orgs where the actor didn't realize the body was self-referential. AC: when `body.userId === auth.userId`, both handlers return `errorEnvelope("self_membership_modification", "Cannot modify your own membership")` with HTTP 400 BEFORE any DB read; the rejection is audited as `member.self_modification_blocked` with `metadata: { action: "role_set"|"remove", attemptedRole? }` so security review can see attempts; two new EN+ES i18n keys under `apiErrors.self_membership_modification` (parity-gated); 4 new api-layer tests cover (admin self-demote rejected, admin self-remove rejected, admin demoting another admin still works, admin removing another admin still works); no change to non-admin self-leave UX (which the codebase doesn't expose today — handled in the larger "let-me-leave" ticket if/when it lands). **Non-goals:** no new "leave org" surface, no last-admin guard for cross-admin demotion (the existing protection is informally documented but not enforced; that's a sibling ticket), no UI affordance (route-level rejection is enough for v1; the web Members panel already hides the row for the current user). **Summary:** Added a pre-DB self-check to both handlers — when `userId === auth.userId`, the request returns HTTP 400 with `errorEnvelope("self_membership_modification", ...)` and writes a `member.self_modification_blocked` audit row with `metadata.action: "role_set"|"remove"` (plus `attemptedRole` on the role-set path). New code joins the `ApiErrorCode` union; EN+ES catalogs gained the parity-gated `apiErrors.self_membership_modification` key. New `apps/api/src/routes/members-routes.test.ts` with the 4 AC cases (self-demote rejected, self-remove rejected, admin demoting another admin still works, admin removing another admin still works). No permission catalog change; admin-on-admin operations stay byte-for-byte. |
| ENG-164 | Refresh transitive deps with known CVEs | Shipped | P1 | — | `pnpm audit --audit-level moderate` flags moderate-severity CVEs reaching us through transitive deps of `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` and `@supabase/realtime-js`: Hono `<4.12.18` (cache middleware XSS), `ws` (uninitialized memory disclosure), `uuid` (buffer overrun in browser ESM build), `qs`, `ip-address`. None are exploitable through Janusly's own code today (we don't expose Hono routes or browser-side `uuid`), but they appear in supply-chain scans and block enterprise procurement reviews. AC: bump `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` and `@supabase/realtime-js` (or their direct parents in the dep tree) to versions that pin `hono >= 4.12.18`, `ws >= 8.20.1`, `uuid >= 11.1.1`; if upstream hasn't released a clean version, document the residual finding in a new `SECURITY.md` § "Known transitive advisories" with the rationale and revisit cadence; update the CI `dep_audit` job's `--audit-level` from `high` to `moderate` so the next moderate regression fails the build; existing build + test + e2e pass byte-for-byte. **Non-goals:** no auto-merge bot, no Renovate config rewrite (the existing manual cadence is fine for now), no upgrade of pinned production deps (Node 24 / TS 6 / Drizzle 1.0 RC / Zod 4 / React 19 / Vite 8 — those are explicit AGENTS.md baselines), no swap of `@supabase/realtime-js` for an alternative client. **Summary:** 4 of 5 advisories cleared by extending `pnpm-workspace.yaml > overrides` with `hono >= 4.12.18`, `ws >= 8.20.1`, `qs >= 6.15.2`, `ip-address >= 10.2.0` (same surface as the existing `fast-uri` override). `@supabase/supabase-js` realigned to `^2.106.1` across `apps/api` + `apps/web`. The residual `uuid@8.3.2` advisory (`GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq`, peer-locked by `jaeger-client` in the OTel Jaeger exporter) is silenced via the migrated `auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and documented in a new `SECURITY.md` with parent chain, why-it-is-safe-for-us rationale (the `buf` arg vector + browser ESM target don't apply to our server-side OTel path), and monthly revisit cadence. CI `dep_audit` flag flipped `high → moderate`. `pnpm audit --audit-level moderate` now exits 0; lockfile regenerated. |
| ENG-165 | Coalesce platformVersion polling and dedup API requests | Pending | P1 | — | Two coupled web-perf gaps surface as visible jank on terminal-run completion: (1) `bumpPlatformVersion()` in `apps/web/src/store.ts:316` is observed by 20+ panel subscribers as a `useEffect` dep — a single bump fires 8-12 fetches within 50ms (RecoveryCenterPanel, VersionHistoryPanel, McpConnectionsPanel, OperationsPanel, FailureClustersCard, etc.); (2) `apps/web/src/api.ts` has no in-flight request deduplication, so two panels asking for the same endpoint in the same tick fire two network requests. Together they amplify a single mutation into a 12-request waterfall and occasionally trip the per-org Redis rate-limit bucket. AC: add a tiny request-dedup wrapper in `apps/web/src/api.ts` that shares in-flight Promises keyed by `method + path + serialized body` with a 500ms TTL (the response is shared, not cached — the next call after TTL goes to the network); add a debounce/coalesce layer on top of `bumpPlatformVersion` so multiple bumps within a 100ms window collapse to one subscriber notification (Zustand `subscribe` is synchronous — wrap the bumper in a microtask queue or use a generation counter the polling effects compare against); the existing terminal-run flow in `apps/web/src/App.tsx:298-335` keeps working byte-for-byte from the user's perspective (data still refreshes promptly); no fetch is silently dropped if the second caller passed a different AbortController; tests cover the dedup happy path, dedup-with-different-body misses the cache, dedup TTL expiry, coalesced-bump fires exactly one effect, and the polling loop still triggers per its cadence. Measurable target: average network requests per terminal-run event drops from 12 to ≤4 in DevTools network panel. **Non-goals:** no introduction of TanStack Query / SWR (the existing API helper stays the chokepoint), no migration to Server-Sent Events for live updates, no per-endpoint cache TTL tuning (uniform 500ms for v1). |
| ENG-166 | Stabilize React Flow edge styles via CSS classes | Pending | P1 | — | `apps/web/src/App.tsx:365-379` recomputes inline `style` and `markerEnd` objects on every `visibleEdges` render (depended on by `selectedEdgeId`, `nodeStatuses`, and `i18n.language`). React Flow uses identity comparison on edge objects to skip work; the fresh object literals defeat that memoization and every poll tick re-renders the entire canvas. On a 20-edge workflow this costs ~100-200ms per platformVersion bump. The `i18n.language` dep additionally re-runs `visibleNodes` (line 363) on every locale toggle. AC: move edge selection styling from inline `style={{...}}` to CSS classes + `data-selected` attribute on the edge element (`.we-edge--selected { stroke: var(--we-primary); ... }` in `apps/web/src/index.css`); the edge `style` prop on `visibleEdges` becomes a stable constant or omitted; `markerEnd` is computed via a module-scoped lookup table keyed by status, not built per-render; split the node label memoization so the `i18n.language` dep only invalidates label computation, not the full node graph (status + selection memos stay independent); tests cover (no edge re-render when an unrelated node status changes, selection visually applies via the data attribute, language toggle does not invalidate edge memos); React DevTools Profiler trace before/after shows the visibleEdges memo re-runs go from N-per-tick to 1-per-tick on platformVersion bumps. **Non-goals:** no rewrite of the React Flow integration, no migration to a custom canvas, no introduction of `react-compiler` (separate eval). |

Same shape appears in (in priority order) `RunsList`-style panels surfaced inside `OperationsPanel`, the audit-log views surfaced by `PermissionGrantsPanel` / `AuditLogPanel`, and the `FailureClustersCard` sample-rows expansion. | ENG-169 | Add composite + GIN indexes on hot-path tables | Shipped | P1 | — | Three index gaps will hurt as tenant data volume crosses ~100k rows/day. (1) `usage_events` currently has `(orgId, metric)` (`usage_events_org_metric_idx`) but no `createdAt DESC` covering — billing summaries in `packages/data/src/budgetRepo.ts` and the value dashboard in `packages/data/src/recoveryMetricsRepo.ts` filter `(orgId, metric)` and ORDER BY `createdAt DESC` LIMIT N; at scale this degrades from index range scan to scan-then-sort. Add composite `(orgId, metric, createdAt DESC)`. (2) `audit_logs.metadata` is queried via `metadata->>'bucket'` for the rate-limit degradation dedup in `packages/data/src/rate-limit-degradation.ts:285-295` — today this is a full-table scan on every degraded write and will become a thundering-herd hotspot if/when other dedup paths copy the pattern. Add `CREATE INDEX audit_logs_metadata_gin_idx ON audit_logs USING GIN (metadata)` (jsonb_path_ops opclass to keep the index narrow). (3) `auto_healing_runs` `recordDecision` CAS query in `packages/data/src/autoHealingRepo.ts:259-295` filters `(orgId, status='validated')` — adds a partial index `(orgId) WHERE status='validated'` so the watcher loop stays sub-millisecond at scale. AC: one new Drizzle migration generated via `pnpm --filter @janusly/db db:generate` containing all three indexes (use `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` directly in the SQL — Drizzle's `index()` builder emits blocking variant); migration is verified locally with `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on each of the three target queries against a seeded ~100k-row dataset showing the new index is chosen and reduces buffers; existing tests stay green; AGENTS.md "Migrations" bullet gains one new sentence noting the index-only data hot paths so future composite-filter queries reach for the same shape; no application code change beyond the schema/migration. **Non-goals:** no full sweep of every jsonb column (the other GIN-candidate columns — `credentials.metadata`, `usage_events.metadata` — are not queried by `->>` today; add them when the query lands, not speculatively), no BRIN partitioning (still premature). **Summary:** Three indexes landed in migration `20260524204719_calm_norrin_radd/` — composite `usage_events_org_metric_created_idx (orgId, metric, createdAt DESC NULLS LAST)`, GIN `audit_logs_metadata_gin_idx USING gin (metadata jsonb_path_ops)`, and partial `auto_healing_runs_org_validated_idx (orgId) WHERE status='validated'`. Discovery during PHASE 2: drizzle-orm's `migrate()` wraps every migration in `db.transaction(...)`, and Postgres rejects `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` inside a transaction (error 25001) — the AC's "CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY directly in the SQL" path is fundamentally incompatible with drizzle-kit migrate. Pivoted to a two-file pattern: `migration.sql` uses plain `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` (safe inside tx; near-instant on empty/small dev + CI DBs), sibling `production-rollout.sql` (not run by drizzle-kit) carries `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS` variants for ops to apply via `psql` BEFORE the deploy's `pnpm migrate` — the IF NOT EXISTS guard then short-circuits and the migration row lands in `__drizzle_migrations` without re-building. AGENTS.md "Migrations" bullet documents the two-file convention. EXPLAIN ANALYZE smoke against 115k+ seeded rows confirmed each new index gets picked: Q1/Q2 Bitmap Index Scan on the composite (1.3ms / 0.7ms), Q4 Bitmap Index Scan on the partial (0.188ms). GIN gets picked when the query shape removes the LIMIT-1 shortcut (containment `metadata @> '{"bucket":"X"}'` returns 1050 rows via Bitmap Index Scan on the GIN at 210k row scale — defense-in-depth confirmed). |
| ENG-170 | Cap runEvents fan-in queries and move p95 to Postgres | Pending | P1 | — | `packages/data/src/workflowHealthRepo.ts:216-223` loads every `run_events` row for an arbitrary `runIds` array (no `LIMIT`) and then computes p95 latency client-side in JS. For a 30-day window on a high-volume workflow this reaches ~5k events × ~5 events/run = 25-100k rows pulled into the API process, ~10MB heap allocation per request, plus 200ms+ scan time. Two coupled fixes. (1) Add an explicit `.limit(windowDays * 1440 * 2)` defensive ceiling on the query (worst-case two events/minute/run window — anything beyond means the workflow itself is broken and the rollup is meaningless). (2) Move the p95 computation to Postgres via `PERCENTILE_CONT(0.95) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY (payload->>'durationMs')::int)` inside the SELECT — Drizzle 1.0 RC supports raw SQL fragments via the `sql` template. AC: `collectHealthSignals` returns the same shape it does today but the per-runId latency events arrive pre-aggregated as `{ runId, p95DurationMs, eventCount }` instead of the raw event list; the `computeWorkflowHealth` consumer in `packages/engine/src/workflow-health.ts` is updated to consume the pre-aggregated shape (its `latency` band logic stays unchanged); the new query plan via `EXPLAIN` shows Postgres performs the percentile without sorting more than N rows where N = the capped fan-in; `MIN_RUNS_FOR_DELTA = 5` neutral default still applies; tests cover (cap kicks in for over-sized fan-in, percentile matches the JS implementation for a small fixture, null-latency events are filtered server-side); the `/workflows/health` and `/workflows/health/delta` routes return byte-identical responses for fixtures under the cap. **Non-goals:** no materialized view (premature; the on-demand query is fine once the cap + Postgres percentile land), no migration of the cluster-failures repo (separate query, separate ticket if it shows the same shape). |
| ENG-171 | Retention sweep for audit_logs and scim_processed_events | Pending | P1 | — | Two append-only tables grow unbounded today: `audit_logs` (no TTL, ~10k rows/day enterprise org = 3.65M rows/year) and `scim_processed_events` (~10k events/day = 3.6M rows/year). Both will eventually slow the queries they index and, more importantly, complicate the GDPR/SOC2 "right to be forgotten" + retention-window posture that enterprise prospects ask for during procurement. The pattern already exists in the codebase: `packages/engine/src/memory-retention-scheduler.ts` is the canonical global-cron sweep (uses the reserved `system:` BullMQ id prefix, reads env-configurable cron, never throws, audits per-org outcomes). AC: new sibling module `packages/engine/src/audit-retention-scheduler.ts` registered at worker boot via `registerAuditRetentionScheduler()`, fires daily at `0 4 * * *` UTC (env override `JANUSLY_AUDIT_RETENTION_CRON`, validated via the shared `validateCronExpression`), deletes `audit_logs` rows older than 730 days by default (env `JANUSLY_AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS`, range 30..3650 — short enough to keep operationally tight, long enough to satisfy 2-year SOC2 evidence); same pattern for `scim_processed_events` with default 90 days (env `JANUSLY_SCIM_EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS`, range 7..365 — the dedup window only needs to outlast WorkOS retries which max at 24 hours, so 90 days is generous); both sweeps batch in 10k-row chunks via `DELETE … WHERE id IN (SELECT id FROM … WHERE created_at < cutoff LIMIT 10000)` so a sweep on a 10M-row table doesn't lock for minutes; one new audit action per sweep `audit_logs.retention.purged` / `scim.event_log.retention.purged` with `orgId: "system"` sentinel + `metadata: { rowsDeleted, cutoffAt, runtimeMs }`; AGENTS.md gains a new bullet under "Migrations" or a fresh "Retention" bullet documenting the new env knobs + the default windows; tests cover (idempotent re-fire skips already-deleted rows, dry-run-style row-count check via `SELECT COUNT(*)`, cron parse failure warn-falls-back-to-default, Postgres outage degrades to warn log not crash); new EN+ES i18n keys under `auditRetention.*` and `scimEventRetention.*` for any operator-facing surface (none for v1 — the audit row is the observable). **Non-goals:** no archive-to-cold-storage path (a future ticket can teach the sweep to write to S3 first if compliance requires evidence retention beyond the live-DB window), no per-org override of the retention window (system-wide for v1; per-tenant policy lands when the first enterprise asks), no UI for trigger/status (audit row is enough). |
| ENG-172 | Tighten transaction boundaries and document cascade posture | Pending | P2 | — | Two related data-integrity gaps. (1) `packages/data/src/memoryEntriesRepo.ts:361-384` inserts a `memory_entries` row, then separately calls `writeEntryCreatedAudit`; the same shape repeats for recall-fail audits and SCIM provision audits — if the audit write fails after the entity insert succeeds (Postgres blip, connection drop) the entity exists with no audit trail. The same pattern appears in the membership upsert + audit path in `apps/api/src/routes/sso-routes.ts` (SSO callback) and the MCP connection create + tool descriptor insert + audit path in `apps/api/src/routes/mcp-routes.ts`. The existing precedent for the right shape is `startRun` (documented in AGENTS.md as a single Drizzle transaction wrapping `runs` insert + `runNodes` batch insert + `run.started` event). (2) Cascade behavior on parent deletes (org delete, workflow delete) is documented inconsistently — workflow delete is explicit in AGENTS.md, but org delete and member delete leave behind workflows, runs, run_events, memory_entries, audit_logs, mcp_connections, etc. The trade-off (no FK constraints because `workflow_versions.workflowId` is text + intentionally orphan-tolerant) is intentional, but the broader cleanup posture is not written down so a maintainer adding a new repo doesn't know whether to cascade or orphan. AC: wrap each of the three identified entity+audit sequences (memory commit, SSO membership upsert, MCP connection create+descriptor insert) in a single `db.transaction(...)` call; expose a typed `withAuditTx<T>(handler)` helper in `packages/data/src/audit-tx.ts` that takes a transaction-bound `audit()` call so the audit row can NEVER outlive a rollback; failures bubble as `{ ok: false, error }` envelopes from the repo (no thrown exception) so calling routes keep their existing error-handling shape; AGENTS.md gains a new bullet under "Decision engine / RL" or a fresh "Cascade posture" bullet enumerating which parent deletes cascade vs orphan, with the rationale for each; tests cover (transaction rollback when audit write fails leaves no entity row, transaction commit writes both rows, the audit-tx helper rejects naked `audit()` calls via type system); no schema change. **Non-goals:** no introduction of FK constraints (intentional per AGENTS.md), no rewrite of the audit chokepoint signature (the helper is additive), no migration of every entity+audit pair in the codebase (the three identified are the high-traffic ones — others can adopt the helper opportunistically). |

Collateral: `apps/web/e2e/workflow.spec.ts` + `report-delivery.spec.ts` añaden click a "AI Studio" antes de asertar `.workflow-node` (canvas ya no monta en default tab); `apps/api/src/sso-routes.test.ts` gana timeout defensivo 10s. AGENTS.md añade el bullet "Layout & canvas visibility" como single source of truth. **Shipped via commit `9c84adb`** ("feat(web): gate workflow canvas to builder tabs"); 11 archivos staged, build + test verdes, validación responsive a 1440/900/480 px confirmada vía Playwright. **Honest non-goals (cumplidos en v1):** no `ReactFlowProvider` wrapper (viewport del canvas se resetea en `inspector → otra tab → inspector`; documentado como limitación reconocida en AGENTS.md; fix correcto en follow-up); no sub-tab rail en Operations (eso es ENG-181); no severity stripes (eso es ENG-182); no reorg sidebar (ENG-183); no split god-component (ENG-184). |

| ENG-186 | Lift ReactFlowProvider for canvas viewport persistence | Pending | P2 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 follow-up — cierra la "acknowledged limitation" documentada en el bullet "Layout & canvas visibility" de AGENTS.md y en ENG-180's risks. Hoy el canvas React Flow re-monta cada vez que el usuario navega de un canvas tab (`copilot` / `inspector`) a un non-canvas tab y de vuelta — esto resetea el zoom / pan / posición de scroll del lienzo. Para workflows largos (>20 nodos) es fricción operacional real: el editor pierde su contexto visual cada vez que va a verificar Operations o Members y vuelve. Causa raíz: no hay `<ReactFlowProvider>` envolviendo el árbol de `<App>` — el `WorkflowCanvas` instance se destruye en cada unmount porque su context vive INSIDE del componente y muere con él. AC: envolver `<App>` en `<ReactFlowProvider>` en `apps/web/src/main.tsx` (alrededor del `<App />` en el `createRoot.render(...)`). Cambiar la dispatcher logic en `App.tsx:770-810` para que el canvas SIEMPRE se monte como un sibling del slot main y se oculte con `display: none` (vía un data-attribute en su wrapper, ej. `<div data-canvas-visible={isCanvasTab(activeTab) ? "true" : "false"}>`). El `ContextualMain` toma su lugar visualmente cuando el canvas está oculto SIN destruirlo. El viewport state sobrevive porque ReactFlow lo persiste en su context provider. Test: nuevo case en `apps/web/src/types.test.ts` o `App.canvas-visibility.test.tsx` que assert (a) canvas DOM presente en TODOS los 12 tabs (verifica via `querySelector('.react-flow')` returning truthy), (b) `data-canvas-visible="false"` aplicado en non-canvas tabs. Manual smoke obligatorio: navegar `inspector` → editar workflow grande (pan + zoom) → `operations` → `inspector` → verificar que zoom y pan se preservan byte-for-byte. AGENTS.md doc-sync: ELIMINAR la "Acknowledged limitation" del bullet "Layout & canvas visibility" porque deja de ser limitación. **Honest non-goals v1:** no upgrade de versión de React Flow (queda en la versión actual); no cambios a shortcuts del canvas (delete, copy, paste siguen iguales); no añadir `useReactFlow()` hooks fuera del canvas mismo; no persistir viewport entre page reloads (el provider lo persiste sólo en memoria — un F5 sigue reseteando, eso es OK); no cambios al `RecoveryCenterPanel` (home sigue siendo su rama dedicada sin canvas). **Riesgo:** mounting el canvas en TODOS los tabs (aunque hidden) carga React Flow + sus subscribers desde el primer render, no lazy. Para un usuario que nunca abre AI Studio / Inspector eso es ~50 KB extra de JS en el initial bundle (aunque React Flow ya carga vía vendor chunk, no es bundle creep nuevo). Aceptable porque el caso "usuario nunca abre el editor" es raro en Janusly. **Por qué P2:** mejora la experiencia de un sub-grupo de operadores (los que editan workflows largos); no es un fix de seguridad ni de fiabilidad. Ship cuando aparezca un reporte real de fricción. |
| ENG-187 | Cross-surface vital-signs status strip | Pending | P2 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 follow-up — cierra la "Datadog pattern" mencionada en el análisis de mercado pero que no entró en ENG-181/182. Hoy `OperationsPanel` tiene 4 metric tiles arriba (success rate, MTTR, p95 latency, replay success) que comunican el estado operacional del workspace en un vistazo. El `RecoveryCenterPanel` (home) tiene un strip similar pero distinto (greeting + Health ring + 4 tiles propias). Inconsistencia: el operador aprende dos vocabularios visuales para "vital signs" según en qué surface esté. Datadog usa el mismo strip persistente en TODA superficie operacional — el operador aprende una sola fila y sabe la salud del producto sin importar dónde está. AC: extraer el metric tile pattern (4-card row con icon + value + label + sparkline opcional + severity tint) a un componente reutilizable `apps/web/src/components/VitalSignsStrip.tsx`. Render via `<VitalSignsStrip windowDays={N} surface={"operations"|"home"} />` con props que permiten variantes contextuales en tile order pero la APARIENCIA es idéntica. Mount points: (1) sticky header de la nueva `OperationsPage.tsx` (que ENG-181 introduce promoting los metric tiles al header de Operations — este ticket extrae el componente reutilizable); (2) NUEVO mount sticky en el top de `RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx` debajo del greeting hero pero arriba de "Recommended next actions". Ambos consumen el mismo `GET /recovery/metrics` endpoint con el mismo cache key → cero duplicación de fetches. ~6 i18n keys EN+ES bajo `vitalSigns.*` (no hay strings nuevos, sólo extraer las existentes). Tests: `VitalSignsStrip.test.tsx` (3 cases — render con 4 metrics + severity tint + empty state cuando no hay data). **Honest non-goals v1:** no cambios al endpoint backend `/recovery/metrics` (sigue devolviendo el mismo shape); no añadir métricas nuevas (mantener las 4 actuales); no animación de transición entre snapshots (snap directo); no mount en builder/inspector (sólo operational surfaces — home + operations); no sparkline (los tiles son numeric-only por ahora, sparkline es follow-up); no responsive collapse para mobile (los 4 tiles stackean naturalmente <760 px). **Depende de:** ENG-181 debe shippearse PRIMERO porque promueve los metric tiles al header de Operations (donde el componente extraído se monta); después este ticket extrae el strip y lo re-mounta en home. Si ENG-181 está en flight, este ticket puede arrancar en paralelo pero el merge se hace después. **Por qué P2:** mejora la consistencia visual cross-surface; no desbloquea funcionalidad. Ship cuando ENG-181 esté merged y haya capacity para un pulido cross-surface. |

### 3c. Open decisions blocking tickets

The following tickets are not in §3b because they wait on a strategic decision (see `docs/PLAN.md` §13). Move them into §3b once the decision is made.

- **Pricing model decision.** Affects `usage_events` shape (ENG-012). If we pick per-token, the row writes one shape; per-run, another. Currently writing both is fine — unblock when pricing is chosen.
- **License choice.** Affects MCP server bundling (ENG-013) and contributor pipeline (ENG-026 sub-item). Stays as a footnote on those tickets.
- **Self-host vs cloud canonical product.** Affects retention defaults (ENG-026) and SaaS-only flags. Not blocking ENG-008..023.
- **AI training data / memory opt-in policy.** Affects future memory/RL tickets (ENG-025) and is tracked explicitly by ENG-114. The closing-draft policy lives in [`docs/memory-policy.md`](memory-policy.md); it covers eligibility, two-flag opt-in consent, per-kind retention, deletion/export, embedding provider posture, prompt-injection framing, the `org_configs.memory.*` catalog, audit actions, DPA language, and incident response. ENG-115+ must not ship persistent embeddings or cross-run memory until that policy is reviewed by product + legal + engineering per the checklist at the end of the doc. Embeddings are accepted after the gate; OpenAI-specific schema assumptions are not.

---

## 4. How to add a ticket

1. Pick the next `ENG-NNN` (highest existing + 1).
2. Add the row to §3b with `Status: Pending` and a Scope cell that contains acceptance criteria.
3. If the work is part of a Phase from `docs/PLAN.md` §11, set `Phase` accordingly. If it's a standalone quick-win or fix, leave Phase as `—`.
4. If the ticket depends on another, mention the blocker in the Scope cell ("Depends on ENG-NNN").
5. If the ticket is blocked by an open question (`docs/PLAN.md` §13), set `Status: Gated` and reference the question in §3c.

When the implementer skill (`janus-ship`) closes a ticket, it flips Status to `Shipped` (or `Partial` with an updated `Remaining:`) and appends a 2-3 line `**Summary:**` to the Scope cell. The reviewer skill (`janus-review`) verifies the flip is coherent with the staged diff.
